## Goal

Ship the Spec-023 Electron desktop shell and React + Vite renderer as a three-process system (main / renderer / supervised daemon) that lands the renderer-untrusted trust stance, the narrow typed preload bridge, the full Electron Fuses + ASAR-integrity + CSP hardening baseline, `utilityProcess`-based daemon supervision, `electron-updater` v6.8.9 auto-update (re-pinned 2026-09-01 from 6.8.4) with a project-owned rollback state machine, `@napi-rs/keyring` + `safeStorage` keystore access with Linux plaintext-fallback detection, code-signing + notarization on macOS / Windows / Linux, Crashpad + `@sentry/electron` v7.11.0 crash reporting with main-process PII scrubbing, and the WebAuthn PRF ceremony via a per-platform native-binding strategy that is native on all three platforms (re-derived 2026-09-01: an own-built `AuthenticationServices` addon on macOS, `webauthn.dll` on Windows, `libfido2` on Linux, with the Device Authorization Grant loopback the per-host fallback for a ceremony-time capability probe that finds no usable authenticator or no PRF, on any of the three platforms — I-023-15). Renderer ships the shell chrome, router, and composer, and **mounts** the V1 Signature Feature views (timeline, approvals, invites, runs, multi-agent channels) that their owning plans author in those plans' own subtrees — this plan creates a mount point and a route, never a view body (corrected 2026-08-10 by the Tier-8 remainder readiness audit, NS-20, M-023-5; the pre-correction sentence read as though this plan composed the five views itself). It is the shell surface, not the authority. **The console** — the Meridian surface set `Spec-023 §Console Design (Meridian)` fixes (icon rail, all-sessions list, session workspace, the closed pane-kind set, the two auxiliary windows, the fixture bridge) — is registered by this plan at §Target Areas Renderer (`console/`) and built on lane 1 as the Tier-1 explicit-label supplement Phase 1C inside that widened spec envelope (2026-09-01); Phase 6 **adopts** it by mounting and moving, never by re-authoring (T-023r-6-1 EXTEND).

## Scope

- `apps/desktop/` — **new app package.** The Electron main process (`apps/desktop/src/main/`), the preload bridge (`apps/desktop/src/preload/`), and the React + Vite renderer (`apps/desktop/src/renderer/src/`).
- Electron runtime target: **44.x** (44.1.1 at 2026-09-01) per `ADR-016 §Decision` as amended 2026-09-01 — Electron 41 reached end-of-support on 2026-08-25 with the 44.0.0 release, and the detached-`WebContentsView` bounds fix the console's `browser` pane kind depends on (electron/electron PR #53031) was backported to 42 / 43 / 44 only. The 41.1.0+ floor this line carried from 2026-04-17 (the Q1 2026 CVE batch — CVE-2026-34769, -34770, -34771, -34772, -34774, -34764, -34776, all fixed 2026-04-02 in 39.8.5 / 40.8.5 / 41.1.0 per the [Electron GHSA feed](https://github.com/electron/electron/security/advisories)) is satisfied by every release on a supported branch. The `apps/desktop/package.json` pin moves at T-023p-1B-4, together with the `better-sqlite3` 13.0.3 move [ADR-022 §Decision Log](../decisions/022-v1-toolchain-selection.md#decision-log) binds to it.
- Build toolchain: **`electron-vite`** (alex8088) v5.0.0 stable — Vite-powered main / preload / renderer build with HMR, source-code protection, and a single `electron.vite.config.ts`. Alternative considered + rejected in §Risks And Blockers.
- Packaging + auto-update: **`electron-builder`** 26.15.x + **`electron-updater`** 6.8.9 per `Spec-023 §Implementation Notes` §Build And Packaging and §Auto-Updater (re-pinned 2026-09-01 from 26.9.0 / 6.8.4; the manifest schema the trust chain binds to is unchanged across the line).
- Renderer stack: React 19 + Vite 6 + TypeScript strict (per `ADR-016 §Success Criteria`).
- Preload bridge: single `window.sidekicks` object via `contextBridge.exposeInMainWorld`; narrow typed capability surface (no `any`) per `Spec-023 §Preload Bridge Contract`.
- Security hardening: every `BrowserWindow` created with the exact `webPreferences` locked in `Spec-023 §Security Hardening Baseline`; every release build packaged with the nine Electron Fuses + the ASAR Integrity Digest (new in Electron 41 via `@electron/asar` v4.1.0+); strict CSP per Spec-023.
- Daemon supervision: `utilityProcess.fork()` (not `child_process.fork`) with the exponential-backoff restart policy + 5-attempt ceiling + `"memory-eviction"` special-case from `Spec-023 §Implementation Notes` §Utility Process Vs Child Process.
- Keystore: **`@napi-rs/keyring`** v1.2.0 primary; Electron `safeStorage` for one-shot-blob cases; Linux `safeStorage.getSelectedStorageBackend()` must reject `basic_text` / `unknown` per `Spec-023 §Implementation Notes` §Native Keystore.
- Auto-update: the update feed's `latest-*.yml` manifest is Ed25519-signature-verified (`manifest.sig` over the exact manifest bytes) against a build-time-embedded public key, and each downloaded artifact is hash-bound to the authenticated manifest's per-file artifact-hash entry before staging — the trust chain is pinned key → signed manifest → hash-bound artifact, with no per-artifact signature. Before an update applies, the prior version is retained (with a recorded integrity hash) in `<app-user-data>/retained-versions/`, and retention failure refuses the update fail-closed; on post-apply daemon-handshake failure the rollback state machine re-installs from the integrity-verified retained copy — never from the live executable path, which the apply itself overwrites. The rollback state machine is **authored by this plan**; it is not an out-of-the-box `electron-updater` feature.
- WebAuthn: main-process orchestration via a per-platform strategy that is **native on all three platforms** (re-derived 2026-09-01, [cross-plan-dependencies.md §6](../architecture/cross-plan-dependencies.md) node NS-99, superseding the accepted scope degradation this bullet recorded from 2026-04). macOS binds an **own-built** universal (arm64 + x64) Swift N-API addon over Apple `AuthenticationServices` in `apps/desktop/native/webauthn-darwin/` — Vault12 `electron-webauthn-mac` v1.0.0 is the **reference implementation and is not adopted**, on two measured disqualifiers: it generates the WebAuthn challenge internally, so the relying party's challenge cannot be supplied and no assertion it produces is server-verifiable, and its prebuilt binary is arm64-only and non-fat with no `os` / `cpu` field and no arch guard. Windows binds the OS `webauthn.dll` API and Linux binds the distribution's own `libfido2` for roaming FIDO2 keys, both through **`koffi`** (exact-pinned, floor `3.1.6`, MIT — per-platform prebuilt `optionalDependencies`, so nothing compiles at install and **no `onlyBuiltDependencies` entry is warranted**). Linux exposes no platform authenticator and this plan claims none. The **Device Authorization Grant (DAG) loopback** stays exactly the `Spec-023 §Fallback Behavior` degradation for a host whose **ceremony-time capability probe** finds no usable authenticator or no PRF — on any of the three platforms — and is never a per-platform assignment (I-023-15).
- Code-signing + notarization: Apple Developer ID Application cert + `xcrun notarytool` (macOS); Azure Artifact Signing Basic SKU OR EV cert from DigiCert / Sectigo / SSL.com (Windows, subject to CSC-31's new 15-month validity ceiling); GPG-signed `.deb` / `.rpm` + optional AppImage signature (Linux).
- Crash reporting: **`@sentry/electron`** v7.11.0 with `SentryMinidump` default integration (Crashpad-sourced); per-process `init()` in main + every renderer + the `utilityProcess` daemon; main-process `beforeSend` scrubber strips tokens, session IDs, file-paths, content payloads before upload; minidump-byte-level scrubbing is a server-side posture documented as a gap (see §Risks And Blockers).
- Deep-link: register the `sidekicks://` protocol handler on all three platforms; URL parsing + token extraction + capability-exchange happen in the main process, never in the renderer.
- E2E test harness: **Playwright `_electron`** namespace (still officially marked experimental in v1.58.x per [Playwright class-electron docs](https://playwright.dev/docs/api/class-electron), but the only production-viable option; Spectron has been archived since 2022).
- Monorepo placement: `apps/desktop/` as a workspace package, sibling to `packages/runtime-daemon/`, `packages/contracts/`, `packages/control-plane/` per the monorepo topology in [container-architecture.md](../architecture/container-architecture.md) §Client Delivery Sequence.

## Non-Goals

- **Pixel-level mockups and per-screen visual specifications.** `Spec-023 §Console Design (Meridian)` fixes the design language, the surface set, the pane and window model, the budgets, and — through `Spec-023 §Console Libraries` — the component-library and theme decisions; this plan registers the console subtree and its adoption path (§Target Areas Renderer, T-023r-6-1 EXTEND) and carries its invariants (I-023-11, I-023-12), while the per-surface composition is the console's own code and fixture scenarios. _Re-derived 2026-09-01: the prior bullet deferred all of this to a design track that has since run and landed in Spec-023._
- **Daemon internals.** Owned by `component-architecture-local-daemon.md` and Spec-007 / Plan-007. This plan consumes Plan-007's daemon contract; it does not re-specify it.
- **PTY supervision from the shell.** Per `Plan-024 §Target Areas`, `PtyHostSelector` + `RustSidecarPtyHost` + `NodePtyHost` are owned by the **daemon** (`packages/runtime-daemon/src/pty/`). The shell never consumes the PtyHost contract directly. A Plan-023 edit wiring PTY supervision into the main process is a review rejection.
- **The CLI client.** CLI first-run onboarding lives in Plan-026; CLI session surfaces are owned by a separate plan. Plan-023 is desktop-only.
- **Mobile or browser-hosted renderer surfaces.** Out of V1 per ADR-015; out of Spec-007 scope; out of this plan's scope.
- **Provider-driver protocols.** Owned by Spec-005.
- **Workflow authoring semantics.** Owned by the workflow-engine track — V1 feature 17 since the ADR-015 amendment 2026-04-22 (Spec-017 / Plan-017, Tier 8; the node-graph builder under ADR-026). The console mounts Plan-017's `workflows/` subtree as the `workflow-run` and `workflow-builder` pane kinds of `Spec-023 §Console Design (Meridian)` and authors none of its bodies (re-worded 2026-09-01; the prior bullet said this plan composes no workflow view, which the pane-kind set supersedes at the mount level only).
- **MSIX (Windows Store) distribution.** V1 ships NSIS + MSI direct-download; MSIX is a V1.1 re-evaluation per `Spec-023 §Open Questions`.
- **Reproducible builds.** Not claimed by V1 per `Spec-023 §Open Questions`; neither `electron-builder` nor `electron-vite` guarantees bit-reproducibility.
- **Snap / Flatpak packaging.** V1 ships `.AppImage` + `.deb` + `.rpm`; Snap / Flatpak is a user-demand-gated follow-up per `Spec-023 §Open Questions`.
- **Reconciling `security-architecture.md` §Local Daemon Authentication.** Reconciled under BL-056 on 2026-04-18 before Plan-023 execution. Plan-023 consumes the renderer-untrusted stance as declared in `Spec-023 §Trust Stance` and now consistent in `security-architecture.md` §Local Daemon Authentication; no further alignment work is required in this plan.

## Preconditions

The ADR + Spec preconditions below (including the plan-readiness-audit box) gate **both** the Tier 1 partial and the Tier 8 remainder. The seven `**(Tier 8 remainder only.)**` boxes — Plan-007's daemon-control surface, Plan-018's token API, Plan-008's tRPC + WebSocket clients, the Plan-007-owned contracts type exports, the Spec-002 / Plan-002 non-consuming invite-metadata read, the Plan-002 T3.5 typing update path, and the Plan-007 / Plan-008 subscription request-param channel — gate **only the Tier 8 remainder** (full main-process behavior consumes those plans' surfaces). Each is put on the machine-enforced path by a per-phase `preconditions:` entry in [§Tier 8 Remainder PR Sequence](#tier-8-remainder-pr-sequence), carried onto every phase it gates — all seven via `precondition_box_checked` prefix entries, the invite-metadata box additionally paired with `{ type: plan_phase, plan: 002, phase: 2, status: merged }` on Phases 5 and 6, which resolves that box against the tasks that actually produce the surface (Plan-002 T2.6–T2.8, its declared-but-unshipped Phase-2 remainder) rather than against a backlog reference. An unticked box on its own is documentation; only the paired entry halts dispatch. The Tier 1 partial has no upstream-plan preconditions per [§Tier 1 Partial PR Sequence](#tier-1-partial-pr-sequence) — it is a standalone substrate slice authored against ADR-016 + Spec-023 alone.

No task, phase, invariant, file, or column is added — Plan-030 Phase 5 is gated on this plan's Phase 6 merged, and the components that fill the mount are authored by Plan-030, not here — so no phase gate on this plan moves. **Re-opened 2026-08-29** by the desktop-console parity amendment, scoped to the two new sub-sketches (Session Composer, MCP Servers View) and the Timeline View's input-ask bullet; **re-checked in the same diff** by its targeted readiness-audit delta ([cross-plan-dependencies.md §6](../architecture/cross-plan-dependencies.md) node NS-93). **Re-opened 2026-09-01, scoped to the console-design amendment ([cross-plan-dependencies.md §6](../architecture/cross-plan-dependencies.md) node NS-97):** this swap mints invariants **I-023-11** (the renderer scheme is registered privileged before `app.ready`) and **I-023-12** (an auxiliary window is a hardened `BrowserWindow` with its own bridge instance and no shared store), registers the `console/` subtree at §Target Areas Renderer with its Phase-1C authoring rule, adds `§Console growth slate` under §API And Transport Changes, authors the Tier-1 explicit-label supplements Phase 1B (T-023p-1B-1..4 — the renderer-load substrate and the pin move; T-023r-2-8 retired into T-023p-1B-3) and Phase 1C (T-023p-1C-1..8 — the console, on lane 1), mints I-023-13 / I-023-14 for the fixture bridge and the budgets, EXTENDs T-023r-3-4 (composition-only) / T-023r-6-1 / T-023r-6-2, and moves the Electron and packaging pins — four new invariants and two new phases are a [§Status Flip Rule](../operations/plan-implementation-readiness-audit-runbook.md#status-flip-rule) row-3 trigger, so this plan flipped `approved → review`. **Delivered 2026-09-01, in the same diff:** the console-design targeted readiness-audit delta restored `approved` in the swap that raised it (the NS-73 in-swap flip-and-restore shape), so this box closes re-checked. No existing phase gate on this plan moves; the two phases this swap adds — the Tier-1 explicit-label supplements 1B (the renderer-load substrate and the pin move) and 1C (the console) — gate only on Phase 1 and Phase 1B respectively, the console is consumed by Phase 6 through adoption, and every wire it does not yet have is named on the growth slate rather than assumed. **Re-opened 2026-09-01, scoped to the WebAuthn per-platform-strategy amendment ([cross-plan-dependencies.md §6](../architecture/cross-plan-dependencies.md) node NS-99):** this swap withdraws the accepted scope degradation §Scope carried since 2026-04, mints invariants **I-023-15** (WebAuthn capability is probed per host at ceremony time and never inferred from `process.platform`) and, at the Codex round-1 fold, **I-023-16** (every ceremony input is main-resolved and server-issued — the preload bridge takes no ceremony parameter and returns no key material), adds Phase-4 tasks **T-023r-4-5** (the Windows `webauthn.dll` binding), **T-023r-4-6** (the Linux `libfido2` binding), and **T-023r-4-7** (the password-derived KEK custody leg on the no-PRF path, the disposition `ADR-010 §Consequences` had already decided and this plan had never carried), rewrites **T-023r-4-3** onto the own-built macOS addon and its two providers, narrows the `webAuthn` bridge surface at T-023r-2-5, registers `apps/desktop/native/webauthn-darwin/` (with its universal build, owned by T-023r-4-3 from the Codex round-3 fold) and the **four** new `webauthn/*.ts` modules at §Target Areas (`windows.ts`, `linux.ts`, `kek-fallback.ts`, and — from the Codex round-2 fold — `passphrase-prompt.ts`, beside that fold's `preload/secret-entry.ts` entry point and `renderer/src/secret-entry/` route body), adds two E2E spec files and re-scopes two more, and pins `koffi` as the FFI — two new invariants plus new tasks are a [§Status Flip Rule](../operations/plan-implementation-readiness-audit-runbook.md#status-flip-rule) row-3 trigger, so this plan flipped `approved → review`. **Delivered 2026-09-01, in the same diff:** the per-platform-strategy targeted readiness-audit delta restored `approved` in the swap that raised it (the NS-73 in-swap flip-and-restore shape), so this box closes re-checked. The Codex round-3 fold adds **CP-023-9** (cross-plan obligations eight → nine) inside the same re-open, mints no invariant and no task, and re-points `Plan-022` CP-022-3 onto that port, which flips and restores Plan-022 in the same swap. The Codex round-4 fold adds **CP-023-10** (cross-plan obligations nine → ten) inside the same re-open, likewise minting no invariant and no task on this plan, and **does move one phase gate**: Phase 4 gains `{ type: external_plan_phase_merged, plan: 018, phase: 6 }`, because that fold established that no plan owned the ceremony's relying-party half and assigned it to Plan-018's new Phase 6 — the bindings this phase builds have no server to call until it merges. Its `plan_phase` precondition on Phase 3 and its Plan-018 token-API box are unchanged, every round-4 edit lands inside already-declared tasks, and the macOS end-to-end PRF verification stays [BL-108](../backlog.md)-gated exactly as the signing work already was. The Codex round-5, round-6, and round-7 folds land inside this same re-open and mint invariants **I-023-17** (PRF material is provisional until the relying party's verdict and zeroized on every refusal arm), **I-023-18** (a per-binding single-flight slot held to the native callback, so a cancel-then-retry refuses rather than racing a device handle the first call still owns), **I-023-19** (the per-installation custody root every wrapping key wraps a copy of), and **I-023-20** (the DPoP proof key held outside that root, since main must sign the token-request proof before the reply that would unwrap anything) — invariants fourteen → **twenty** across the whole node — together with Phase-4 task **T-023r-4-8** (tasks seven → **eight**), the round-2 secret-entry window, and the round-7 correction that takes CP-023-9 off its macOS-only scope onto resolved host capability, which flips and restores Plan-022 and Spec-022 in the same swap. - [ ] **(Tier 8 remainder only.)** Plan-007 exposes the `DaemonStart`/`DaemonStop`/`DaemonRestart`/`DaemonStatusRead` surface the shell supervisor consumes. Plan-007 ownership of the daemon-start contract is load-bearing; if Plan-007 ships without an explicit `DaemonHello` readiness signal, Step 7 below blocks until it lands.
- [ ] **(Tier 8 remainder only.)** Plan-018 exposes a main-process-consumable API for issuing / refreshing PASETO v4.public tokens + storing the matched DPoP key. The shell does not mint tokens; it stores and presents them.
- [ ] **(Tier 8 remainder only.)** Plan-008 exposes a tRPC v11 client + WebSocket (JSON-RPC 2.0) client surface the main process can instantiate. The shell is a forwarder, not a protocol implementer.

This plan's preload-bridge types re-export them. The Tier 1 partial ships the `SidekicksBridge` interface stub against the Plan-007 type names as forward-declared imports; Tier 8 remainder narrows to the canonical Plan-007 type definitions when the daemon contract namespace ships.
- [ ] **(Tier 8 remainder only.)** Spec-002 / Plan-002 expose the **non-consuming invite-metadata** read the deep-link confirmation surface calls before acceptance — it validates the token and returns display metadata WITHOUT burning the single-use invite. End-state contract pinned in [Spec-023 §Deep-Link Invite Flow](../specs/023-desktop-shell-and-renderer.md#deep-link-invite-flow) ("Non-consuming invite-metadata path required"); **Resolved on the contract axis 2026-08-11** (PR #322): the surface is [`invite.preview`](../specs/002-invite-membership-and-presence.md#interfaces-and-contracts) — an anonymous, non-consuming (zero writes, the `jti` survives), POST-only tRPC mutation returning `{sessionId, joinMode, expiresAt, sessionName, inviterDisplayName}` with `InviteAccept`'s exact refusal ordering. **This box now gates on its producer, not on a backlog item (2026-08-25, NS-81):** Plan-002 declares the build at T2.6–T2.8 and has shipped only T2.1–T2.5, so Phases 5 and 6 carry `{ type: plan_phase, plan: 002, phase: 2, status: merged }` — strictly stronger than the `{ type: bl_closed, ref: 133 }` entry it replaces, because it fails closed on the unshipped code rather than on a doc status. [BL-133](../backlog.md#bl-133-non-consuming-invite-metadata-endpoint-for-the-deep-link-confirmation-surface) **stays open at `todo`** in `docs/backlog.md`: its criterion (c) is a two-client smoke that must _pass_ end-to-end, which no doc-only diff can satisfy, so it is narrowed onto T-023r-8-5 as its named exit vehicle rather than retired by reinterpretation. Its remaining consumption leg is authored below as T-023r-5-5 / T-023r-6-3, and leaving the item open costs this box nothing — the gate no longer keys on it.

Type-name availability does not satisfy this box; the parameterized signature must exist.

## Target Areas

### New package

- `apps/desktop/` — **created by this plan.** Workspace package, sibling to `packages/*`.
- `apps/desktop/package.json` — declares the Electron 44.x runtime (re-pinned 2026-09-01 from 41.1.0+), `electron-vite` v5 as the build tool, `electron-builder` 26.15.x + `electron-updater` 6.8.9 as packaging + update toolchain (re-pinned 2026-09-01 from 26.9.0 / 6.8.4; the Electron pin itself moves at T-023p-1B-4), `@napi-rs/keyring` v1.2.0, `@sentry/electron` v7.11.0, `@electron/asar` v4.1.0+, `@electron/fuses` for fuse-flipping at build time, and `koffi` (exact, floor `3.1.6`) as the Windows / Linux WebAuthn FFI; the macOS WebAuthn addon is built from `apps/desktop/native/webauthn-darwin/` in this repository rather than installed (re-derived 2026-09-01 — `electron-webauthn-mac` is the reference implementation and is not a dependency). Renderer deps: `react` v19, `react-dom` v19. Playwright `_electron` for E2E testing. **Console dependencies (2026-09-01, exact-pinned, each admitted by a `Spec-023 §Console Libraries` verdict row and landing with the console PR that first imports it):** `@tanstack/react-virtual`, `micromark` + `mdast-util-from-markdown` + the GFM extensions, `remend`, `shiki` (`shiki/core` + `@shikijs/stream`), `katex`, `mermaid` (lazy, opt-in), `@xterm/xterm` + the WebGL / fit / search / unicode11 / serialize addons, `anser`, `diff`, `@xyflow/react` (with `@xyflow/system` in lockstep), `react-resizable-panels`, `@atlaskit/pragmatic-drag-and-drop`, `@base-ui/react`, `tinykeys`, `@floating-ui/react-dom`, `zustand`, `idb`, `unplugin-icons` + `@iconify-json/tabler`, and the IBM Plex Sans / Mono foundry packages; test-only `@vitest/browser-playwright`, `@playwright/test`, `axe-core` (MPL-2.0, ADR-020 §Decision Log waiver — bare rather than the `@axe-core/playwright` wrapper, which binds to a Playwright `Page` that the browser-mode tier does not expose), `memlab`, `size-limit`, `knip`. Nothing on the AVOID column of that table may appear here. **Vitest-family lockstep (2026-09-01, Codex round 1):** `@vitest/browser-playwright` peers on `vitest` at the exact same version, so it is admitted through the `testing` catalog in `pnpm-workspace.yaml` beside `vitest` and `@vitest/coverage-v8`, never as a desktop-local exact pin — the catalog entry is the single pin, the family moves together, and the registry-latest rule yields to catalog lockstep for any package whose peer is exact-versioned.

### Main process

- `apps/desktop/src/main/index.ts` — **created.** Main entrypoint. Calls `app.requestSingleInstanceLock()`; reads startup config; initializes Sentry + crashReporter **first** (before any other module loads, per [`@sentry/electron` docs](https://docs.sentry.io/platforms/javascript/guides/electron/)); registers the `sidekicks://` protocol handler; creates the main window; starts the daemon supervisor.
- `apps/desktop/src/main/window.ts` — **created.** `createMainWindow()` factory that returns a `BrowserWindow` with the exact `webPreferences` locked in `Spec-023 §Security Hardening Baseline`. A build-time assertion asserts these values match the spec. **Phase 1B (T-023p-1B-2):** the main window's `loadURL` over the custom scheme, the private `constructLockedWindow` that is the single owner of the locked literal, and `createAuxiliaryWindow(route)` over the closed `'timeline' | 'agent-console'` route type (I-023-12). **Phase 4 (T-023r-4-7, Codex round 2):** `createSecretEntryWindow()` joins them as a third factory through that same private constructor — so the build-time assertion still finds exactly one locked literal — and deliberately outside the auxiliary route type, since the secret-entry window hosts no pane and the auxiliary set stays at two.
- `apps/desktop/src/main/menu.ts` — **created at Phase 1B (T-023p-1B-2).** The application menu: platform-default roles plus the `Window` submenu whose two entries and accelerators open the auxiliary windows — the menu-bar path `Spec-023 §Main Process Responsibilities` ships first because it needs no new bridge namespace.
- `apps/desktop/src/main/protocol.ts` — **created.** Custom protocol (`sidekicks-renderer://`) the renderer bundle is served from — not `file://`, per Spec-023's `GrantFileProtocolExtraPrivileges` fuse-disabled posture. **Registered privileged before `app.ready` (I-023-11, 2026-09-01):** `protocol.registerSchemesAsPrivileged([{ scheme: 'sidekicks-renderer', privileges: { standard: true, secure: true, supportFetchAPI: true } }])` runs at module top level in `main/index.ts`'s import order, ahead of the ready handler, because a scheme not registered `standard` has no origin and therefore no IndexedDB and no `localStorage` — the console's persisted layouts, drafts, and pins (`Spec-023 §Console Design (Meridian)` §Persistence on the renderer scheme) would have no home. A test asserts the registration precedes `whenReady()`. **Created at Phase 1B (T-023p-1B-1)** beside the bundle handler: `installRendererProtocol(rendererRoot)` serves the built tree through the pure `resolveRendererAsset` (root-containment guard, closed content-type map, the locked CSP header on every response, streaming bodies via `net.fetch`, empty-bodied 403 / 404).
- `apps/desktop/src/main/bridge/index.ts` — **created.** IPC handler registration. One `ipcMain.handle` per preload-bridge method from `Spec-023 §Preload Bridge Contract`.
- `apps/desktop/src/main/bridge/daemon.ts` — **created.** `daemon.call` / `daemon.subscribe` handlers. Forwards to the Plan-007 daemon client with the session token attached server-side. **Gated on the request-param `## Preconditions` box:** `Spec-023 §Preload Bridge Contract` pins the generic `daemon.subscribe<E>(event, handler)` signature as carrying no request-param channel and defers the parameterized shape to Plan-007 / Plan-008, so every parameterized subscription the remainder needs (`presence.subscribe` with `{ sessionId }` is the pinned example) has no shape to call until that box clears — Phase 2 carries the matching `precondition_box_checked` entry. Type-name availability is not the resolution; the parameterized signature is.
- `apps/desktop/src/main/bridge/control-plane.ts` — **created.** `controlPlane.call` (tRPC/HTTPS request-response) and `controlPlane.subscribeRelay` (relay WSS event subscription) handlers. `controlPlane.call` attaches `Authorization: DPoP <paseto-v4.public>` + `DPoP: <signed-proof>` headers (RFC 9449 §7.1 — the DPoP-bound-token scheme, never `Bearer`) to the outbound request; **`subscribeRelay` attaches neither** — the relay WSS authenticates solely via the [Spec-008](../specs/008-control-plane-relay-and-session-join.md) §Relay Connection Lifecycle `Sec-WebSocket-Protocol` subprotocol carrying the main-owned R3-issued `connectionToken` (browsers cannot set custom WS headers — see §Interfaces And Contracts). Both handlers strip auth material from responses before returning to the renderer; the relay `connectionToken` is consumed in the main process and never crosses the bridge. **Header attachment is conditional, not unconditional:** `controlPlane.call` attaches the credential pair only when a control-plane credential is held, so a procedure the control plane defines as anonymous is reachable with no credential at all. `invite.preview` is the V1 instance — [Plan-008](./008-control-plane-relay-and-session-join.md) I-008-4 gates `invite.create` / `invite.accept` / `invite.revoke` and deliberately excludes `preview` because "the previewer may hold no registered identity yet", warning that requiring authentication ahead of the confirmation step would regress the deep-link flow. **The obligation is reachability, not suppression:** anonymous per `Spec-002 §Interfaces And Contracts` means authentication is not _required_, not that a held credential must be stripped, and the `Spec-021 §Canonical Endpoint Group Registry` row keys the procedure per token-hash rather than per principal — so a signed-in previewer's credential changes neither admission nor throttling. What re-introduces the regression one layer down, in the client this plan owns, is a `call` that can only issue **credentialed** requests: it would have nothing to send before sign-in, and the `authentication-required` outcome T-023r-5-5 mints would be unreachable by construction.
- `apps/desktop/src/main/bridge/native.ts` — **created.** `showOpenDialog` / `showSaveDialog` / `showMessageBox` / `showNotification` / `openExternal` (with allowlist validation) / `copyToClipboard` / `revealInFileExplorer` (operating on opaque `FilePathRef` tokens).
- `apps/desktop/src/main/shell-rpc.ts` — **created at T-023r-4-7 (Codex round 5).** The main-side dispatcher for the reverse leg registered in [api-payload-contracts.md](../architecture/contracts/api-payload-contracts.md) §Shell-Hosted Method Registry: a method table keyed by registered name, request-id correlation with a bounded timeout, the supervised-child port-identity assertion, and refusal of any unregistered name. It is handed its `MessagePortMain` by `daemon-supervisor.ts` and holds no port of its own, so it cannot outlive the child it answers. Reachable from no `SidekicksBridge` namespace (CP-023-9).
- `apps/desktop/src/main/bridge/webauthn.ts` — **created.** `webAuthn.register` / `signIn`, each taking **no** ceremony parameter (I-023-16): the handler fetches the server-issued options from the control plane, validates them against the **paired control-plane origin** — the one main pinned when the install was bound to its control plane, held in main-process configuration and chosen by no renderer, corrected from "build-pinned relying party" at Codex round 5 because an `rpId` compiled into the shell would fail the origin check on every self-hosted deployment ([ADR-020](../decisions/020-v1-deployment-model-and-oss-license.md) parity; `Spec-023 §WebAuthn Credential Flow`) — and dispatches to the per-platform strategy from `webauthn/*.ts`. The sign-in options fetch is issued **uncredentialed**, because a participant on a cold install holds nothing to present and `Spec-018 §Required Behavior` makes those two operations unauthenticated; the enrolment fetch is credentialed. It resolves a `WebAuthnCeremonyOutcome` and never an assertion, a PRF output, or a derived key.
- `apps/desktop/src/main/bridge/update.ts` — **created.** `update.getState` / `subscribe` / `requestCheck` / `requestRestart`. Forwards to the `updater.ts` state machine.
- `apps/desktop/src/main/bridge/onboarding.ts` — **created (Plan-023 authors the surface; Plan-026 implements the flow logic).** `onboarding.presentChoice` / `onboarding.telemetryPrompt` per `Spec-026 §Desktop Surface`. Plan-023 registers the stubs + wires the modal window; Plan-026 lands the flow body.
- `apps/desktop/src/main/bridge/invite.ts` — **created.** `invite.subscribePending` / `invite.subscribeOutcome` / `invite.confirmPending` / `invite.retryPending` / `invite.dismissPending` — the deep-link confirmation handoff surface. `subscribePending` is the main → renderer channel `Spec-023 §Deep-Link Invite Flow` step 3 pins (display metadata plus the opaque reference, never the token); `confirmPending` is step 5's renderer signal, resolving the reference inside main and driving the `acceptInvite` exchange; `dismissPending` releases a reference the participant declined, so a refused invite does not sit pinned until its TTL. `retryPending` re-drives a preview that failed in transport and takes **no argument** — main already knows which deep link is pending, and an argument-free retry cannot be aimed at a reference the participant never saw. The membership outcome rides `subscribeOutcome`, the bridge event step 6 requires and step 8 names — this file is that event's declared emitter, and the internal `session:joined` main-process signal is what it publishes.
- `apps/desktop/src/main/daemon-supervisor.ts` — **created.** `utilityProcess.fork(daemonEntryPath)` with the full backoff + crash + version-mismatch + `"memory-eviction"` special-case logic from `Spec-023 §Daemon Supervision Lifecycle`. Emits lifecycle events that the renderer observes through the bridge.
- `apps/desktop/src/main/keystore.ts` — **created.** Abstraction over `@napi-rs/keyring` + Electron `safeStorage`. Exports `getSecret(key)` / `setSecret(key, value)` / `deleteSecret(key)`. At startup asserts `safeStorage.isEncryptionAvailable()` + on Linux asserts `safeStorage.getSelectedStorageBackend()` ∉ {`basic_text`, `unknown`}; on failure, surfaces the degradation to `update.subscribe`-style event and sets a `keystoreAvailable: false` flag consumed by Plan-026.
- `apps/desktop/src/main/updater.ts` — **created.** `electron-updater` wrapper + update trust-chain verification + rollback state machine. Before an update applies, retains the prior version in `<app-user-data>/retained-versions/` and tracks its version, retained-copy path, integrity hash in `apps/desktop/src/main/state/updater-state.json`; retention failure refuses the update fail-closed. On post-apply daemon-handshake failure, re-installs from the integrity-verified retained copy — never from the live executable path, which the apply itself overwrites — and re-launches. Scheduled update checks on 4-hour cadence + startup + explicit user request.
- `apps/desktop/src/main/webauthn/index.ts` — **created.** Strategy dispatcher, and — from the Codex round-3 fold — the home of the main-owned `PrfKeyDerivationService` port that CP-023-9 registers as Plan-022's Tier-8 KEK producer, reachable from no bridge namespace. `process.platform` selects the binding module and settles nothing else — `darwin` → `mac.ts`, `win32` → `windows.ts`, `linux` → `linux.ts`. Every module exports `createCredential` / `getAssertion` / `deriveKek` with identical signatures — the third renamed off the retired bridge method's name at the Codex round-4 sweep, since `deriveKeyMaterial` now names nothing that exists and `PrfKeyDerivationService.deriveKek` is what calls it — and `fallback-dag.ts` is reached from **any** of the three when that host's ceremony-time capability probe reports no usable authenticator or no PRF (I-023-15).
- `apps/desktop/src/main/webauthn/mac.ts` — **created.** Binding to the own-built `apps/desktop/native/webauthn-darwin/` addon, which drives the platform and security-key providers on independent arms. Guarded at load time by `process.platform === 'darwin'`. Reads `LAContext.canEvaluatePolicy(.deviceOwnerAuthentication)` through the addon as an **advisory diagnostic that gates nothing** — credential-free, recorded beside the outcome rather than consulted before it, since `PublicKeyCredential.getClientCapabilities()` is a Web IDL member with no main-process existence and the one direct framework answer is entitlement-gated browser-class API. The platform provider is attempted on every macOS host (corrected 2026-09-01, Codex round 2). The security-key arm carries its own macOS 26.4 PRF floor.
- `apps/desktop/src/main/webauthn/windows.ts` — **created.** `koffi` binding to `webauthn.dll` — `WebAuthNGetApiVersionNumber`, `WebAuthNIsUserVerifyingPlatformAuthenticatorAvailable`, `WebAuthNAuthenticatorMakeCredential`, `WebAuthNAuthenticatorGetAssertion`, `WebAuthNFreeCredentialAttestation`, `WebAuthNFreeAssertion`, `WebAuthNGetCancellationId`, `WebAuthNCancelCurrentOperation`. Guarded at load time by `process.platform === 'win32'`. Cancellation is a **two-call pair, not one call**: `WebAuthNGetCancellationId(GUID*)` is called before the ceremony and its GUID is carried on the options struct's `pCancellationId` member, and `WebAuthNCancelCurrentOperation(const GUID*)` takes that same GUID — without the first call there is no id to cancel with, and the modal dialog can only be dismissed by the user. Both APIs and both `pCancellationId` members are `WEBAUTHN_API_VERSION_1` baseline, so the pair is gated on nothing. Sends `dwVersion = min(desired, supported)`; PRF at assertion time gates on API ≥ 4 (`pHmacSecretSaltValues`), registration-time PRF on ≥ 6 / ≥ 8. `HRESULT` is read as `int32_t`, every `PCWSTR` as `str16`, and the `HWND` is the **value** inside `BrowserWindow.getNativeWindowHandle()` — never the buffer's address. Out-structs are copied with `koffi.decode()` before being freed, because `koffi.view()` throws under Electron.
- `apps/desktop/src/main/webauthn/linux.ts` — **created.** `koffi` binding to the distribution's `libfido2.so.1` — the `fido_dev_info_*` enumeration; `fido_dev_get_cbor_info` for the `"hmac-secret"` capability scan; the **make-credential** chain `fido_cred_new` → `set_type` → `set_clientdata_hash` → `set_rp` → `set_user` → `set_extensions(FIDO_EXT_HMAC_SECRET)` → `set_rk` → `set_uv` → `fido_dev_make_cred`, reading back `fido_cred_id_ptr` / `_len`; and the `fido_assert_*` chain including `set_hmac_salt` and `set_extensions(FIDO_EXT_HMAC_SECRET)`. Guarded at load time by `process.platform === 'linux'`. Never bundles the library, and **does not depend on a udev-rules package** — device access comes from the host's `systemd` / `udev` (≥ 244) descriptor autodetection. Both `set_uv` calls are pinned explicitly, never left at `FIDO_OPT_OMIT`. Roaming FIDO2 keys only — Linux exposes no platform authenticator. Applies the WebAuthn PRF salt transform itself, since `fido_assert_set_hmac_salt` takes the raw CTAP2 salt (`Spec-023 §WebAuthn Platform-Authenticator Native Module`, guard 3).
- `apps/desktop/native/webauthn-darwin/` — **created, owned by this plan.** The own-built Swift N-API addon over **both** relying-party providers — `ASAuthorizationPlatformPublicKeyCredentialProvider` (`createCredentialAssertionRequest(challenge:)` / `createCredentialRegistrationRequest(challenge:name:userID:)`) and `ASAuthorizationSecurityKeyPublicKeyCredentialProvider` (`createCredentialAssertionRequest(challenge:)` / `createCredentialRegistrationRequest(challenge:displayName:name:userID:)` — one argument wider), both macOS 12.0+ — with `ASAuthorizationPublicKeyCredentialPRFAssertionInput` supplied through each request's directly-named `prf` property, never an `extensions` dictionary. **Two floors:** platform PRF at macOS 15.0+, security-key PRF only at macOS 26.4+. It also exposes the `LAContext.canEvaluatePolicy` platform proxy the probe reads. Built by T-023r-4-3 (Codex round 3 — no task owned the build before): `swift build` per architecture joined with `lipo -create` into one universal `.node`, driven by an `apps/desktop/package.json` script the desktop build depends on, run on the macOS CI leg, and emitted into the `asarUnpack`ed path. Toolchain floor **Xcode 26.4** (Swift 6.3, macOS 26.4 SDK, requires macOS 26.2+), set by the SDK because the security-key `prf` property is a 26.4 symbol; the deployment target stays macOS 12.0. Compiled universal (arm64 + x64) and prebuilt on the macOS CI runner, which already carries Xcode for signing and notarization.
- `apps/desktop/src/main/webauthn/fallback-dag.ts` — **created.** Device Authorization Grant flow: open the system browser to the control-plane's `authorize` endpoint; bind a loopback server to `127.0.0.1:<ephemeral>` (NOT `localhost` — avoids IPv6 `::1` DNS ambiguity per the Apr-2026 research pass); accept one inbound callback bearing the authorization-code + state; 5-min timeout via `AbortSignal.timeout(300_000)`; PKCE S256 via `oauth4webapi` v3.8.5 `randomPKCECodeVerifier()` + `calculatePKCECodeChallenge()`.
- `apps/desktop/src/main/webauthn/kek-fallback.ts` — **created.** The password-derived key-encryption key for a host that reached the DAG fallback: Argon2id (OWASP 2026 parameters, `@noble/hashes`) + XChaCha20-Poly1305 (`@noble/ciphers`), wrapping the long-term Ed25519 identity key into the OS keystore beside the refresh token. The primitives are `ADR-021`'s, reused rather than re-chosen; the passphrase is never persisted and never logged, and nothing derived from it returns across any bridge.
- `apps/desktop/src/main/webauthn/custody-root.ts` — **created at T-023r-4-8 (Codex round 6).** The installation's custody root key and its whole lifecycle — mint at first enrolment, wrap one copy per wrapping key (each credential's PRF-derived KEK and the fallback passphrase-derived one) through the `ADR-021` XChaCha20-Poly1305 primitives, store each copy through `keystore.ts` under `custody-root/credential/<credentialId>` (and `custody-root/passphrase`), unwrap on sign-in, and delete one copy when a credential is removed. The refresh-token and identity-key envelopes are wrapped under the root once and are never rewritten by an enrolment or a removal (I-023-19).
- `apps/desktop/src/main/webauthn/passphrase-prompt.ts` — **created at T-023r-4-7 (Codex round 2).** The coordinator that gets main a passphrase at all: opens the secret-entry window, awaits its single write-only delivery, closes the window, and zeroizes the transferred buffer once `kek-fallback.ts` has derived the KEK. Electron's native dialogs carry no text input, so without this module the §Fallback Behavior custody leg is specified and unreachable.
- `apps/desktop/src/preload/secret-entry.ts` — **created at T-023r-4-7 (Codex round 2).** The secret-entry window's own preload: a **separate entry point**, not a `SidekicksBridge` namespace, exposing exactly one write-only operation that delivers the passphrase to main once and resolves `void`. No accessor on it returns the value, and no session window loads it (`Spec-023 §Preload Bridge Contract`, the secret-entry paragraph).
- `apps/desktop/src/renderer/src/secret-entry/` — **created at T-023r-4-7 (Codex round 2), owned by this plan.** The `secret-entry` window route's body — a passphrase field and a confirm action, nothing else. It is not a pane kind, takes no entry in the auxiliary route registry, and is outside the console subtree.
- `apps/desktop/src/main/crash-reporter.ts` — **created.** Initializes `@sentry/electron/main` with `beforeSend` scrubber per `Spec-023 §Implementation Notes` §Crash Reporting. The scrubber deletes `token`, `dpop`, `session_token`, `prf_output` top-level keys; replaces session IDs with stable SHA-256 hashes; truncates file paths to extension; elides `event.request.data.content`. Renderer-side init lives in `apps/desktop/src/renderer/src/sentry.ts`.
- `apps/desktop/src/main/deep-link.ts` — **created.** `app.setAsDefaultProtocolClient('sidekicks')`. On macOS: `open-url` event handler. On Windows / Linux: `second-instance` event handler (the URL arrives via `argv` on secondary-instance launch). URL parse + token extraction in the main process, then the three-step `Spec-023 §Deep-Link Invite Flow` sequence: resolve display metadata through the non-consuming `invite.preview` read, hand the renderer an opaque pending-invite reference plus that metadata for explicit confirmation, and issue `controlPlane.acceptInvite(token)` only after the participant confirms. The renderer receives only the metadata, the opaque reference, and the post-exchange session capability — never the token.

### Preload

- `apps/desktop/src/preload/index.ts` — **created.** Single `contextBridge.exposeInMainWorld('sidekicks', bridge)` call per Spec-023. The bridge object's type is re-exported from `packages/contracts/src/desktop-bridge.ts`. The concrete object is minted by `createTier1Bridge()`, the factory exported from that same contracts module — its per-namespace `NotImplementedAtTier1Error`-throwing stubs are the named replacement surface downstream plans fill in namespace by namespace as their IPC handlers ship, so a plan wiring a bridge namespace edits that factory rather than re-declaring the bridge shape.
- `apps/desktop/src/preload/invite.ts` — **new, owned by this plan.** The renderer-facing half of the `invite` namespace: `subscribePending` / `subscribeOutcome` / `confirmPending` / `retryPending` / `dismissPending`, each forwarding to the matching main-process handler over `ipcRenderer` and doing nothing else. It resolves no reference and holds no token — the opaque pending-invite reference crosses this file untouched, so I-023-10's main-only resolution survives the hop. `preload/index.ts` gains one import and one spread to mount it, the same one-line namespace-addition shape Plan-026 T5.1 uses for `onboarding`. That shape is the general rule, not an invite detail: a Tier-8 bridge namespace is reachable only with **all three** arms present — the contracts interface member plus its `createTier1Bridge()` stub, the preload namespace file plus its `index.ts` spread, and the main-process handler. Registering the interface and the handler alone leaves every renderer call resolving to the throwing stub.

### Contracts

- `packages/contracts/src/desktop-bridge.ts` — **created by this plan.** The `SidekicksBridge` interface from `Spec-023 §Preload Bridge Contract`, re-exporting the Plan-007 daemon types, the Plan-008 control-plane types, and new types `OpenDialogOptions` / `SaveDialogOptions` / `MessageBoxOptions` / `NotificationOptions` / `FilePathRef` / `PrfInput` / `UpdateState`. Exported as **type-only** (no runtime). The negative-test (`Spec-023 §Acceptance Criteria`: "No auth material appears on the bridge") is a TypeScript conditional-type test in `packages/contracts/src/desktop-bridge.test-d.ts`.

**Bridge namespaces beyond the Spec-023 block (authoring rule).** `Spec-023 §Preload Bridge Contract`'s interface block binds the **Tier-1** bridge shape verbatim; a Tier-8 namespace joins it only where a spec section names this plan as the owner of that surface. Two namespaces land that way and neither is drift: `onboarding` (owned per `Spec-026 §Desktop Surface`, which assigns the surface here and the flow body to Plan-026) and `invite` (owned per [Spec-023 §Deep-Link Invite Flow](../specs/023-desktop-shell-and-renderer.md#deep-link-invite-flow), whose blockquote assigns "the precise reference data shape, its main-process lifecycle, and its expiry / error semantics" to this plan at Tier 8 — a stronger authorization than a cross-spec delegation, since the owning spec is this plan's own). A namespace with no such named delegation is a review rejection, and each one added here extends the same `desktop-bridge.ts` interface so T-023r-2-1's exhaustive registration keeps failing the build on an unhandled method.

### Renderer

- `apps/desktop/src/renderer/src/main.tsx` — **created.** React entrypoint. Initializes Sentry renderer-side (`@sentry/electron/renderer`). Renders `<App />` into `#root`.
- `apps/desktop/src/renderer/src/App.tsx` — **created.** Top-level router + layout shell. Mounts each Signature Feature view as a route; the views themselves are created by their owning plans in their own `apps/desktop/src/renderer/src/` subtrees per the dependency map's §2 (`Spec-023 §Signature Feature Composition Sketches` names the owner of each). This plan owns the router, the layout chrome, and the composition points — never a feature view's body.
- `apps/desktop/src/renderer/src/shell/` — **created by this plan (Tier 8).** The Plan-023-owned shell subtree: layout chrome, routing surface, and the session composer. `apps/desktop/src/renderer/src/shell/MessageComposer.tsx` is the composer's pinned home — deliberately in this plan's shell subtree and never inside a feature-view tree owned by Plan-013 or Plan-016, so the typing-indicator producer leg has an unambiguous owner. It drives `activity.typing` set / refresh / clear through the daemon presence handler surface Plan-002 T3.5 ships, never by direct Awareness injection (CP-023-4).
- `apps/desktop/src/renderer/src/console/` — **registered by this plan 2026-09-01; built on lane 1 as the Tier-1 explicit-label supplement Phase 1C** (T-023p-1C-1..8, dispatched by label per [CONTRIBUTING.md §How Code Lands: Work Classification](../../CONTRIBUTING.md#how-code-lands-work-classification) lane 1 — `Plan-023` title token, manifest rows, preflight) inside the `Spec-023 §Console Design (Meridian)` envelope. The console subtree: the Meridian tokens and primitives, the icon rail, the all-sessions list, the session workspace (cast bar, deck, sidebar), the pane-kind registry and its single mount door, the auxiliary-window routes, the command palette and keybinding service, the settings and operator pages' frame, the fixture bridge (`bridge/` with the live and fixture implementations, the scenario manifest, and the scenarios), and the console's test tiers. **Phase 1C rule.** The tree is built ahead of Phase 6 because every surface it holds is a projection over bridge namespaces the corpus already registers or names on `§Console growth slate`, and because `Spec-023 §Console Design (Meridian)` is the approved envelope it stays within; a console task that mints a wire method, event type, error code, table, or column is lane 4 and amends first. It **absorbs by import** the shipped Tier-1 renderer components — `session-bootstrap/`, `session-members/`, `runtime-node-attach/`, and any later plan-owned subtree — and never re-authors or moves a body another plan owns; it touches nothing under `timeline/`, `usage-meters/`, or `run-controls/`, whose owners mount into it on the CP-023-6 / CP-023-7 host-mounts shape, while `shell/` is this plan's own tree and the composer keeps its pinned home there (authored by T-023p-1C-3, wired live by T-023r-6-2). **Adoption.** Phase 6 adopts the console by making `shell/AppShell.tsx` the thin host and by moving the shell chrome T-023r-6-1 names into the console's app frame (T-023r-6-1 EXTEND); the invite-confirmation surface stays at its pinned `shell/` home and is hosted by the console workspace. Until Phase 1B ships, the console's persistence layer runs on an in-memory adapter and reports that state in its own settings page.
- `apps/desktop/src/renderer/src/sentry.ts` — **created.** Renderer-side `@sentry/electron/renderer` init. No `beforeSend` override (main-process scrubber catches everything that upload-ships; renderer stays thin).
- `apps/desktop/src/renderer/src/sidekicks-bridge.d.ts` — **already created by Plan-002 Phase 6; Tier 8 EXTENDS, does not CREATE.** The `window.sidekicks` ambient bridge type, hoisted by Plan-002 Phase 6 out of `SessionBootstrap.tsx`'s `declare global` block into this dedicated `.d.ts` (the conventional Electron+Vite contextBridge-global typing pattern). Tier-8 work extends this surface as the bridge grows; it does not re-create the file or re-declare the global.

### Build / Config

- `apps/desktop/electron.vite.config.ts` — **created.** `electron-vite` v5 config: main / preload / renderer targets; source-code protection (opt-in; obfuscates main + preload output); HMR on dev; strict TypeScript.
- `apps/desktop/electron-builder.yml` — **created.** Multi-platform config: macOS (`.dmg`, `.zip`, arm64 + x64, hardened runtime + notarization), Windows (NSIS + MSI, x64, Artifact Signing OR EV cert), Linux (`.AppImage`, `.deb`, `.rpm`, x64 + arm64). ASAR packing; auto-update feed URL.
- `apps/desktop/build/fuses.ts` — **created.** Uses `@electron/fuses`'s `flipFuses()` to apply the nine-fuse posture from `Spec-023 §Security Hardening Baseline`. Called after packaging, **before** signing (fuse-flip invalidates the signature; signing must be the last step).
- `apps/desktop/build/asar-digest.ts` — **created.** Runs `asar integrity-digest on <app-path>` via `@electron/asar` v4.1.0+ after fuse-flip, before signing, so the embedded digest is part of what gets signed.
- `apps/desktop/build/code-sign.ts` — **created.** Orchestrates signing: macOS (detached `xcrun notarytool submit` + resumable poll + `xcrun stapler staple` — see §Notarization Submission And Poll below); Windows (Azure Artifact Signing basic-SKU signing, or EV-cert fallback via `signtool`); Linux (GPG-sign `.deb` / `.rpm`).
- `apps/desktop/eslint.config.mjs` — **created.** Flat-config ESLint; `no-restricted-imports` rule bans `require` / `process` / `global` / `electron` modules from the renderer (`apps/desktop/src/renderer/src/**`). This is a **CI gate** — any renderer file importing Node built-ins fails the build.
- `apps/desktop/knip.json` — **created 2026-09-02.** The `structure:dead-code` gate's configuration: entry roots, project globs, the Vitest plugin, and `ignoreExportsUsedInFile`. Run from the workspace root, so the pnpm catalog and the root tooling manifest resolve; `--treat-config-hints-as-errors` makes a stale pattern fail the gate. Its only admissible `ignore` entry names the task that will import the symbol (I-023-14).
- `apps/desktop/.dependency-cruiser.mjs` — **created 2026-09-02.** The `structure:layering` gate's rule set: `no-circular`, `no-orphans`, the two process-boundary rules, `shared-imports-nothing`, `console-not-plan-subtree`, and one `console-layering-<family>` rule per family encoding the `Spec-023 §Console Test Tiers` console family DAG as forbidden upward edges (I-023-14).
- `apps/desktop/AGENTS.md` + `apps/desktop/CLAUDE.md` — **created 2026-09-02.** The package's structure standard, tool-neutral so every agent in the repository reads it; `CLAUDE.md` is the one-line `@AGENTS.md` import. It restates the layout, the import boundaries, the chokepoints, the module shape, and the two mechanical gates as binding developer rules; it mints nothing and states no rule this plan or `Spec-023` does not already carry.

#### Notarization Submission And Poll

`Spec-023 §Code Signing And Notarization` supplies the **requirement** — the release pipeline "must include timeout + retry logic rather than synchronous blocking on notarization" — and records the observed Apple-queue delay range of **24–120+ hours**. It states no timeout value; this plan owns the concrete mechanism and the ceiling, set above that observed range so a normal queue never trips it. The design is **detached-submit + resumable poll**, not a single blocking wait:

1. **Submit detached.** `xcrun notarytool submit <artifact> --no-wait -f json` returns immediately with a submission `id`. `--no-wait` is `notarytool`'s default; the flag is passed explicitly so the shape survives a future default change.
2. **Persist the submission id.** The `id` is written to the release job's artifact store keyed by `{version, platform, artifact}` before the job may exit. This is what makes the poll resumable: a CI job that ends — timeout, runner eviction, deliberate release-engineer detach — loses nothing, because Apple's Notary service keeps processing regardless. `notarytool --timeout` is a **polling** limit, not a submission cancel: per the tool's own help text, "The Notary service will continue processing even if the timeout is reached."
3. **Poll in bounded increments.** Each poll invocation runs `xcrun notarytool wait <id> --timeout 30m -f json` (or `notarytool info <id>` for a non-blocking status read). A poll that returns `In Progress` re-queues rather than failing the release. No single CI job is held open for days.
4. **Overall abandonment ceiling: 168 hours (7 days)** from first submission, accumulated across resumed polls. The ceiling sits above the spec's documented 120-hour upper bound with headroom, so it is an abandonment signal for a genuinely stuck submission — never a resubmission trigger for a merely slow queue. Resubmission is a manual release-engineering decision after the ceiling trips, because Apple's queue treats a resubmission as new work and a resubmit-on-timeout loop is self-defeating.
5. **Terminal handling.** `Accepted` → `xcrun stapler staple <artifact>` and the artifact is releasable. `Invalid` / `Rejected` → `xcrun notarytool log <id>` is captured into the release record and the artifact is not distributed.

Pinned tool version: `notarytool` 1.1.2 (41), the version shipped with the CI image's Xcode command-line tools; the detached-submit + `info` / `wait` / `log`-by-submission-id surface this design depends on is verified against it. Bumping the CI image re-verifies `xcrun notarytool submit --help` before the release cut.

### Test

- `apps/desktop/test/e2e/` — **created.** Playwright `_electron` spec files.
  - `launch.spec.ts` — asserts app launches, main window appears, renderer bundle loads via custom protocol, `window.sidekicks` is defined, `window.require` is `undefined`.
  - `fuses.spec.ts` — asserts `@electron/fuses.getCurrentFuseWire()` on the packaged binary returns the posture from Spec-023.
  - `bridge-surface.spec.ts` — asserts the `window.sidekicks` surface matches the type re-exported from `packages/contracts/src/desktop-bridge.ts`; asserts no property name containing `token` / `dpop` / `prf` is exposed.
  - `daemon-supervision.spec.ts` — asserts daemon start, `DaemonHello` received within 10s, daemon crash triggers restart, 5 consecutive crashes surface persistent-error state, `"memory-eviction"` exits do NOT increment the failure counter.
  - `deep-link.spec.ts` — asserts `sidekicks://invite/<token>` invocation routes through main process, raw token does not appear in any bridge-transcript event.
  - `webauthn-mac.spec.ts` (darwin-only) — asserts what a hosted macOS runner can actually assert, which is credential-free: the own-built `webauthn-darwin` addon loads and is universal (both slices present); the caller's challenge and the resolved `rpId` reach the `ASAuthorization*` request unmodified, read back through the addon's request-shape accessor rather than through a ceremony; and an unsigned, unentitled hosted build surfaces the **expected** entitlement-failure diagnostic rather than a hang or a generic throw. It asserts no assertion and no PRF byte — the runner has no Touch ID, no enrolled credential, and no associated-domains entitlement, so a passing assertion here would be evidence of a stub. It additionally asserts, on a synthetic `rawAuthenticatorData` fixture, that a UV flag disagreeing with the recorded mode derives nothing and routes to the fallback, and that an addon whose slice for this architecture is absent produces the load diagnostic rather than an unhandled rejection (both Codex round 3, both credential-free). The real assertion lives in the recorded signed-hardware matrix in `Spec-023 §WebAuthn Platform-Authenticator Native Module`, which stays [BL-108](../backlog.md)-gated. Re-scoped 2026-09-01 from the Vault12 addon; the assertion-shape claim was withdrawn in the same pass as unrunnable on hosted CI.
  - `webauthn-windows.spec.ts` (win32-only) — asserts what a hosted runner with no Windows Hello enrollment can actually assert: the FFI binding loads and resolves `WebAuthNGetApiVersionNumber` from `webauthn.dll`; the request and response struct layouts round-trip byte-exact against a fake `webauthn.dll` fixture the test supplies; `WebAuthNIsUserVerifyingPlatformAuthenticatorAvailable` returning `FALSE` suppresses only the platform arm and still attempts the roaming ceremony, reaching `fallback-dag.ts` only when that attempt also fails — the negative control for I-023-15, since routing straight to fallback on a `FALSE` would strand every desktop whose only authenticator is an attached key; the cancellation pair round-trips, a GUID from `WebAuthNGetCancellationId` reaching `pCancellationId` and `WebAuthNCancelCurrentOperation` settling a pending modal call as a refusal; and **two** version-floor fixtures degrade to the fallback rather than silently dropping the extension, one per PRF route, because the two live on different structs at different floors — an assertion-time `pHmacSecretSaltValues` request against a fixture declaring API < 4, and a registration-time `bEnablePrf` request against a fixture declaring API < 6. Two more fixtures land at Codex round 3: an assertion whose authenticator-data UV bit disagrees with the recorded mode derives nothing and routes to the fallback, and a fake `webauthn.dll` exporting none of the declared entry points produces the load diagnostic and the fallback rather than an unhandled rejection. The real Windows Hello ceremony is a recorded manual verification, not a CI assertion (§Risks And Blockers).
  - `webauthn-linux.spec.ts` (linux-only) — the CI-assertable properties against `libfido2`: the binding loads and resolves both chains' entry points, `fido_dev_info_manifest` and `fido_dev_make_cred`; the struct layouts round-trip against a fake library fixture; **both** `set_uv` calls are asserted to be pinned, since a fixture that records `FIDO_OPT_OMIT` reaching the library is the negative control for guard 4 and is the only automated check on a defect that otherwise surfaces as an intermittently wrong key; the registration chain sets `FIDO_EXT_HMAC_SECRET` (`0x01`) and never `FIDO_EXT_HMAC_SECRET_MC` (`0x20`); and an empty device manifest (no key attached — the hosted-runner condition) routes to `fallback-dag.ts`, while a fixture presenting a security-token-tagged node that fails `access(R_OK|W_OK)` produces the **permission** diagnostic rather than the no-key one, which libfido2 alone cannot distinguish. Codex round 3 adds the response-side half of guard 4 — an assertion fixture whose authenticator-data UV bit disagrees with the recorded mode derives nothing and routes to the fallback, which is the check the request-side pinning cannot make — and a library-absent fixture asserting that an unresolvable `libfido2.so.1` produces the load diagnostic and the fallback rather than an unhandled rejection. The real ceremony needs a physical FIDO2 key passed through and is likewise a recorded manual verification.
  - `webauthn-fallback.spec.ts` (all three platforms) — asserts DAG loopback server binds to `127.0.0.1` (not `localhost`), accepts one callback, closes within 5 minutes, rejects `plain` PKCE method. Platform-scoped to all three since 2026-09-01: the fallback is a per-host capability degradation, not a Windows / Linux assignment.
  - `keystore.spec.ts` — asserts `safeStorage.isEncryptionAvailable()`; on Linux, asserts the plaintext-fallback backend is refused.
  - `updater-rollback.spec.ts` — simulated post-apply daemon-handshake failure triggers re-install from the integrity-verified retained copy — never from the live executable path; simulated retention failure refuses the update before apply.

## Data And Storage Changes

No Postgres or Durable Object schema changes — the desktop shell stores only:

### OS-level keystore entries (platform-backed)

- Key `ai-sidekicks:paseto-refresh-token` — PASETO v4.local refresh token.
- Key `ai-sidekicks:daemon-session-token` — cached across daemon restarts for fast handshake (rotated on daemon restart per Security Architecture §Session Token; the cache is a latency optimization only).
- Key `ai-sidekicks:participant-identity-key:<participant-id>` — participant-identity private key material (one per participant identity).
- Key `ai-sidekicks:prf-wrapping-key:<participant-id>` — WebAuthn PRF-derived refresh-token-wrapping key.

All four keys are accessed through `keystore.ts` only. Renderer code must not import `keystore.ts` (enforced by ESLint restricted-imports).

### Shell-local config

- `<app-user-data>/config/settings.json` — window state (size, position, maximized), non-sensitive user preferences. Read on startup, written on `before-quit`.
- `<app-user-data>/config/updater-state.json` — current version, retained-version record (version, retained-copy path, integrity hash), `rollback_blacklist`, last-check timestamp, staged-update metadata. Managed by `updater.ts` rollback state machine.
- `<app-user-data>/retained-versions/` — the retained copy of the prior version (bounded to one prior version), written before an update applies and deleted only on the confirmed-healthy post-apply daemon handshake — never on the rollback path. The sole rollback re-install source. Managed by `updater.ts` rollback state machine.
- `<app-user-data>/config/supervisor-state.json` — daemon PID, last-heartbeat timestamp, restart attempt count. Diagnostic only; not authoritative for daemon state.

`<app-user-data>` resolves per Electron `app.getPath('userData')`: `~/Library/Application Support/AI Sidekicks` (macOS), `%APPDATA%\AI Sidekicks` (Windows), `~/.config/AI Sidekicks` (Linux).

### Build-time asset

- `apps/desktop/src/main/updater-public-key.ts` — **created, committed to source.** The Ed25519 public key used to verify `manifest.sig` over the update feed's `latest-*.yml` manifest bytes — the root of the update trust chain (pinned key → signed manifest → hash-bound artifact) — embedded at compile time as a `Uint8Array` constant. Update-key rotation is a build-time change, not a runtime change. This key is distinct from the code-signing certificate (`Spec-023 §Pitfalls To Avoid` names conflating them as a rotation-mishap vector).

## API And Transport Changes

### Preload bridge contract (new, owned by this plan)

The full `SidekicksBridge` interface from `Spec-023 §Preload Bridge Contract` lives in `packages/contracts/src/desktop-bridge.ts`. Key requirements:

- All methods return `Promise<T>` or a synchronous primitive; no callbacks (`ipcRenderer`-style pub-sub uses the `subscribe(...): Unsubscribe` pattern).
- No property or method signature references `Token`, `DPoP`, `PRF`, or `SessionToken` in its type or return shape.
- `FilePathRef` is an opaque branded type (`string & { __brand: 'FilePathRef' }`); the renderer cannot dereference a filesystem path — dereferencing requires a second bridge round trip.

### Daemon JSON-RPC forwarding

- Wire format: Content-Length-framed JSON-RPC 2.0 per Spec-007 / ADR-009.
- Transport: Unix domain socket (macOS / Linux) or named pipe (Windows) per Spec-007. The shell instantiates the client from Plan-007's shared client SDK.
- The main process attaches the daemon session token as an RPC-level header; the renderer never sees the token.

### Control-plane tRPC + WebSocket forwarding

- Wire format: tRPC v11 over HTTPS for request/response; the relay WSS connection speaks [Spec-008](../specs/008-control-plane-relay-and-session-join.md) §Message Framing **binary wire frames** for relay event subscription (`subscribeRelay`), while presence/collaboration events use the WebSocket **JSON-RPC 2.0** subset -- per ADR-014 / Spec-008.
- The main process attaches `Authorization: DPoP <paseto-v4.public>` + `DPoP: <signed-proof>` headers (RFC 9449 §7.1) to every outbound HTTP request and the presence/collaboration WebSocket handshake; the **relay WSS** connection carries no such headers — it authenticates solely via the [Spec-008](../specs/008-control-plane-relay-and-session-join.md) §Relay Connection Lifecycle `Sec-WebSocket-Protocol` subprotocol carrying the short-lived R3-issued `connectionToken` (PASETO v4.public, `aud=relay-connect`, TTL 300s), its sole credential (browsers cannot set custom WS headers — the bearer-token-over-subprotocol idiom), never `Authorization`/`DPoP`. **A second carve-out is the anonymous-procedure arm:** header attachment is conditioned on holding a credential, and `invite.preview` — anonymous per `Spec-002 §Interfaces And Contracts` and excluded from Plan-008 I-008-4's gated set — is therefore issuable with no headers at all, so a signed-out deep-link recipient reaches the confirmation surface before authenticating — anonymous meaning authentication is not _required_ here, not that a held credential must be stripped.
- Response sanitization: the main process strips `set-cookie` headers and any upstream auth-material-carrying headers before returning the response body to the renderer.

### Deep-link transport

- Protocol: `sidekicks://<action>/<...args>`. Actions: `invite/<token>`, `session/<id>`.
- URL arrival:
  - macOS: `app.on('open-url', (event, url) => ...)` handler.
  - Windows / Linux: `app.on('second-instance', (event, argv) => argv.find(a => a.startsWith('sidekicks://')))` handler; the URL is the last `argv` entry on secondary-instance launch.
- Metadata resolution (pre-acceptance): `invite.preview` over the same main-process control-plane client `bridge/control-plane.ts` instantiates for `acceptInvite` — not the daemon gateway, because the confined token must not leave the main process (CP-023-5). The procedure is **anonymous** (`Spec-002 §Interfaces And Contracts`; its anonymity is a recorded Plan-008 I-008-4 gated-endpoint exclusion), so the confirmation resolves and renders before any credential for the target session exists, and credential presence is never a precondition for rendering it.
- Token lifecycle: parsed in main process; previewed non-consumingly; on explicit confirmation exchanged for a session capability via `controlPlane.call('acceptInvite', { token })`; raw token is memory-discarded after exchange and **never** crosses the bridge. Neither the preview nor the accept is issued without the participant-facing confirmation between them.

### Update feed transport

- Transport: HTTPS GET against the project-operated static artifact store.
- Manifest format: `electron-updater` v6.8.9's YAML manifest (`latest-mac.yml`, `latest.yml`, `latest-linux.yml`) + an Ed25519-signed `manifest.sig` covering the YAML bytes. The manifest's per-file artifact-hash entries (`sha512` in the `electron-updater` v6.8.9 manifest schema) are what bind each downloaded artifact to the signed manifest; no per-artifact signature exists.
- Artifact format: `.dmg` / `.zip` (macOS), `.exe` NSIS + `.msi` (Windows), `.AppImage` + `.deb` + `.rpm` (Linux).
- NSIS-only block-map delta updates enabled; macOS + Linux download full payloads per [`electron-updater` docs](https://www.electron.build/auto-update).

### Console growth slate

Every console surface is built now against the fixture bridge (`Spec-023 §Console Design (Meridian)` §The fixture bridge) and wired live only once the wire it consumes is registered by that wire's owner. The table is the console's honest ledger of what it does not yet have: each row names the wire, the document that owns it, and the console surface family that consumes it. A row leaves the table when its amendment lands; a console PR that wires a surface live against an unregistered wire is a review rejection, and the scenario manifest's per-method live-status field is checked against this table by a test.

| Wire the console needs | Owning document | Console surface that consumes it |

| `browser` pane kind, the `browser.*` bridge namespace, and the two node-wide browser settings | `Spec-023 §Preload Bridge Contract` + `§Console Design (Meridian)`; the embedded-browser Type-2 ADR (`Spec-023 §ADR Triggers`) | browser pane |
| Browser tool set as [Spec-005](../specs/005-provider-driver-contract-and-capabilities.md) callback-tool rows plus the daemon-to-desktop tool-call relay | `Spec-005 §Required Behavior` (the session callback-tool registry); the same ADR | browser pane |
| Terminal pane as a renderer surface with the shared-terminal write lease's renderer obligations | [Spec-003](../specs/003-runtime-node-attach.md) | terminal pane (its only gate — it consumes no `browser.*` wire and does not wait on the embedded-browser ADR) |
| Live-listener probe for the dev-server chip | Spec-007, or the embedded-browser ADR | browser pane (the dev-server chip) |
| Session lifecycle verbs — rename, archive, close, reactivate (one amendment) | [Spec-001](../specs/001-shared-session-core.md) | all-sessions list, workspace header |
| `SessionRead` snapshot read wired to a store's base state, and a participant's session-directory read (no directory read is registered anywhere) | Spec-007 (the daemon method union); Spec-001 (the `session.read` payloads) | session-store initialisation, all-sessions list, auxiliary context picker |

Registered nowhere, and not one of the thirteen rows above: every registered run operation — `runStart`, `runRead`, `runCancel`, `runResume` — addresses ONE run by an id the caller must already hold, so a surface that lists a session's runs has no wire to ask and no id to ask it with | `Spec-017 §Interfaces And Contracts` (the run operations, none of which enumerates); [Plan-017](./017-workflow-authoring-and-execution.md) (the shared-contracts and client-SDK registration an enumeration would join) | workflows destination (the runs it holds) |

Two operations and never one: an update carrying no goal is malformed rather than a clear | `Spec-016 §Session Goals`; [Plan-016](./016-multi-agent-channels-and-orchestration.md) (the shared-contracts and client-SDK registration) | approvals pane (the session goal card) |
