| The shell's own status as one feed — the daemon supervisor's step and its attempt count out of five, the `daemon.hello` negotiation ack (`compatible`, `protocolVersion`, `reason`, `daemonSupportedProtocols`), the loopback-fallback signal, and the keystore-unavailable signal. Every one of them is a main-process fact and none is a daemon call: the renderer is not a direct daemon client, the ack belongs to the connection the main process holds, and a second handshake issued from the renderer would be refused as one already completed. `DaemonHelloAck` is published by `packages/contracts` and served by no bridge namespace; the supervisor state, the transport posture, and the keystore verdict are published by nothing at all | `Spec-023 §Preload Bridge Contract` (no namespace carries any of it) + `§Daemon Supervision Lifecycle` (the six steps and the five-attempt ladder) + `§Fallback Behavior` (the loopback fallback and the offline read-only mode) + `§Native Keystore` (the memory-only degradation); [Spec-007](../specs/007-local-ipc-and-daemon-control.md) (the `DaemonHelloAck` shape) | frame shell-state chrome — the daemon chip, the version banner, the reconnect and read-only banners, and the loopback / keystore notice strip |
| `onboarding.presentChoice` and `onboarding.telemetryPrompt` — the two preload-bridge methods `Spec-026 §Desktop Surface` names. Neither is on the shipped bridge and neither is a JSON-RPC wire method: they are the main-process hosts for the relay choice's secret entry (the self-host admin token, which `Spec-026 §Pitfalls To Avoid` forbids rendering in the renderer, so only an opaque handle comes back) and for the telemetry answer, which that spec makes its own step after the choice resolves. | `Spec-023 §Preload Bridge Contract` (which admits `onboarding` by name as delegated) + [Spec-026](../specs/026-first-run-onboarding.md) §Desktop Surface | first-run onboarding (group A) |

## Invariants

These promote behaviors the plan previously stated only in narrative into a named `I-023-N` namespace, so every Tier-8 task's `Verifies invariant:` marker resolves against a plan-side id instead of pointing at an unnumbered `Spec-023 §Acceptance Criteria` bullet (the fabricated-`AC-N` failure mode — see [§Notes](#notes)). Added 2026-08-10 by the Tier-8 remainder readiness audit (NS-20, C-023-3).

| ID | Invariant | Tasks |
| --- | --- | --- |
| I-023-1 | No property or method on the `SidekicksBridge` surface carries auth material: no name matches `/token\|dpop\|prf\|secret/i` and no return type transitively contains one (`Spec-023 §Preload Bridge Contract`, `Spec-023 §Acceptance Criteria`). | T-023p-1-4 (Tier 1, type-level); T-023r-2-1, T-023r-2-2, T-023r-2-3, T-023p-1B-3 (project registration; enables the per-module units), T-023r-4-7, T-023r-8-1 |
| I-023-2 | Every `BrowserWindow` is constructed with the locked `webPreferences` block and loads over the custom protocol, never `file://` (`Spec-023 §Security Hardening Baseline`). | T-023p-1-3 (Tier 1, build-time assertion); T-023p-1B-1, T-023p-1B-2 (Tier 1 supplement — the load and both factories); T-023r-3-4, T-023r-4-7, T-023r-8-1 |
| I-023-3 | The packaging pipeline orders fuse-flip → ASAR digest embed → sign; signing is always last, because the two preceding steps invalidate any prior signature (`Spec-023 §Build And Packaging`, `Spec-023 §Code Signing And Notarization`). | T-023r-7-1, T-023r-7-2, T-023r-7-3 |
| I-023-4 | A `"memory-eviction"` daemon exit restarts without incrementing the failure counter; only non-memory-eviction exits advance the backoff ladder toward persistent-error state (`Spec-023 §Daemon Supervision Lifecycle`). | T-023r-3-1, T-023p-1B-3 (project registration; enables the per-module units), T-023r-8-1 |
| I-023-5 | A raw invite token parsed from a `sidekicks://` URL never crosses the preload bridge and never appears in a bridge-transcript event (`Spec-023 §Deep-Link Invite Flow`). | T-023r-5-3, T-023r-5-4, T-023r-5-5, T-023r-8-1 |
| I-023-6 | On Linux, a `basic_text` or `unknown` `safeStorage` backend is refused; long-lived token persistence degrades to memory-only rather than writing plaintext (`Spec-023 §Native Keystore`). | T-023r-4-1, T-023r-4-2, T-023p-1B-3 (project registration; enables the per-module units) |
| I-023-7 | Test-only probe code is compile-time eliminated from release bundles; the `__SIDEKICKS_SMOKE_BUILD__` define collapses the guard so no probe body survives tree-shaking (`Spec-023 §Pitfalls To Avoid`). | T-023r-8-3 |
| I-023-8 | The renderer imports no Node built-in, no Electron module, and nothing under `src/main/`, `src/preload/`, or `apps/desktop/native/` (re-derived 2026-09-01: the native tree is a trusted-side sibling of `src/`, so neither prior prefix reached it); the ESLint restricted-imports rule is a CI gate, not advice (`Spec-023 §Trust Stance`). | T-023p-1-6 (Tier 1, partial ban list); T-023r-2-6 |
| I-023-9 | No `sidekicks://invite/<token>` URL is accepted without an explicit participant confirmation. The non-consuming `invite.preview` is the **only** control-plane call the deep-link path issues before that confirmation, and it performs zero writes and never burns the `jti`; on any typed preview refusal the main process mints **no** pending reference at all, so there is nothing for the renderer to confirm and no fall-through to acceptance exists (`Spec-023 §Deep-Link Invite Flow` steps 3–5, property (b)). | T-023r-5-3, T-023r-5-5, T-023r-6-3, T-023r-8-1, T-023r-8-5 |
| I-023-11 | The renderer scheme is registered through `protocol.registerSchemesAsPrivileged` as `standard: true` and `secure: true` **before** `app.ready`, so the renderer origin has IndexedDB and `localStorage`; a registration after ready, or a scheme left non-standard, fails the startup-composition test (`Spec-023 §Renderer Bundle`, `Spec-023 §Console Design (Meridian)` §Persistence on the renderer scheme). Added 2026-09-01 (NS-97). | T-023p-1B-1, T-023p-1B-3, T-023r-3-4 |
| I-023-12 | Every window other than the main one is a `BrowserWindow` constructed by the same locked-`webPreferences` factory as the main window, loads the same bundle at a window route over the custom protocol, carries its own preload and its own bridge instance, and shares no in-memory store and no auth material with any other window; an **auxiliary** window additionally subscribes to the daemon itself (`Spec-023 §Main Process Responsibilities`, `Spec-023 §Console Design (Meridian)` §The surface set, `Spec-023 §Acceptance Criteria` final bullet). Added 2026-09-01 (NS-97). **Subject widened 2026-09-01 (Codex round 2)** from "every auxiliary window" so it reaches T-023r-4-7's secret-entry window, which satisfies every conjunct except the daemon subscription — it subscribes to nothing — and which is deliberately **not** a member of the two-window auxiliary set; without the widening the one window built to hold a passphrase would have been the one window whose isolation no invariant covered. | T-023p-1B-2, T-023p-1B-3, T-023r-3-4, T-023r-4-7, T-023r-8-1 |
| I-023-13 | The fixture bridge is typed from the same `packages/contracts` desktop-bridge types as the live bridge, is shape-identical to `SidekicksBridge` namespace for namespace, is compiled out of release bundles by the same `define` gate as the smoke probes, and its scenario manifest's per-method live-status field agrees with `§Console growth slate` — a method marked live whose row is still on the slate, or a slate row whose method is marked live, fails the console's architecture tier; and the fixture-only **console growth port** (`console/bridge/growth-port/growth-port.ts`) carries exactly one method per open slate row and none for a landed one, the live bridge's port resolving every method to the typed refusal that renders the _not checked_ absence, so a surface consuming an unregistered wire compiles against the port and never against a widened `SidekicksBridge` (`Spec-023 §Console Design (Meridian)` §The fixture bridge). Added 2026-09-01 (NS-97). | T-023p-1C-1..7, T-023r-6-1 |
| I-023-14 | Every console PR passes `Spec-023 §Console Test Tiers` in full, the budgets `Spec-023 §Console Design (Meridian)` §Budgets sets — bundle size, p95 frame time under four streaming lanes, renderer heap at rest and under the flagship replay, idle and streaming CPU, per-terminal memory, and first-ledger-row latency — and both `apps/desktop` structure gates the `Spec-023 §Console Libraries` structure-enforcement row admits: `structure:dead-code` (no file, export, type, or dependency the entry points do not reach) and `structure:layering` (no cycle, no orphan, no process-boundary break, and no upward edge against the console family DAG). All of it as CI gates, never as review notes; a console PR that raises a budget or takes a `knip` ignore entry without naming the consuming task amends the spec first. Added 2026-09-01 (NS-97); widened 2026-09-02 (NS-100). | T-023p-1C-1..8 |
| I-023-15 | WebAuthn capability is decided by a per-host probe at ceremony time and never inferred from `process.platform`, which selects a binding module and settles nothing else. Every leg reads its own credential-free capability call before the ceremony — `LAContext.canEvaluatePolicy` on macOS (an explicit proxy: no non-browser native call answers the capability question, and `getClientCapabilities()` is a Web IDL member absent from the main process), `WebAuthNGetApiVersionNumber()` plus `WebAuthNIsUserVerifyingPlatformAuthenticatorAvailable()` on Windows, `fido_dev_info_manifest` plus `fido_dev_get_cbor_info` on Linux. A **platform**-authenticator probe gates the platform arm only, never the ceremony, and only where the OS publishes a direct availability call — Windows' `WebAuthNIsUserVerifyingPlatformAuthenticatorAvailable` does; macOS publishes only a proxy, so its probe is advisory and **gates nothing**, the platform provider being attempted on every host (Codex round 2). The roaming arm is attempted independently on both macOS and Windows, and fallback is reached only when every arm this host offers has been attempted. An assertion that returns with the PRF output absent is treated as "no PRF on this host" rather than derived from, so a host with no usable arm routes alone to `fallback-dag.ts` (`Spec-023 §WebAuthn Platform-Authenticator Native Module`, `Spec-023 §Fallback Behavior`). | T-023r-4-3, T-023r-4-5, T-023r-4-6, T-023r-4-4, T-023r-8-1 |
| I-023-16 | Every WebAuthn ceremony input is main-resolved and server-issued. The preload bridge's `webAuthn` methods take **no** ceremony parameter, so the untrusted renderer supplies neither the challenge, the `rpId`, the origin, nor the transaction id; main obtains them from the control plane over its own channel and validates them against the **paired control-plane origin** — pinned at install-to-control-plane pairing and held in main-process configuration, never a build constant and never renderer-supplied (corrected at Codex round 5 so a self-hosted deployment is not refused by its own `rpId`) — before any native binding is invoked, refusing on mismatch; the sign-in fetch is deliberately uncredentialed, since `Spec-018 §Required Behavior` makes those operations pre-authentication. The reply is a `WebAuthnCeremonyOutcome` and never an assertion, a PRF output, a derived key, or a handle to one (`Spec-023 §Preload Bridge Contract`, `Spec-023 §Trust Stance`, `Spec-023 §WebAuthn Credential Flow`). | T-023r-2-5, T-023r-4-3, T-023r-8-1 |
| I-023-17 | PRF material is provisional until the relying party admits it. A leg holding an assertion and its PRF output derives nothing, caches nothing, and unwraps nothing until the control plane's verification reply returns a positive verdict; on any refusal arm — unknown credential, bad signature, RP or origin mismatch, UV mismatch, counter regression — it zeroizes the provisional bytes first. Deriving before verifying produces key material from an assertion the relying party then rejects, and a wrong wrapping key is indistinguishable from a right one until something later fails to open (`Spec-023 §WebAuthn Credential Flow` steps 3–5; `Spec-018 §Required Behavior`). | T-023r-4-3, T-023r-4-5, T-023r-4-6 |
| I-023-18 | A ceremony holds one in-flight slot per binding and a participant cancellation is terminal. The slot is taken at dispatch and released only when the native call settles, the timeout arm included — never on the caller's cancellation — so a cancel-then-retry takes a typed busy refusal instead of putting a second call on a device handle the first still owns. A cancellation ends the whole ceremony: it tries no second provider arm, opens no DAG loopback, and is carried on `WebAuthnCeremonyOutcome`'s refused arm rather than mapped into a probe result, because the participant answered the question and the answer was no (`Spec-023 §WebAuthn Platform-Authenticator Native Module`, `Spec-023 §WebAuthn Credential Flow`). | T-023r-4-3, T-023r-4-5, T-023r-4-6 |
| I-023-19 | Envelopes are wrapped under the installation's custody root key, never under a credential's KEK, and the root is minted on the first **verified ceremony of either kind** that finds none — sign-in included, because a fresh install using an already-enrolled synced credential never enrols locally and an enrolment-only trigger would leave it rootless. The refresh-token envelope and the long-term identity-key envelope are wrapped once, at the moment the root is minted; every wrapping key the desktop derives — each enrolled credential's PRF-derived KEK and the §Fallback Behavior passphrase-derived one — wraps only a **copy** of that root, keyed by credential id in the OS keystore, so enrolling an authenticator writes one copy and removing one deletes one copy, and neither touches an envelope. Wrapping directly under a credential's KEK makes a second enrolled authenticator open nothing, and the re-wrap that would repair it needs a live ceremony on the credential the participant is enrolling a backup against (`Spec-023 §WebAuthn Platform-Authenticator Native Module`, `Spec-023 §Shell ↔ OS Keystore`). | T-023r-4-7, T-023r-4-8 |
| I-023-20 | One DPoP proof key per installation, generated on first use, persisted outside the custody root, and reused. The ceremony's verification procedure issues a sender-bound token pair, so the request must carry a proof of key possession; main generates that key pair on first use and keeps it as its own OS-keystore entry under the [ADR-021](../decisions/021-cli-identity-key-storage-custody.md) ladder rather than wrapped under the custody root, because the proof is signed before the reply that would let a root be minted. The proof is the token-request form — over the request's method and URI, carrying no `ath`, there being no access token yet to hash ([RFC 9449 §5](https://datatracker.ietf.org/doc/html/rfc9449#section-5)) — and the same key is reused on every later request the issued pair authenticates, since the pair's `cnf.jkt` is that key's thumbprint and a regenerated key would bind a pair the shell cannot use (`Spec-023 §WebAuthn Credential Flow`, `Spec-023 §Shell ↔ OS Keystore`; Plan-018 I-018-20). | T-023r-4-3 |
| I-023-10 | The pending-invite reference is opaque, single-use, and TTL-bounded, and resolves to the confined token only inside the main process: it is minted in main, carries no token-derived material, and is consumed when acceptance is issued (not when confirmation is requested, so an authentication detour preserves it) or released on dismissal — a reference that is unknown, already consumed, or expired is refused rather than re-resolved (`Spec-023 §Deep-Link Invite Flow` step 8, property (a)). | T-023r-2-5, T-023r-5-5, T-023r-6-3, T-023r-8-1, T-023r-8-5 |

## Cross-Plan Obligations

Plan-023 owns the `apps/desktop/src/{main,preload,renderer}/` **namespaces**, but several files inside them carry other plans' **content** — the per-file content exceptions recorded in the dependency map's §2. The rule this section encodes: Plan-023's Tier-8 work composes, imports, and registers that content; it never rewrites a body it does not own. Added 2026-08-10 by the Tier-8 remainder readiness audit (NS-20, C-023-4), which the dependency map's §3 Plan-023 row names as the owner of the CP-023-4 return-cite.

- **CP-023-1 — Tier-8 `main/index.ts` composes, never replaces, the Plan-001 will-quit drain.** `apps/desktop/src/main/sidecar-lifecycle.ts` is Plan-001 content sitting inside a Plan-023-owned tree. When Tier-8 work grows `main/index.ts` into the full startup sequence (Sentry-first init, protocol registration, daemon supervisor, deep-link handler), it wires the existing drain into that sequence rather than re-authoring or displacing it; a Tier-8 diff that removes the drain wiring is a review rejection. Reciprocal of CP-001-1. **Direction:** Consume from [Plan-001](./001-shared-session-core.md). **Tasks:** T-023r-3-4.
- **CP-023-2 — the Tier-8 daemon supervisor publishes `daemon.process.*` into the Plan-007 R3 status projector.** `daemon-supervisor.ts` owns the producer half of the process-lifecycle signal (start / ready / crash / restart / persistent-error); Plan-007 R3 owns the consumer projection and the status pill. Until this task ships, R3's pill surfaces transport state only — the documented runtime residual, not a build-order cycle. Reciprocal of CP-007-11. **Direction:** Provide to [Plan-007](./007-local-ipc-and-daemon-control.md). **Tasks:** T-023r-3-1.
- **CP-023-3 — the Tier-8 `bridge/index.ts` registry imports and registers the Plan-007-owned `bridge/daemon-status.ts`.** That file is Plan-007 R3 content landing at Tier 4 as the `bridge/` tree's first occupant; Plan-023's registry registers it on the `daemon.subscribe` channel and never rewrites its body — the same shape as `main/sidecar-lifecycle.ts` under CP-023-1. Together with CP-023-2 this is the closing edge on R3's runtime gap: R3's renderer `daemon.subscribe('daemon.status', …)` path has no live backend until both this registry and the non-stub preload ship (the Tier-1 preload throws `NotImplementedAtTier1Error`). Reciprocal of CP-007-12. **Direction:** Consume from [Plan-007](./007-local-ipc-and-daemon-control.md). **Tasks:** T-023r-2-1.
- **CP-023-4 — the Tier-8 composer drives `activity.typing` exclusively through the daemon presence surface.** `apps/desktop/src/renderer/src/shell/MessageComposer.tsx` issues set / refresh / clear against the daemon presence handler surface Plan-002 T3.5 ships; the renderer never injects Awareness state directly, and it never publishes a typing indicator for a membership-restricted channel — the sender-side suppression is applied daemon-side before anything leaves the machine, and the composer must not route around it. The scalar-vs-concurrent-runs limitation Plan-002 records affects the **agent** producer leg only; the human composer scalar is one per publishing client. Reciprocal of the Plan-002 T3.5 human producer leg, whose §3 row assigns this return-cite to the Tier-8 readiness audit. **Direction:** Consume from [Plan-002](./002-invite-membership-and-presence.md). **Tasks:** T-023r-6-2.
- **CP-023-5 — the Tier-8 deep-link confirmation consumes Plan-002's `invite.preview` verbatim.** The pre-acceptance metadata resolution issues Plan-002's non-consuming `invite.preview` (`Spec-002 §Interfaces And Contracts`, built by Plan-002 T2.6 / T2.7) exactly as that plan defines it: this plan never re-implements the preview's validation or its refusal ordering, never decodes the `v4.local` token locally (it holds no key — the opacity pin in `Spec-023 §Deep-Link Invite Flow` is what forces a server-side preview at all), and never substitutes the consuming `acceptInvite` to populate the confirmation. **Two distinct consumers, one procedure — not a duplicate leg.** The daemon-side invite leg [CP-002-9](./002-invite-membership-and-presence.md#cross-plan-obligations) routes to [Plan-008](./008-control-plane-relay-and-session-join.md) Tier 5 (the `invite.*` daemon IPC handlers, the live control-plane caller, and `membershipClient.preview`) serves the renderer's in-app invite-accept view, one of that Tier-5 bullet's own named client surfaces; the leg registered here is the **main-confined deep-link path**, which resolves the preview over the same main-process control-plane client `bridge/control-plane.ts` uses for the step-5 `acceptInvite` exchange. The preview does not choose a transport — it inherits the accept's, because a preview and the accept it precedes validating the same token over different channels would be incoherent. That the channel is main-to-control-plane rather than the daemon gateway is `Spec-023 §Deep-Link Invite Flow`'s own choice: step 2 holds the deep-link token "in main-process memory only" and step 5 has the main process call the control-plane procedure directly. **The claim is scoped to this path's token**, not to invite tokens generally — the renderer's invite-accept view supplies a different token on the daemon leg, which is why CP-002-9 exists and is not a counterexample to this one. Neither transport is the other's, and both consume the one Plan-002 procedure — the same router-vs-handoff disambiguation the dependency map's §3 draws on the Plan-002 ↔ Plan-008 edge. No reciprocal id is minted upstream: Plan-002 T2.6 / T2.7 already produce the procedure and Plan-008's Tier-5 scope bullet already owns the daemon leg, so this obligation records a consumption, not new upstream work. **Direction:** Consume from [Plan-002](./002-invite-membership-and-presence.md). **Tasks:** T-023r-5-5, T-023r-6-3.

### CP-023-6 — Sidekick definition editor mount (reciprocal of [Plan-030](./030-sidekick-definitions-and-peer-invocation.md) CP-030-2)

Plan-030 authors the sidekick definition editor and the peer-invocation enablement control as components in its **own** `apps/desktop/src/renderer/src/sidekick-definitions/` subtree; this plan mounts that subtree into the renderer shell and router at Phase 6 and owns nothing inside it. The split follows the NS-73 precedent exactly — Plan-004 authored `run-controls/` and Plan-013 owned the mount — and keeps the editor's eligibility and refusal logic with the plan that owns the daemon contract it projects, rather than duplicating that logic in a shell that would then be a second source of truth.

**Resolution.** Plan-030 Phase 5 is gated on this plan's Phase 6 merged, so the mount point exists before the components that fill it. The reciprocal is registered at Plan-030 CP-030-2 in the 2026-08-26 swap that mints that plan. This plan gains no task: the mount is a route-table plus shell-slot registration of the shape Phase 6 already performs for every other plan-owned subtree.

### CP-023-7 — MCP-governance settings page mount (reciprocal of [Plan-028](./028-mcp-server-configuration-and-governance.md) CP-028-8)

Plan-028 authors the MCP Servers operator page as components in its **own** `apps/desktop/src/renderer/src/mcp-governance/` subtree (its T28.5.7); this plan mounts that subtree into the renderer shell's settings surface and router at Phase 6 and owns nothing inside it. The split is the CP-023-6 shape unchanged, which is itself the NS-73 precedent: the owner authors the components and their eligibility projection, the host provides the mount, and neither edits the other's subtree. The reason it matters more here than as tidiness is that the MCP Servers View sketch under `Spec-023 §Signature Feature Composition Sketches` requires the page to derive no trust state and no effective idempotency class of its own — an eligibility projection authored in the shell, away from the plan that owns the governance rules, is exactly how a second source of truth for those decisions gets written.

**Resolution.** Registered 2026-08-29 with the reciprocal at Plan-028 CP-028-8 in the same swap. This plan gains no task: the mount is a route-table plus settings-slot registration of the shape Phase 6 already performs for every other plan-owned subtree, and Plan-028's own dispatch order is unchanged.

### CP-023-8 — Provider command-and-skill enumeration consumed from [Plan-005](./005-provider-driver-contract-and-capabilities.md)

The composer's command-and-skill autocomplete calls the client-facing `driver.listProviderCommands` and reads the `provider_commands` and `context_compaction` declarations through the existing `driver.listCapabilities` cache; Plan-005 owns all three and this plan authors none of them. What this plan owns is the **binding key**: the composer issues the read for the **target agent** and offers the result only in a composer addressed to that agent, re-reading rather than filtering when the target changes. Since the round-2 fold (2026-08-29) the wire shape carries that key directly — the verb takes `{ sessionId, agentId }` and the daemon resolves the agent's live provider binding — so the composer names the thing it is addressed to and never handles a binding identifier, which no client-facing read publishes. An agent with no live binding is answered `driver.unavailable` and the affordance simply has nothing to offer, this being a live read rather than a catalog that outlives a session. That is `Spec-005 §The provider command and skill surface`'s routing invariant landing on the one surface that could violate it — a composer that cached one enumeration and reused it across agents would offer a Claude command to a Codex agent without any daemon call being wrong.

**Resolution.** Registered 2026-08-29. Plan-005 Phase 4 registers the verb; this plan's Phase 6 composer consumes it. No reciprocal task is owed on Plan-005 beyond the registration it already carries, so this row is one-directional by design.

### CP-023-10 — WebAuthn ceremony relying-party half from [Plan-018](./018-identity-and-participant-state.md) (reciprocal of CP-018-14)

[ADR-010](../decisions/010-paseto-webauthn-mls-auth.md) has made WebAuthn the desktop's primary credential path since acceptance, and `Spec-023 §Main Process Responsibilities` makes this plan's main process the party that runs the ceremony and submits the result for verification. **No plan owned the other side.** A repo-wide search at the Codex round-4 audit found no control-plane route, no task, no credential store, and no cross-plan obligation anywhere in the corpus that issues a WebAuthn challenge or verifies a response — Plan-018's dependency supplied tokens only — so `register()` and `signIn()` reached a native binding with no server producer to call, and the three surfaces this plan depends on had no source: the server-issued options T-023r-4-3 validates against the **pairing-pinned** control-plane origin ("build-pinned relying party" until Codex round 5), the verifier the Linux leg's attestation payload is asserted against, and the **server-held user-verification mode** guard 4 compares its response bit with.

**Provider: Plan-018 Phase 6 (T6.1–T6.5)** — `webauthn_credentials` + `webauthn_challenges`, the **five** WebAuthn routes on the Plan-008 tRPC `sessionRouter` skeleton (four ceremony operations plus the authenticated `WebAuthnCredentialRevoke`, added at Codex round 7 so a removed authenticator stops being able to complete the unauthenticated verify), and the single-use challenge fence. Registered 2026-09-01 (Codex round 4); Plan-018 registers CP-018-14 and flips in the same swap.

**The dependency is gated mechanically, not by prose.** Phase 4's precondition block gains `{ type: external_plan_phase_merged, plan: 018, phase: 6 }`, on the Plan-026 Phase-7 precedent — a born-unchecked box would have held the phase on a human marker, and this is a phase-to-phase build-order fact the gate can resolve. The over-capture is accepted for the same reason the Plan-004 R3 entry accepts it: Gate 5 resolves per phase and never per task, and prose-only scoping is invisible to it. It holds T-023r-4-1 and T-023r-4-2 (the keystore pair) alongside the ceremony tasks, which is harmless — the keystore stores what the ceremony produces.

**Resolution.** Reciprocity present in the same diff. No Plan-008 task is added: the tRPC `sessionRouter` host is a shipped Tier-1 deliverable and Plan-018 mounts procedures on it, so no build-order edge is created beyond the Plan-018 → Plan-023 one this row states.

### CP-023-9 — Main-process PRF key derivation for [Plan-022](./022-data-retention-and-gdpr.md)'s Tier-8 KEK branch (reciprocal of CP-022-3)

`Plan-022 §Cross-Plan Obligations` CP-022-3 has owed, since it was written, "a `deriveKeyMaterial`/PRF surface on Plan-023" as the producer for the forward-declared Tier-8 branch of its daemon-master-key KEK ladder. The Codex round-1 fold retired `webAuthn.deriveKeyMaterial(input)` from the preload bridge — correctly, since it let the untrusted renderer choose the PRF salt and handed derived key material back across the bridge (I-023-16) — and that left CP-022-3 naming a producer the corpus no longer has. Registered 2026-09-01 (Codex round 3) to close the gap rather than leave a shipped obligation pointing at a deleted method.

**The replacement is main-owned and does not move.** `apps/desktop/src/main/webauthn/index.ts` exports `PrfKeyDerivationService`, a single-method port (`deriveKek`) that runs the ceremony through the resolved per-platform binding and returns the derived bytes **to its main-process caller only**. It appears in no `SidekicksBridge` type and is reachable from no bridge namespace.

**The transport is named, because a port cannot cross a process (corrected 2026-09-01, Codex round 4).** The round-3 text said Plan-022's daemon-side branch would take the port "by injection", and that is not a thing that can happen: the daemon is a separate `utilityProcess`, so a TypeScript object cannot be handed to it and there is no in-process call for it to make. Injection is how **main** wires the service into its own handler; the daemon reaches it over a **typed request/response on the existing shell-to-daemon supervisor channel** — the `MessagePortMain` duplex the supervisor already owns, which `Spec-023 §Trust Stance` grades trusted-to-trusted and `Spec-023 §Utility Process Vs Child Process` establishes. The method is **`shell.deriveKek`**, registered in [api-payload-contracts.md](../architecture/contracts/api-payload-contracts.md) §Shell-Hosted Method Registry. It is the corpus's **first shell-hosted method** — every method registered before it is one the daemon answers and the shell calls — and that direction is stated rather than assumed, because JSON-RPC is symmetric but this corpus's registries had not yet used the reverse leg. The canonical `dotted-camelCase` format applies unchanged, which is why the namespace root is `shell` and not `shellKey`: the registry's own regex forbids an uppercase character in the first segment.

**Who may call it is settled by the transport, not by a check bolted on after one.** The port that carries this method is created at `utilityProcess.fork` and handed to exactly one endpoint — the supervised daemon child. It is never exposed on the preload bridge, never on a socket, and never on the control-plane client, so a request from anywhere else is not refused, it is **unreachable**; the handler additionally asserts the receiving port's identity and drops a message arriving on any other, which is a local assertion on a private channel rather than a wire refusal, so **no error code is minted**. The derived KEK crosses that one channel and no other. The distinction is the whole point of the seam: retiring the bridge method removed a renderer-reachable derivation surface, not the derivation.

**The transport has modules, not just a name (2026-09-01, Codex round 5).** Round 4 named the method and left every part that carries it unassigned, which is how a registered contract ends up with no implementation on either side. Four files own it. `packages/contracts/src/desktop/shell-rpc.ts` (CREATE, this plan) holds the request envelope and the `ShellDeriveKekRequest` / `ShellDeriveKekResponse` Zod schemas, so both processes parse the same shapes from one source. `apps/desktop/src/main/shell-rpc.ts` (CREATE, this plan) is the **main-side dispatcher**: a method table keyed by the registered name, request-id correlation with a pending map and a bounded timeout that settles an orphaned request rather than leaking it, the port-identity assertion above, and refusal of any name not in the table. `apps/desktop/src/main/daemon-supervisor.ts` (EXTEND) hands the `MessagePortMain` to that dispatcher at fork and takes it away on exit, so a dispatcher never outlives the child it answers. The **daemon-side caller** is Plan-022's, at `packages/runtime-daemon/src/crypto/shell-rpc-client.ts` (CREATE, named on that plan's T22.1.3), beside the `crypto/` sibling T22.1.4 already creates. A duplex round-trip test drives a real `MessagePort` pair end to end — request, correlation, response, and an unmatched id — rather than stubbing either half, because the correlation bug this transport can have is invisible to a test that mocks the channel.

**The derivation runs on the shell side, so no authenticator-derived secret crosses the RPC (2026-09-01, Codex round 5; the inputs narrowed at round 6).** The request carries **one** input, Plan-022's HKDF salt. Round 5 had it carry a second — the credential's persisted PRF evaluation input — and round 6 removes it: that input is a contract-fixed constant the shell already holds (Plan-018 I-018-18, restated), and the daemon could never have supplied a per-credential one, holding no credential row and sitting on the far side of this boundary. Main runs the ceremony, unwraps the installation's **custody root key** (T-023r-4-8, I-023-19) with the ceremony-derived wrapping key, feeds **the root** into HKDF-SHA-256 under the caller's salt and the contract-fixed info string `"ai-sidekicks/daemon-master/v1"`, and the response carries the finished **32-byte KEK**. Two properties fall out of that placement, and both are load-bearing. Returning raw PRF output for the daemon to transform would send the authenticator's secret across a process boundary to be used by the party that does not own it — the same shape, one layer down, that retiring the bridge method removed. And deriving from the root rather than from PRF output directly makes this KEK **stable across enrolments**: a key taken straight from one credential's PRF bytes changes the moment a second authenticator is enrolled, and the daemon cannot re-wrap what it can no longer decrypt. The info string is fixed by the contract rather than chosen by the caller, because a caller-chosen info is one more axis on which two honest implementations derive different keys from one authenticator, and Plan-022's own §Notes records that its "HKDF removed entirely" claim is narrowed rather than kept.

**Resolution.** The edge stays forward-declared and explicitly not a V1 blocker, but it is **no longer macOS-scoped (2026-09-01, Codex round 7).** CP-022-3 was written when this plan claimed a macOS-only PRF posture; that posture is withdrawn, and the same main-owned derivation now runs on all three platforms behind the per-host capability probe (I-023-15). The branch is therefore selected by **resolved host capability, never by platform name**: a host whose probe finds a usable authenticator with PRF takes this edge, and a host whose probe does not — no authenticator, no PRF on the arm it has, or a binding that would not load — takes Plan-022's Argon2id passphrase branch. `process.platform` selects a binding module and settles nothing about which KEK branch a host ends on. **Producer: T-023r-4-7**, which already owns this plan's key-custody surface, and which additionally registers the `shell.deriveKek` handler and wires the injected `PrfKeyDerivationService` behind it. **Consumer: Plan-022 T22.1.3**, its Argon2id-KEK task, whose PRF branch issues the request instead of calling a function it cannot reach. Plan-022's CP-022-3 row, its Tier-2 ladder pointer, its §Reciprocal-obligations bullet (2), and T22.1.3's `Consumes:` line are all amended in the same diff; because those are contract rows rather than mirror prose, Plan-022 takes an in-swap flip-and-restore. `Spec-022` **does** take an edit in this same diff, unlike at round 3: its ladder framing is provenance-based and needed nothing, but `§Daemon Master Key`'s desktop branch stated the derivation itself — over raw PRF output, through the renderer's `CredentialsContainer`, under a since-superseded W3C Candidate Recommendation — and all three are false against this plan as it now stands, so that branch is corrected with the scope and Spec-022 flips and restores with Plan-022.

## Implementation Steps

1. **Scaffold `apps/desktop/` workspace package.** Create `package.json` with the dependency set declared in §Target Areas. Add to the root workspace `package.json`'s `workspaces` array. Configure TypeScript strict (`"strict": true`, `"noUncheckedIndexedAccess": true`, `"exactOptionalPropertyTypes": true`).
2. **Author the preload-bridge type.** `packages/contracts/src/desktop-bridge.ts` — full `SidekicksBridge` interface per Spec-023. Re-export Plan-007 daemon types + Plan-008 control-plane types. Type-only file, no runtime. Author the conditional-type negative test (`Expect<Not<SidekicksBridge[any] includes 'token' | 'dpop' | 'prf'>>`) in `desktop-bridge.test-d.ts`.
3. **Author `electron.vite.config.ts`.** `electron-vite` v5.0.0 config with three entries (main / preload / renderer). Per-target module-system shape: main emits ESM (`build.rollupOptions.output.format: 'es'`); preload emits CJS via `build.rollupOptions.output.format: 'cjs'` + an explicit `.cjs` output extension (Electron's sandboxed preload runtime is CJS-only — an ESM preload fails to register with `SyntaxError: Cannot use import statement outside a module`, and the `.cjs` extension overrides the package-level `"type": "module"` so Node loads the file as CJS regardless of the package field); renderer emits browser-ESM. Renderer loads via custom protocol, not `file://`. Enable `build.sourcemap: 'hidden'` (sourcemap emitted but not referenced from the bundle, so they are available for Sentry upload but not shipped to end users). The `apps/desktop` `build` script uses `electron-vite build` (not `tsc -b`); this is what enables both the per-target output shape above AND the smoke-mode `define` substitution described next. **Smoke-mode gate:** declare a compile-time-static identifier `__SIDEKICKS_SMOKE_BUILD__` via Vite's `define` plugin — substituted with the literal `false` in default `electron-vite build` and with the literal `true` in `electron-vite build --mode=smoke`. Test-only probe code paths gate on this identifier in an outer `if (__SIDEKICKS_SMOKE_BUILD__ && <runtime-opt-in>)`; Rollup's tree-shaker collapses the `if (false && expr)` branch in release bundles, physically eliminating the probe body from the shipped artifact (the "test machinery never embedded in production binaries" property — see `Spec-023 §Pitfalls To Avoid`). The inner runtime opt-in remains as defense-in-depth so the probe never auto-runs even in a smoke bundle without explicit invocation.
4. **Author the main-window factory.** `apps/desktop/src/main/window.ts` — `createMainWindow()` returns a `BrowserWindow` with the `webPreferences` block from `Spec-023 §Security Hardening Baseline`. Write a build-time assertion (`apps/desktop/build/assert-webprefs.ts`) that greps the factory's source and fails the build if any of the locked-in values are changed.
5. **Author the preload bridge.** `apps/desktop/src/preload/index.ts` — single `contextBridge.exposeInMainWorld('sidekicks', bridge)` call. Each bridge method delegates to `ipcRenderer.invoke('<channel>', ...)`; no direct Node API use. The bridge object must be structurally typed as the `SidekicksBridge` type from step 2.
6. **Wire the IPC handlers.** `apps/desktop/src/main/bridge/index.ts` + the seven per-surface bridge files (`daemon.ts`, `control-plane.ts`, `native.ts`, `webauthn.ts`, `update.ts`, `onboarding.ts`, `invite.ts` — re-derived from §Target Areas Main process by direct count; this step read "five" from PR #6 until 2026-08-25, predating both the `onboarding` and `invite` namespaces). One `ipcMain.handle('<channel>', handler)` per method. Each handler validates input against the Spec-023 contract; auth material is attached server-side; responses are sanitized before return.
7. **Author the daemon supervisor.** `apps/desktop/src/main/daemon-supervisor.ts` — `utilityProcess.fork(daemonEntryPath, args, { serviceName: 'ai-sidekicks-daemon' })` invoked **after** `app.whenReady()` (per `Spec-023 §Implementation Notes` §Utility Process Vs Child Process). Exit-reason switch: `"memory-eviction"` → restart without incrementing failure counter; any other non-zero exit → increment counter, back off per the Spec-023 ladder (`100ms, 300ms, 1s, 3s, 10s`), surface persistent error after 5 attempts. Version-negotiation gate: on `DaemonHello` check `protocol_version` against the shell's pinned minimum; on mismatch, block mutating bridge calls and allow only read-only subscriptions.
8. **Author the keystore abstraction.** `apps/desktop/src/main/keystore.ts` — exports `getSecret`/`setSecret`/`deleteSecret`. `@napi-rs/keyring` is the primary path; `safeStorage` is a fallback for platforms where `@napi-rs/keyring` is unavailable. On startup: `safeStorage.isEncryptionAvailable()` must return `true`; on Linux, `safeStorage.getSelectedStorageBackend()` must return `gnome_libsecret` or `kwallet[456]` — `basic_text` and `unknown` are refused. On refusal: emit `keystoreUnavailable` event via the bridge; `setSecret` subsequently throws `KeystoreUnavailableError`; Plan-026 consumes this event to refuse long-lived token persistence and degrade to memory-only session.
9. **Author the WebAuthn strategy dispatcher and its three platform bindings.** `apps/desktop/src/main/webauthn/index.ts` selects the binding module from `process.platform` — `darwin` → `mac.ts` (the own-built `native/webauthn-darwin/` addon), `win32` → `windows.ts` (`webauthn.dll` through `koffi`), `linux` → `linux.ts` (`libfido2` through `koffi`) — and each module is dynamically imported behind its platform guard so no host loads a binding it cannot use. PRF derivation is implemented on all three and stays in the main process, exported as the injected `PrfKeyDerivationService` port (CP-023-9) rather than as a bridge method. Whether **this host** can serve a ceremony is a capability probe run at ceremony time, never inferred from the platform name (I-023-15). **Rewritten 2026-09-01 (Codex round 3):** this step still ended by throwing `WebAuthnPrfUnsupportedError` and reauthenticating on the refresh token alone, which contradicts both the typed outcome the round-1 fold introduced and the custody leg the round-1 fold added. A negative probe, an authenticator that answers without PRF output, a UV flag that disagrees with the recorded mode, or a binding that will not load all resolve the **`fallback-required` arm of `WebAuthnCeremonyOutcome`** — a value, not a throw — and the handler then runs `fallback-dag.ts` for authentication and `kek-fallback.ts` (T-023r-4-7) for custody, in that order, because the Device Authorization Grant reauthenticates the participant and cannot reproduce the key-encryption key that wraps this host's long-term Ed25519 identity key. Refresh-token-only reauthentication is retired here: it left the host signed in and unable to unwrap its own identity key.
10. **Author the DAG fallback.** `apps/desktop/src/main/webauthn/fallback-dag.ts`. `http.createServer()` bound to `'127.0.0.1'` (explicitly NOT `'localhost'`) with port `0` for an OS-assigned ephemeral port; `server.listen(0, '127.0.0.1', () => { const port = server.address().port; open(<authorize-url>?redirect_uri=http://127.0.0.1:${port}/callback&code_challenge=...&code_challenge_method=S256) })`. One-shot callback acceptance; `AbortSignal.timeout(300_000)` aborts the server on 5-min timeout. PKCE via `oauth4webapi` v3.8.5 `randomPKCECodeVerifier()` + `calculatePKCECodeChallenge()`; `plain` method is rejected at authorize-URL construction time per [RFC 7636 §4](https://datatracker.ietf.org/doc/html/rfc7636). Binding to `127.0.0.1` (not `localhost`) avoids the IPv6 `::1` DNS-ambiguity pitfall surfaced in the Apr-2026 research pass and is what RFC 8252 §7.3 normatively scopes.
11. **Author the auto-update state machine.** `apps/desktop/src/main/updater.ts`. `autoUpdater` (from `electron-updater`) on a 4-hour cadence + startup + explicit check. At check time: fetch the platform's `latest-*.yml` + sibling `manifest.sig` (Step 16 produces the signed pair) and verify the Ed25519 signature over the exact manifest bytes against the `updater-public-key.ts` embedded key — an unverifiable manifest is discarded and logged and the cycle skipped. `on('update-downloaded')` handler: match the downloaded artifact's hash against the authenticated manifest's per-file artifact-hash entry (`sha512` in the `electron-updater` v6.8.9 manifest schema); on mismatch → discard artifact, log, refuse; if both legs hold → stage artifact at `apps/desktop/<staging-dir>` + retain the prior version **before** apply: copy the running version's rollback source — per platform, the previously-downloaded artifact for the running version from the shell-owned update-artifact cache when present, else the installed application itself (macOS `.app` bundle; Windows installed directory; Linux `AppImage`) — into `<app-user-data>/retained-versions/` (bounded to one prior version) and write its version, retained-copy path, integrity hash to `updater-state.json`. Retention failure refuses the update fail-closed — apply aborted, installed version keeps running, refusal logged and re-surfaced — because an in-place apply overwrites what the live executable path points to, so recording that path retains nothing. On `app.relaunch() + app.quit()` flow: on post-relaunch, wait for `DaemonHello`; on timeout (10s) or version-mismatch, trigger rollback — verify the retained copy against its recorded integrity hash and re-install from it, never from the live executable path, then re-launch. A confirmed-healthy handshake deletes the retained copy and completes the update; the rollback path never deletes it. Rollback is a one-way ratchet: the version that failed is marked `rollback_blacklist = [version]` in `updater-state.json` and will not be re-attempted automatically.
12. **Author the crash reporter.** `apps/desktop/src/main/crash-reporter.ts` — called **first** in `main/index.ts` before any other module loads. Initializes `@sentry/electron/main` v7.11.0 with `beforeSend` scrubber. Enable `SentryMinidump` integration (default; captures Crashpad minidumps with Sentry breadcrumbs/context per [Sentry Electron docs](https://docs.sentry.io/platforms/javascript/guides/electron/configuration/integrations/electronminidump/)). Renderer + utility-process SDKs are initialized inside those processes respectively — **the v7 SDK does not auto-initialize renderers**; explicit `init()` is required per the Apr-2026 research pass.
13. **Author the deep-link handler.** `apps/desktop/src/main/deep-link.ts`. `app.setAsDefaultProtocolClient('sidekicks')` at startup. `open-url` (macOS) and `second-instance` (Windows / Linux) handlers parse the URL and confine the token to main, then run `Spec-023 §Deep-Link Invite Flow` in order: resolve display metadata through the non-consuming `invite.preview` read; emit the pending-invite bridge event carrying that metadata plus an opaque single-use reference; and call `controlPlane.call('acceptInvite', { token })` only when the renderer confirms through that reference. Publish the main-process `session:joined` signal on success as the `joined` arm of the membership-outcome bridge event `invite.subscribeOutcome` carries (its emitter is `bridge/invite.ts`); a typed preview refusal emits a refusal-shaped prompt carrying no reference, and the raw token is never transmitted through `window.webContents.send` on any path.
14. **Author the renderer shell.** `apps/desktop/src/renderer/src/App.tsx` (top-level router + layout) plus the `apps/desktop/src/renderer/src/shell/` subtree — layout chrome and `MessageComposer.tsx`. This step authors **no feature-view body**: each Signature Feature view is created by its owning plan in that plan's own `apps/desktop/src/renderer/src/` subtree per the dependency map's §2, and this step only mounts it as a route and holds the composition points. The composer drives `activity.typing` set / refresh / clear through the daemon presence handler surface Plan-002 T3.5 ships, never by direct Awareness injection (CP-023-4).
15. **Author the build pipeline.** `apps/desktop/build/fuses.ts` + `asar-digest.ts` + `code-sign.ts`. Pipeline order: `electron-builder` packages → `fuses.ts` flips fuses → `asar-digest.ts` embeds the digest → `code-sign.ts` signs (per-platform). Fuse-flipping + digest-embedding **must** precede signing; both invalidate any prior signature.
16. **Author the update-feed hosting stubs.** `apps/desktop/release/manifest-sign.ts` — signs `latest-*.yml` files with an Ed25519 private key held by the release-engineering role (out-of-band key). Outputs `manifest.sig` alongside each `latest-*.yml`. The signing key is distinct from code-signing certs (per `Spec-023 §Pitfalls To Avoid`).
17. **Author the renderer ESLint config.** `apps/desktop/eslint.config.mjs` — `no-restricted-imports` rule banning `electron` / `node:*` / `fs` / `child_process` / `net` / `os` / `path` / `process` / `keytar` / `@napi-rs/keyring` / `./src/main/**` / `./src/preload/**` from `apps/desktop/src/renderer/src/**`. CI runs this lint; any renderer-side Node import fails the build.
18. **Author the Playwright `_electron` E2E suite.** `apps/desktop/test/e2e/*.spec.ts` per §Target Areas Test section. Playwright v1.58.x is the current line as of April 2026; the `_electron` namespace is still experimental per [Playwright class-electron docs](https://playwright.dev/docs/api/class-electron), but it is the only production-viable harness (Spectron archived in 2022 with no revival). Tests must tolerate minor API churn between Playwright releases.
19. **Author the CI gate scripts.** `.github/workflows/desktop-build.yml` (or project-native CI format) — on every PR touching `apps/desktop/**` or `packages/contracts/src/desktop-bridge.ts`: run `pnpm lint` (ESLint) + `pnpm typecheck` (TS strict) + `turbo run test:renderer test:main-unit test:smoke --filter=@ai-sidekicks/desktop` (the renderer + smoke split per the §Test Plan §Testing posture sub-section landed by BL-118, plus the `main-unit` project T-023p-1B-3 registers; the Turbo task DAG handles `build:smoke` ordering + content-hash skip) + `pnpm test:e2e` (Playwright `_electron` E2E suite landed by step 18) + `pnpm build:fuses --verify` (asserts the packaged-binary fuse wire matches the declared posture). A per-platform release job signs + notarizes on tag pushes.
20. **Document release runbook.** `apps/desktop/RELEASE.md` — per-platform signing prerequisites (Developer ID cert on macOS; Artifact Signing account on Windows OR EV cert + HSM token; GPG key on Linux), notarization retry procedure, rollback procedure, update-signing-key rotation procedure.

## Parallelization Notes

- Step 1 (scaffold) + step 2 (bridge type) must land first; everything else orbits them.
- Steps 3 (vite config) + 4 (window factory) + 5 (preload) can run in parallel; they have no cross-file dependencies.
- Steps 6 (IPC handlers) + 7 (daemon supervisor) + 8 (keystore) + 9 (webauthn dispatch) can run in parallel after steps 1–5.
- Step 10 (DAG fallback) depends on step 8 (keystore) for token persistence.
- Step 11 (auto-update) depends on step 7 (daemon supervisor) for the post-apply handshake, and on step 8 (keystore) for the update-signing-public-key embedding.
- Step 12 (crash reporter) has no dependency and should be authored early so it runs first at startup.
- Step 13 (deep-link) depends on step 6 (IPC handlers) for the membership-outcome bridge emission — `invite.subscribeOutcome`, which publishes the main-process `session:joined` signal.
- Step 14 (renderer shell) depends on step 2 (bridge type) — can start in parallel with steps 6–13 since it consumes the bridge-shape, not the backing implementations. Its composer leg additionally depends on Plan-002 T3.5's daemon presence update path (the `**(Tier 8 remainder only.)**` Plan-002 box), and the router's parameterized subscriptions on the Plan-007 / Plan-008 request-param channel box.
- Step 15 (build pipeline) depends on everything compiling; orchestrates tail-end of release flow.
- Steps 17 (eslint) + 18 (E2E) + 19 (CI) + 20 (runbook) are independent tail-end tasks.

## Tier 1 Partial PR Sequence

The Tier 1 partial slice lands as **1 small PR** following the Plan-008 bootstrap precedent (one cohesive substrate slice). The carve-out exists to unblock Plan-001 Phase 5 (`apps/desktop/src/main/sidecar-lifecycle.ts` + `apps/desktop/src/renderer/src/session-bootstrap/`) and Plan-024 Phase 3 (I-024-4 will-quit drain integration test) — both depend on the `apps/desktop/` directory tree existing per [§Cross-Plan Dependencies §5 Plan-023 Substrate-vs-Namespace Carve-Out](../architecture/cross-plan-dependencies.md#plan-023-substrate-vs-namespace-carve-out-tier-1--tier-8). Resolves [BL-101](../archive/backlog-archive.md#bl-101-c-3--plan-023-tier-1-partial-substrate-carve-out-mirrors-plan-007-partial--plan-008-bootstrap) (option (a)). Tier 8 remainder retains the full Implementation Steps 1–20 surface that depends on Plan-007 / Plan-018 / Plan-008 / Plan-024.

### Phase 1: Workspace Package Substrate (apps/desktop/ tree + minimal entrypoints)

**Goal:** `apps/desktop/` workspace package compiles; minimal Electron app launches end-to-end. Directory tree (`apps/desktop/src/main/`, `apps/desktop/src/preload/`, `apps/desktop/src/renderer/src/`) exists; `electron.vite.config.ts` builds all three targets; preload exposes a stub `window.sidekicks` typed against the `SidekicksBridge` interface from `packages/contracts/src/desktop-bridge.ts`; renderer mounts a placeholder React tree. No daemon supervision, no auto-update, no WebAuthn, no keystore, no Sentry, no deep-link — those stay at Tier 8 remainder.

**Spec-023 AC coverage.** Phase 1 ships no Tier-8 _behavior_ — no daemon supervision, auto-update, WebAuthn, keystore, crash reporting, or deep-link — and in exactly that behavioral sense the substrate is pre-behavior plumbing, which is what the phase's `audit_status: substrate_exempt` declaration asserts. It does, however, land three durable enforcement surfaces that partially close `Spec-023 §Acceptance Criteria` bullets: the build-time `webPreferences` assertion (`Spec-023 §Security Hardening Baseline`, shipped as `apps/desktop/build/assert-webprefs.ts`), the conditional-type negative contract test for the no-auth-material bullet (`packages/contracts/src/desktop-bridge.test-d.ts`), and the launch smoke test's renderer-isolation assertions (`window.require` / `process` / `global` all `undefined`). The exemption remains valid — its `#### Tasks` block cites no bracketed `Spec coverage:`, `carve_out_ref` resolves inside §5 of the dependency map, and the tree is single-owned and load-bearing for Plan-001 Phase 5 and Plan-024 Phase 3 — but the exemption is about **behavioral** AC coverage, not about shipping zero verification. Corrected 2026-08-10 by the Tier-8 remainder readiness audit (NS-20, M-023-1): the pre-correction sentence claimed the phase covered no acceptance criteria at all, which its own tasks and shipped files contradict. The remaining `Spec-023 §Acceptance Criteria` bullets (fuses, signing, auto-update rollback, keystore refusal, deep-link token containment) all require the Tier 8 remainder surfaces.

**Precondition:** None at the plan level. Tier 1 entry point. The placeholder `apps/desktop/src/index.ts` shipped by Plan-001 Phase 1 (PR #6) is repositioned during this PR per its forward-declaration comment ("split into `apps/desktop/src/{main,preload,renderer}/` per the electron-vite zero-config convention").

<!-- prettier-ignore -->
```yaml
preconditions:
  - { type: cross_plan_carve_out, ref: "Plan-023 Substrate-vs-Namespace Carve-Out" }
  - { type: audit_status, status: substrate_exempt, carve_out_ref: "Plan-023 Substrate-vs-Namespace Carve-Out" }
```

The `audit_status: substrate_exempt` declaration admits this phase for `/plan-execution` dispatch via the per-phase audit semantics introduced in [the readiness-audit runbook §Per-Phase Audit Semantics](../operations/plan-implementation-readiness-audit-runbook.md#per-phase-audit-semantics). Plan-023's plan-level audit checkbox was added and checked by the Tier-8 remainder audit (NS-20, 2026-08-10); before that swap this phase's `audit_status: substrate_exempt` declaration was the plan's only audit record — the box the exemption path deferred had in fact never been authored, so the swap added it rather than merely flipping it.

- `apps/desktop/package.json` — minimal dependency set: `electron@^41.1.0`, `electron-vite@^5.0.0`, `react@^19`, `react-dom@^19`, `@ai-sidekicks/contracts` (workspace), `@ai-sidekicks/client-sdk` (workspace). Heavy deps (`electron-builder`, `electron-updater`, `@napi-rs/keyring`, `@sentry/electron`, `@electron/asar`, `@electron/fuses`, `koffi`, `oauth4webapi`, `@playwright/test`) deferred to Tier 8 remainder.
- `apps/desktop/electron.vite.config.ts` — `electron-vite` v5 config with three targets (`main` / `preload` / `renderer`). Per-target module shape: main emits ESM; preload emits CJS via an explicit `.cjs` output extension (Electron's sandboxed preload runtime is CJS-only); renderer emits browser-ESM. The `apps/desktop` `build` script uses `electron-vite build` (not `tsc -b`) to support both the per-target output shape and the `__SIDEKICKS_SMOKE_BUILD__` compile-time `define` substitution (Implementation Step 3) that gates test-only probe code paths so Rollup tree-shakes them out of release bundles. Strict TypeScript; HMR on dev. (Implementation Step 3 substrate.)
- `apps/desktop/src/main/index.ts` — minimal main entrypoint: `app.requestSingleInstanceLock()` + `app.whenReady()` → `createMainWindow()`. No Sentry init, no protocol handler, no daemon supervisor, no deep-link handler at Tier 1. (Implementation Step 1 substrate fragment.)
- `apps/desktop/src/main/window.ts` — `createMainWindow()` factory returning a `BrowserWindow` with the `webPreferences` block from `Spec-023 §Security Hardening Baseline` (`contextIsolation: true`, `sandbox: true`, `nodeIntegration: false`, `webSecurity: true`, preload script, custom-protocol load — not `file://`). The build-time webprefs assertion (`apps/desktop/build/assert-webprefs.ts`) ships at Tier 1 to lock in the security posture from Step 1. (Implementation Step 4 substrate.)
- `apps/desktop/src/preload/index.ts` — single `contextBridge.exposeInMainWorld('sidekicks', bridge)` call. The `bridge` object is the typed stub from `packages/contracts/src/desktop-bridge.ts`; method bodies (`daemon.call`, `controlPlane.call`, `webAuthn.*`, etc.) stay unimplemented at Tier 1 (each method throws `NotImplementedAtTier1Error` until the corresponding Tier 8 IPC handler ships at Implementation Step 6). (Implementation Step 5 substrate fragment.)
- `apps/desktop/src/renderer/src/main.tsx` — minimal React entrypoint: `createRoot(document.getElementById('root')!).render(<App />)`. No Sentry-renderer init at Tier 1 (deferred to Tier 8 remainder). (Implementation Step 14 substrate fragment.)
- `apps/desktop/src/renderer/src/App.tsx` — placeholder `<App />` returning a minimal tree (e.g. a single `<div>AI Sidekicks Desktop (Tier 1 substrate)</div>`). The Tier-8 router, the `renderer/src/shell/` layout + composer subtree, and the mounting of each owning plan's feature view defer to the Tier 8 remainder; no `renderer/src/features/*` tree is ever created (see §Renderer). (Implementation Step 14 substrate fragment.)
- `packages/contracts/src/desktop-bridge.ts` — `SidekicksBridge` interface stub re-exporting the Plan-007 `DaemonMethod` / `DaemonParams` / `DaemonResult` / `DaemonEvent` / `DaemonEventPayload` typed unions and declaring the bridge surface method signatures (no implementations on the renderer side at Tier 1; only the type contract). The conditional-type negative test (`packages/contracts/src/desktop-bridge.test-d.ts`) ships here to lock in the no-auth-material invariant from day one. (Implementation Step 2 substrate.)
- `apps/desktop/eslint.config.mjs` — flat-config `no-restricted-imports` rule banning `electron` / `node:*` / `fs` / `child_process` / `net` / `os` / `path` / `process` / `./src/main/**` / `./src/preload/**` from `apps/desktop/src/renderer/src/**`. CI runs this lint at Tier 1; full keystore + WebAuthn import-bans land at Tier 8 remainder when those modules ship. (Implementation Step 17 substrate fragment.)

#### Tasks

- **T-023p-1-1** (Files: `apps/desktop/package.json` (REPLACE the Plan-001 Phase 1 placeholder scaffold) + root `package.json` workspaces no-op; Verifies invariant: none — substrate scaffold) — Replace the Plan-001 Phase 1 placeholder `apps/desktop/package.json` with the minimal dependency set above; remove the placeholder `apps/desktop/src/index.ts`. Pin `electron-vite@^5.0.0` (not `^6.0.0-beta` per §Risks And Blockers). The Plan-001 Phase 1 placeholder forward-declaration ("split into `apps/desktop/src/{main,preload,renderer}/`") is satisfied by this task.
- **T-023p-1-2** (Files: `apps/desktop/electron.vite.config.ts` (CREATE) + `apps/desktop/tsconfig.json` (REPLACE existing scaffold) + `apps/desktop/src/main/tsconfig.json` (CREATE) + `apps/desktop/src/preload/tsconfig.json` (CREATE) + `apps/desktop/src/renderer/tsconfig.json` (CREATE); Verifies invariant: none — toolchain) — `electron-vite` v5 configuration with three build targets; per-target `tsconfig.json` extending the root TS strict + `isolatedDeclarations: true` posture. Build the package: `pnpm --filter @ai-sidekicks/desktop build` succeeds.
- **T-023p-1-3** (Files: `apps/desktop/src/main/index.ts` (CREATE) + `apps/desktop/src/main/window.ts` (CREATE) + `apps/desktop/build/assert-webprefs.ts` (CREATE); Verifies invariant: Spec-023 §Security Hardening Baseline `webPreferences` lock-in) — Minimal main entrypoint + `createMainWindow()` factory with the locked `webPreferences` block. Build-time webprefs assertion runs as part of `pnpm build` and fails the build if any locked value drifts from Spec-023. **No daemon supervision / no Sentry / no protocol-handler at Tier 1** — those wire in at Tier 8 remainder against the same `index.ts`.
- **T-023p-1-4** (Files: `apps/desktop/src/preload/index.ts` (CREATE) + `packages/contracts/src/desktop-bridge.ts` (CREATE) + `packages/contracts/src/desktop-bridge.test-d.ts` (CREATE); Verifies invariant: Spec-023 §Acceptance Criteria "No auth material on `window.sidekicks`" — typed enforcement) — `SidekicksBridge` interface in `packages/contracts/src/desktop-bridge.ts` re-exporting Plan-007 daemon types; bridge methods declared (no implementations); single `contextBridge.exposeInMainWorld` call in preload. Conditional-type test asserts the interface refuses any property name matching `/token|dpop|prf|secret/i`. Method bodies throw `NotImplementedAtTier1Error` until Tier 8 wires real handlers.
- **T-023p-1-5** (Files: `apps/desktop/src/renderer/src/main.tsx` (CREATE) + `apps/desktop/src/renderer/src/App.tsx` (CREATE) + `apps/desktop/src/renderer/index.html` (CREATE); Verifies invariant: none — substrate scaffold) — Minimal React + Vite renderer mount. Renderer must not import any Node API (enforced by T-023p-1-6 ESLint rule).
- **T-023p-1-6** (Files: `apps/desktop/eslint.config.mjs` (CREATE); Verifies invariant: Spec-023 §Trust Stance — renderer-untrusted enforcement at the import boundary) — Flat-config ESLint with `no-restricted-imports` banning `electron` / `node:*` / `fs` / `child_process` / `net` / `os` / `path` / `process` / `./src/main/**` / `./src/preload/**` from `apps/desktop/src/renderer/src/**`. CI gate at Tier 1; the full ban list (adding `keytar` / `@napi-rs/keyring` / `@sentry/electron`) extends at Tier 8 remainder when those modules land.
- **T-023p-1-7** (Files: `apps/desktop/test/launch.smoke.test.ts` (CREATE — Vitest, not Playwright, at Tier 1; Playwright `_electron` E2E suite ships at Tier 8 per Implementation Step 18); Verifies invariant: substrate boots) — Vitest smoke test that programmatically spawns `electron .` against the built bundle, asserts the main window appears within 5 s, asserts `window.sidekicks` is defined as an object, asserts `window.require` is `undefined`. This is the Tier 1 unblock proof.

After Phase 1 merges, [Plan-001 Phase 5](./001-shared-session-core.md#phase-5--client-sdk-and-desktop-bootstrap) (T5.2 + T5.3 — the `apps/desktop/src/main/sidecar-lifecycle.ts` + `apps/desktop/src/renderer/src/session-bootstrap/` work) and [Plan-024 Phase 3](./024-rust-pty-sidecar.md#phase-3--rustsidecarptyhost--env-var-opt-in) (I-024-4 satisfaction integration test) become reachable. Plan-023 Tier 8 remainder picks up Implementation Steps 6–17 (step 14 reduced to the router + shell subtree per §Renderer; step 17's Tier-1 ban list extends with the keystore / Sentry entries) + 18–20 against the substrate established here, decomposed into Phases 2–8 by [§Tier 8 Remainder PR Sequence](#tier-8-remainder-pr-sequence).

The renderer-load legs — the privileged scheme, the bundle handler, the window's `loadURL`, the auxiliary-window factory, and the unit project their tests run in — were re-homed 2026-09-01 (NS-97) from Phase 3 to the Tier-1 supplement Phase 1B below, because none of them consumes a daemon contract and the Phase 1C console cannot run in the shell without them.

### Phase 1B — Renderer Load Substrate (explicit-label supplement)

**Goal:** The shipped Phase-1 window loads nothing: `createMainWindow()` constructs the locked `BrowserWindow` and never calls `loadURL`, the smoke probe loads `about:blank` behind the test-only `__SIDEKICKS_SMOKE_BUILD__` define, and the handler that serves the bundle was homed at Phase 3 behind Plan-007 R3. Every Phase 1C console PR needs a renderer that actually runs in the shell, with a real origin, and nothing in that need touches the daemon. This supplement ships the renderer-load substrate on Tier 1: the scheme registered privileged before ready (I-023-11), the `sidekicks-renderer://` handler serving the built bundle with a root-containment guard and the `Spec-023 §Security Hardening Baseline` CSP on every response, the main window's load, the auxiliary-window factory the platform menu opens (I-023-12), the `main-unit` Vitest project that makes their units execute, and the Electron-44 / `better-sqlite3`-13.0.3 pin move ADR-016 and ADR-022 decided on 2026-09-01. Phase 3's T-023r-3-4 then **composes** these legs into the full startup order and adds nothing to them. Added 2026-09-01 (NS-97).

**Gate:** Phase 1 merged (PR #30 / #36 / #38). No upstream plan gate — no leg consumes a daemon, control-plane, keystore, or update contract, and every file this phase touches is Plan-023-owned with one named exception class: T-023p-1B-4 also edits `packages/runtime-daemon/package.json` for the `better-sqlite3` move [ADR-022 §Decision Log](../decisions/022-v1-toolchain-selection.md#decision-log) mandates alongside the Electron pin — a dependency bump no plan owns, recorded here rather than routed to a second PR because ADR-022 forbids the Electron pin moving first — and, because that binding's Node-API-10 prebuild needs Node ≥ 22.14.0, the `engines.node` floor line in the root and every other package manifest, the CI Node pins, and the contributor-facing floor sentences the task enumerates: toolchain configuration no plan owns, moved in one commit with the binding so no interval exists in which the pinned binding and the declared floor disagree. The plan-level audit box governs (this phase carries no `audit_status` entry; it was authored under the 2026-09-01 targeted readiness-audit delta that restored the box).
