```yaml
preconditions:
  - { type: plan_phase, plan: 023, phase: 1, status: merged }
```

- `apps/desktop/src/main/protocol.ts` — the registration and the handler, both exported as functions so the startup order is asserted rather than assumed: `registerRendererScheme()` wraps `protocol.registerSchemesAsPrivileged([{ scheme: 'sidekicks-renderer', privileges: { standard: true, secure: true, supportFetchAPI: true } }])` and throws on a second call, because Electron accepts exactly one registration per process and a silent second call would hide a startup-order regression; `installRendererProtocol(rendererRoot)` wraps `protocol.handle('sidekicks-renderer', …)` and is called inside `whenReady()` **before** any window is constructed. The handler resolves `sidekicks-renderer://app/<path>` under the built `out/renderer` tree through one pure function, `resolveRendererAsset(rendererRoot, url)`, that decodes and normalizes the pathname, refuses any resolution that escapes the root (`..`, percent-encoded separators, backslashes, absolute paths, and symlinks — checked through `fs.realpath` against the root's own realpath), refuses any host other than `app`, and answers a miss with 404 and an escape with 403 — both with an empty body and no path echoed, so a probe learns nothing about the tree. There is no `index.html` fallback: the console routes by hash, so every navigable URL is `index.html` plus a fragment and a fallback would only turn typos into a served shell. One reserved path, `/-/load-failure`, is answered from a document GENERATED in the main process and carrying the (escaped, bounded) failure reason — never read from the tree, because the failure it reports is that the tree could not be loaded and a fallback living there is missing exactly when it is needed; it is matched on the exact decoded path so nothing under it falls through, touches no filesystem call so containment is trivially true rather than checked, and carries no script, link, or retry control (Codex round 1). Bodies stream through `net.fetch(pathToFileURL(resolved))` — the pattern Electron's own `protocol.handle` docs give — so nothing is buffered in main-process memory; the `Content-Type` comes from a closed extension map (`html`, `js`, `mjs`, `css`, `json`, `svg`, `png`, `webp`, `woff2`, `wasm`) with `application/octet-stream` for anything else — except `.map`, which answers 404 unconditionally: the build emits hidden source maps for the Sentry upload and they are not shipped, so the handler refuses them even while they sit beside the bundle in a dev tree, and Phase 5's builder `files` filter excludes `out/**/*.map` from the package (Codex round 2); and every response carries the `Content-Security-Policy` header `Spec-023 §Security Hardening Baseline` locks, which is the CSP's **only** carrier — the shipped `index.html` deliberately has no meta tag.
- `apps/desktop/src/main/window.ts` — `createMainWindow()` gains its load and the auxiliary factory lands beside it. One private `constructLockedWindow(options)` owns the `webPreferences` literal `assert-webprefs.ts` greps for, so the build-time assertion covers both factories through a single block. The main window loads `sidekicks-renderer://app/index.html`; under `electron-vite dev` (`!app.isPackaged && process.env.ELECTRON_RENDERER_URL`) it loads the dev-server URL so HMR works, and that branch is the only place the variable is read. The dev origin differs from the packaged scheme origin, so IndexedDB partitions differ between `electron-vite dev` and the built bundle — accepted and stated, because the console's persistence layer keys nothing on origin and every console test tier runs the built bundle. `createAuxiliaryWindow(launch)` takes a validated launch descriptor — the closed `'timeline' | 'agent-console'` route plus the pane context the route is scoped to (`sessionId`, and for `agent-console` the `agentId`), each checked by shape in the main process (canonical UUID form) and never trusted from the caller — constructs through the same private function, loads `sidekicks-renderer://app/index.html#/window/<route>/<sessionId>[/<agentId>]`, and holds no reference to the main window or its renderer state; each window mints its own preload and therefore its own bridge instance by construction. The menu-bar path carries no context, so a menu-opened window loads the bare `#/window/<route>` and the auxiliary renderer shows a **context picker** — the sessions it can subscribe to through its own bridge, and for `agent-console` that session's agents — before it subscribes; a pane-initiated detach passes its context in the descriptor once the window-control namespace lands (Codex round 2). The auxiliary renderer re-subscribes by id over its own bridge, and a context it cannot see renders the pane's _error_ absence shape, never a blank window. Every locked window also carries the **navigation policy** (`apps/desktop/src/main/navigation.ts`, Codex round 1) installed by the same private function as the locked block: in-window navigation is confined to the renderer scheme's own scheme-and-host pair (plus the dev origin under the same two-condition dev branch), every popup is denied including a same-origin one, and an `http(s)` target is handed to the OS browser through a single main-owned `shell.openExternal` call behind a closed scheme allowlist while every other scheme is refused with no side effect — the locked `webPreferences` block governs what the renderer may DO and said nothing about where it may GO, and a top-level navigation to a remote origin would put attacker-served content behind the same preload and the same partition. A **rejected `loadURL`** no longer leaves a live blank window: the window loads the generated load-failure document instead (see the `protocol.ts` bullet), and when even that cannot be served the window is destroyed — the main window's process exiting non-zero with the diagnostic rather than sitting there as an invisible placeholder a harness can only detect by timing out. An auxiliary window whose renderer process is gone (`render-process-gone`) is closed and disposed with the exit reason logged; the main window learns nothing from the main process until the window-control namespace on `§Console growth slate` lands — the console's pane-error slot `Spec-023 §Console Design (Meridian)` §The surface set names is fed by that namespace, not by an ad-hoc IPC channel minted here.
- `apps/desktop/src/main/menu.ts` — the application menu, built once from a template with the platform-default roles and a `Window` submenu carrying the two auxiliary-window entries with their accelerators (`CmdOrCtrl+Shift+T` timeline, `CmdOrCtrl+Shift+A` agent console), each opening the bare route so the auxiliary renderer's context picker chooses the session (and agent) — the menu has no pane to read context from; this is the menu-bar path `Spec-023 §Main Process Responsibilities` says ships first because it needs no new namespace. **Two seams (Codex closing round; the first re-homed at Codex round 1):** the auxiliary entries are built from the typed **shared route manifest** `apps/desktop/src/shared/auxiliary-routes.ts` that Phase 1C grows as each route body lands (T-023p-1C-2 adds `timeline`, T-023p-1C-4 `agent-console`), so Phase 1B ships the `Window` submenu with no auxiliary entry and a menu command never opens an unimplemented route — shared rather than a main-process registry because the registrant would be a renderer route module, which cannot call into the main process, so a registry is a gate nothing ever opens and both entries stay hidden forever; the renderer's own router consumes the same array, so "hidden until registered" is "absent from the manifest" and the two processes cannot disagree; and the template exposes a typed `registerMenuSection` seam through which an owning plan EXTENDs `menu.ts` with its own section — Plan-026 T7.3 registers the `Session` section's _Set up collaboration_ / _Set up providers_ entries through it, gated on this phase merged, which is the owner Plan-026's menu residual names.
- `apps/desktop/src/main/index.ts` — imports `protocol.ts` first and calls `registerRendererScheme()` at module top level, ahead of every `whenReady()` consumer (when Phase 3's crash reporter lands it takes the top-level slot immediately after this registration — the one exception to its crash-first rule, T-023r-3-2 — and ahead of everything else); inside `whenReady()` the order is `installRendererProtocol` → `installApplicationMenu` → `createMainWindow`. The smoke probe's `about:blank` load is **retired**: the probe now waits for the real bundle's `did-finish-load` and the `__SIDEKICKS_SMOKE_BUILD__` define gates only the probe's assertions, so the smoke test proves the bundle is served, not merely that a window exists.
- `apps/desktop/vitest.config.ts` + `apps/desktop/turbo.json` + `apps/desktop/package.json` — the `main-unit` Vitest project (re-homed here from Phase 2's T-023r-2-8, which this supplement retires), because the units above are the first `src/main/**` tests and the landed §Testing posture's two projects reach neither `src/main/**` nor `build/**`.
- `apps/desktop/package.json` + `packages/runtime-daemon/package.json` + `pnpm-lock.yaml` — the pin move: `electron` to the 44.x line ADR-016 fixes, `better-sqlite3` to **13.0.3** exact per ADR-022 (the Electron pin may not move first, so both move in this one task), with the Electron-41 stamps swept from the shipped Tier-1 comments, the `package.json` description, and `.github/workflows/ci.yml`'s sandbox comment, ADR-024's `lifecycle.gc.test.ts` re-run under 44 (its own tripwire), the `Spec-023 §Console Design Research Pass (2026-09-01)` platform-feature probe re-run on the 44 binary, and the `Spec-015 §Driver Pin` amendment riding in-swap with the re-verification its upgrade policy demands.

#### Tasks

- **T-023p-1B-1 — Renderer scheme registration and bundle handler.** `registerRendererScheme()` at module top level, `installRendererProtocol(rendererRoot)` inside ready before any window; `resolveRendererAsset` as a pure function with the containment guard, the closed content-type map, and the fixed CSP header; streaming bodies through `net.fetch`; 403 / 404 with empty bodies. **Files:** `apps/desktop/src/main/protocol.ts` (CREATE), `apps/desktop/src/main/protocol.test.ts` (CREATE — `main-unit`: the traversal arms `..`, `%2e%2e`, `%2F`, `%5C`, an absolute path, and a symlink planted outside a temp root all answer `forbidden` with no path in the result; every mapped extension yields its type and an unmapped one yields `application/octet-stream`; a `.map` planted beside the bundle answers 404 with an empty body; a second `registerRendererScheme()` throws), `apps/desktop/src/main/startup-order.test.ts` (CREATE — `main-unit`: importing `main/index.ts` under a mocked `electron` records `registerSchemesAsPrivileged` before the first `whenReady()` and `protocol.handle` before the first `BrowserWindow` construction; reordering either fails the test), `apps/desktop/src/main/index.ts` (EXTEND) **Spec coverage:** Spec-023 §Security Hardening Baseline (the bundle is served over a custom protocol, never `file://`; the CSP header on every renderer document); Spec-023 §Renderer Bundle (the before-ready privileged registration) **Verifies invariant:** I-023-2 (the load is over the custom protocol), I-023-11 (registration precedes ready and carries `standard: true` / `secure: true`)
- **T-023p-1B-2 — Window load, the auxiliary-window factory, and the application menu.** `constructLockedWindow` as the single owner of the `webPreferences` literal; `createMainWindow()` loading the scheme URL (dev-server URL only under the two-condition dev branch); `createAuxiliaryWindow(launch)` over the validated launch descriptor at `#/window/<route>[/<sessionId>[/<agentId>]]` (bare route from the menu, context from a pane detach); `render-process-gone` closing and logging; `menu.ts` building the `Window` submenu from the SHARED implemented-route list, so an auxiliary entry appears only once its route body exists (Phase 1B implements neither route, and an entry that opened an unimplemented hash route would be a menu command claiming a capability the build does not have); the exported typed `registerMenuSection` seam Plan-026 T7.3 consumes; the navigation policy (`will-navigate` confinement, unconditional popup denial, main-owned allowlisted `shell.openExternal`) installed by the same private function that owns the locked block; a rejected `loadURL` serving the generated failure document and, when even that fails, destroying the window and exiting non-zero for the main one; the smoke probe moved onto the real bundle. **Files:** `apps/desktop/src/main/window.ts` (EXTEND), `apps/desktop/src/main/menu.ts` (CREATE), `apps/desktop/src/shared/auxiliary-routes.ts` (CREATE — the shared route grammar both processes import: the closed `AUXILIARY_ROUTE_NAMES` array with its DERIVED union, the total `AUXILIARY_ROUTE_LABELS` record, the build-time `IMPLEMENTED_AUXILIARY_ROUTES` list the menu and the console's route table both read, and the symmetric `formatAuxiliaryFragment` / `parseAuxiliaryFragment` pair — so no process owns the route type and the producer that ENCODES a fragment sits beside the consumer that DECODES it, which two files cannot stay agreed on; per-process data stays per-process, window geometry in `window.ts` and menu accelerators in `menu.ts`, each a TOTAL `Record` over the closed set so a third route is a compile error at every site that owes it a decision; the first module in a `src/shared/` subtree, which `apps/desktop/eslint.config.mjs` (EXTEND) holds to the renderer's own import ban because every byte of it is bundled into the renderer, and which `apps/desktop/src/renderer/tsconfig.json` (EXTEND) lists and widens `rootDir` for — the package-root config already covers the Node-context side, so the orphan `apps/desktop/src/main/tsconfig.json` + `apps/desktop/src/preload/tsconfig.json` (DELETE), which no script and no `references` entry ever reached, are removed rather than widened), `apps/desktop/src/shared/wire-errors.ts` (CREATE — `isWireErrorEnvelope` / `isWireErrorEnvelopeWithCode` / `lossyStringify` / `normalizeWireRejection`, hoisted out of the three Plan-003 renderer views that each carried a copy, two of them under a comment naming the duplication and defending it on file scope; the one deliberate divergence between the copies — a TOTAL wrap at a prop boundary versus a bare wrap at a bridge `catch` — is now the `total` option rather than a third normalizer) + `apps/desktop/src/renderer/src/runtime-node-attach/AttachFlow.tsx` + `MixedVersionStatus.tsx` + `NodeRoster.tsx` + `CapabilityDeclaration.tsx` (EXTEND — each repointed at it, every `LOCAL DUPLICATE` body and comment deleted; the below-floor wire-code binding survives in the one view that BRANCHES on the code), `apps/desktop/src/main/navigation.ts` (CREATE — the pure navigation classifier and the single `shell.openExternal` call site), `apps/desktop/src/main/navigation.test.ts` (CREATE — `main-unit`: the renderer scheme and its hash routes are in-window; a DIFFERENT non-special scheme is refused even though `URL.origin` is `"null"` for both, which is the false-PASS a `.origin` comparison would take; `file:` / `javascript:` / `data:` / `blob:` / an OS-handler scheme are refused; a credentialed authority is refused; and `openExternalUrl` re-checks the scheme itself, so a direct call with a refused target opens nothing), `apps/desktop/src/main/protocol.ts` (EXTEND — the reserved `/-/load-failure` document) + `apps/desktop/src/main/protocol.test.ts` (EXTEND — it is served with the locked headers, escapes a markup reason, bounds a long one, and is matched on the exact path so a prefix falls through to a 403), `apps/desktop/src/main/index.ts` (EXTEND — menu install, the two probe gates, and the persistence comment corrected to `Spec-023 §Console Design (Meridian)` §Persistence on the renderer scheme: layout / scroll / selection / pins / expansion sets persist and drafts never do) + `apps/desktop/src/main/probes/smoke-probe.ts` + `apps/desktop/src/main/probes/gc-probe.ts` (CREATE — the two probe bodies lifted out of the entrypoint so it reads as the startup ORDER it is asserted for; the ADR-024 probe's `window-all-closed` flag becomes a private field of a `GcProbe` class and its deferral is authored beside it, so the scheduled callback closes over the probe alone and cannot root the window it measures; release elimination is strengthened rather than preserved — with nothing referencing either module and no top-level side effect in either, Rollup drops both whole instead of only the gated branch), `apps/desktop/src/main/menu.test.ts` (CREATE — `main-unit`, the shared `electron` mock and a stubbed implemented-route list: an empty list renders no auxiliary entry and no leading separator while `minimize` / `close` survive; a one-route list renders exactly that entry with its accelerator and clicking it constructs a window at that route's geometry; a list carrying the whole closed set CROSS-ASSERTS the menu's labels against `AUXILIARY_ROUTE_NAMES` / `AUXILIARY_ROUTE_LABELS`, so a route added to the shared module and forgotten here fails; the SHIPPED list is empty while the closed set is unchanged; and a section registered through `registerMenuSection` renders between View and Window, re-registration under one id replaces rather than stacks, and a section with no items renders nothing), `apps/desktop/src/main/window.test.ts` (CREATE — `main-unit`, mocked `electron`: the main window loads `sidekicks-renderer://app/index.html`; the dev URL is loaded only when `isPackaged` is false **and** the variable is set, and never when either condition fails; both auxiliary routes construct with a `webPreferences` object deep-equal to the main window's and load distinct `#/window/…` URLs; a descriptor carrying context loads it in the fragment, and a malformed id is refused before any window is constructed; two auxiliary windows have distinct `webContents` and neither holds the other's or the main window's `id`; and the pre-load hook both factories take runs — with the constructed window — strictly ahead of `loadURL` in one ordered operation log, so a load-lifecycle listener is on time by construction rather than because Electron emits on a later tick, a hook that throws destroying the window instead of leaving a live blank one; a route this build does not implement is refused before construction; every window carries the navigation policy and each verdict is acted on; and a rejected load serves the failure document, with the total-failure arm destroying the window and exiting non-zero only for the main one), `apps/desktop/test/launch.smoke.test.ts` (EXTEND — the probe asserts `location.protocol === 'sidekicks-renderer:'`, `location.host === 'app'`, `typeof indexedDB === 'object'`, a `localStorage` round-trip, `#root` has rendered children, and that `net.fetch('sidekicks-renderer://app/index.html')` from the main process carries the CSP header; the `about:blank` arm is gone, asserted by the test's own grep of the built main bundle), `apps/desktop/build/assert-webprefs.ts` (EXTEND — additionally asserts the literal appears exactly once in `window.ts`, so a second factory cannot carry a second, unchecked block, and SCANS the whole `src/main/**` tree for `new BrowserWindow(` so the exactly-once property is a property of the process rather than of one file (Codex round 1)) + `apps/desktop/build/assert-webprefs.test.ts` (EXTEND — a sibling module, and a nested one, each constructing a window fails; a sibling that only mentions the construction in a comment or a string passes; an unreadable scan tree fails rather than reporting success for a check it never ran), `apps/desktop/test/helpers/electron-mock.ts` (CREATE — the ONE `electron` module mock, parameterised by `recordOrder` / `packaged`, replacing three hand-rolled `vi.mock("electron")` factories and their three near-identical `MockBrowserWindow` classes across `window.test.ts` / `menu.test.ts` / `startup-order.test.ts`; the two suites that STATICALLY import their module under test keep a local stub because a top-level mock instance would still be in its temporal dead zone when the hoisted factory ran, and neither constructs a window), `apps/desktop/src/renderer/src/main.tsx` (EXTEND — the `#root` non-null assertion becomes a named `MissingRootElementError` naming both the id and the document that was supposed to declare it, because React's own "Target container is not a DOM element" names neither in a window whose only other symptom is a white rectangle), `apps/desktop/tsconfig.build.json` + `apps/desktop/turbo.json` + `apps/desktop/package.json` (EXTEND — the `scripts/` directory joins the typecheck and lint inputs, `test/helpers/**` joins the `main-unit` inputs, and the package `lint` script widens to `build scripts src test`) **Spec coverage:** Spec-023 §Main Process Responsibilities (window management — the two auxiliary windows, the menu-bar path); Spec-023 §Console Design (Meridian) (§The surface set — auxiliary windows); Spec-023 §Security Hardening Baseline (every window carries the locked block) **Verifies invariant:** I-023-2 (every window this phase constructs carries the locked block and loads over the custom protocol), I-023-12 (an auxiliary window is built by the locked factory, loads the bundle at a window route, carries its own preload and bridge instance, and shares no store — asserted by constructing two and proving neither's `webContents` is the other's)
- **T-023p-1B-3 — `main-unit` Vitest project registration.** The third Vitest project — named `main-unit` because `main` is the smoke project — node environment, `include: ["src/main/**/*.test.ts", "build/**/*.test.ts"]`, disjoint from both existing globs so the posture's no-double-discovery property holds; the paired `"test:main-unit": "vitest run --project=main-unit"` script and Turbo task with `dependsOn: ["^build"]` and **not** `build:smoke` (plain-TypeScript units need no `electron-vite` bundle, and hanging them off the smoke build would re-impose the ~25-30 s cost the posture exists to avoid), `inputs` of `src/main/**` + `build/**` + `vitest.config.*` + `tsconfig*.json` + `package.json`; the package-level `test` script grows to `turbo run test:renderer test:main-unit test:smoke --filter=@ai-sidekicks/desktop`. Lands in the same PR as T-023p-1B-1, which authors the first `src/main/**` unit. **Files:** `apps/desktop/vitest.config.ts` (EXTEND), `apps/desktop/turbo.json` (EXTEND), `apps/desktop/package.json` (EXTEND) **Spec coverage:** Spec-023 §Main Process Responsibilities (the main-process behavior these units are the only automated check on); Spec-023 §Acceptance Criteria (the criteria whose per-module verification is unit-level rather than E2E) **Verifies invariant:** I-023-1, I-023-4, I-023-6, I-023-11, I-023-12 (load-bearingly — this registration is what makes every `src/main/**` assertion for those invariants execute at all; without it each unit is inert and the invariants are asserted only by the far slower E2E suite)
- **T-023p-1B-4 — Electron 44 and `better-sqlite3` 13.0.3 pin move.** `apps/desktop/package.json` moves `electron` to the 44.x line (`44.1.1` **exact**, saved with `--save-exact` — the plan's own exact-pin rule, so a lockfile refresh cannot move the runtime outside the lane-3 review every bump owes (Codex round 2) — **landed as exact `44.1.0`**, because 44.1.1 published 2026-09-01T18:25Z and this workspace's `minimumReleaseAge: 1440` refuses every 44.1.1 specifier, exact or ranged alike, until 2026-09-02T18:25Z, so no lockfile shape carries it at landing; the bump to `44.1.1` rides this same PR if it is still open by then and otherwise a lane-3 one-line bump, and a `minimumReleaseAgeExclude` entry for `electron` stays rejected as a permanent, unconditional weakening of the guard for exactly the dependency class it exists for ([ADR-022 §Decision Log](../decisions/022-v1-toolchain-selection.md#decision-log)); the `electron-builder` / `electron-updater` heavy set stays deferred to Phase 5 and 7) and `packages/runtime-daemon/package.json` moves `better-sqlite3` to `13.0.3` exact in the same commit, because [ADR-022 §Decision Log](../decisions/022-v1-toolchain-selection.md#decision-log) forbids the Electron pin moving ahead of the binding whose 12.x line has no ABI-149 prebuild, and **the tier-1 Node floor moves `>=22.12.0` → `>=22.14.0` with it** — the 13.x prebuilds are Node-API 10 (declared `NAPI_VERSION=10` in the package's own `binding.gyp`), and a Node-API-9 host (22.12, 22.13) was **observed** to segfault with no diagnostic at the first `new Database()` — Node's _documented_ refusal for a too-new Node-API module is a load-time throw from `ThrowNodeApiVersionError`, so both grades are stated in [Spec-015 §Driver Pin](../specs/015-persistence-recovery-and-replay.md#driver-pin) and the floor turns on neither — across the root and every package `engines.node` (`packages/contracts`, `packages/runtime-daemon`, `packages/client-sdk`, `packages/crypto-paseto`, `apps/desktop`), the three `22.12` pins in `.github/workflows/ci.yml` and the two in `.github/workflows/docs-corpus.yml`, and the floor sentences in `CONTRIBUTING.md`, `CLAUDE.md`, ADR-024's ADR-022 reference line, and Plan-030's CI-pinned-tool-versions note (the `Node 22.12` code comments that record where a behavior was measured are provenance and stay); `pnpm install` must resolve the daemon's binding from a bundled Node-API prebuild with no source compile, asserted by a CI provenance check with two conjuncts — the binding the daemon resolves (the path `lib/binding.js` selects) lives under the tarball's `prebuilds/<platform>-<arch>.node`, and the package's `build/Release/` holds no `.node` — sound because the 13.x line carries no `prebuild-install` (the 12.x line downloaded its prebuild into `build/Release/`, which is why an absence check alone was ambiguous there) and a source build is the only remaining writer of `build/Release/better_sqlite3.node`; `node-gyp` never runs for it at all — 13.0.3 declares no install script, so the only candidate is the implicit `node-gyp rebuild` npm synthesizes for any package shipping a `binding.gyp`, and `pnpm-workspace.yaml` denies it (`allowBuilds: better-sqlite3: false`): upstream writes that `binding.gyp` to no-op when a host prebuild exists, but configure runs before gyp evaluates the condition, and on `windows-latest` node-gyp 11.5.0 cannot parse the image's Visual Studio 18 install, so approving the build fails `pnpm install` outright on a platform whose prebuild is sitting in the tarball unused — measured on the matrix below, four targets green with the build approved and `win32-x64` red, all five green with it denied — and denial is what makes the second conjunct absolute, since `build/Release/` cannot hold a `.node` if no build ever runs. The task sweeps every Electron-41 stamp that is an instruction rather than provenance — `src/main/window.ts`, `electron.vite.config.ts`, `src/main/tsconfig.json`, the two shipped test files, the package description, and the `.github/workflows/ci.yml` SUID-sandbox comment, whose step also stops working on 44: that line publishes **no install script at all** — a **42.0** breaking change 44 inherits rather than one 44 introduced (`docs/breaking-changes.md` §Breaking API Changes (42.0): `electron` "no longer downloads itself via `postinstall` script", and `ELECTRON_SKIP_BINARY_DOWNLOAD` "is no longer supported"; 41.6.1 published `postinstall: node install.js`, and every manifest from 42 through 45 carries no `scripts` field) — and moved binary acquisition to module scope in `npm/index.js`, reached by every `require('electron')`, so `pnpm install` leaves no `dist/`. **Materialized at install time (Codex round 1 follow-up):** `apps/desktop` carries an idempotent `postinstall` running `apps/desktop/scripts/materialize-electron.ts` (CREATE — the `scripts/` + `.ts` home this task's own **Files** list names, reached at the Codex round-2 fold; the `build/*.mjs` shape it was drafted with is superseded), which invokes Electron's own install entry point when the binary is absent, skips when it is present (mirroring upstream's `isInstalled()` — `dist/version` equal to the package version, `path.txt` present, `dist/<path.txt>` on disk), fails closed if the binary is still absent afterwards, and honours `ELECTRON_SKIP_BINARY_DOWNLOAD` — an escape 42.0 dropped — 44.1.0 still publishes an `install.js`, and it no longer reads the variable — reinstated by this wrapper on its own terms — so a leg that never launches Electron stays fast. The seam is measured, not assumed: a full workspace install runs a workspace project's `postinstall` with that project as cwd, a scoped `--filter` install that excludes the project does not, and `pnpm-workspace.yaml`'s `allowBuilds` gates DEPENDENCY scripts only and never a workspace project's own — which is why the root `prepare` step was rejected (it runs on every install, including the legs that must not download). The smoke-test job runs that same script rather than a second acquisition path and takes the sandbox helper from beside the executable the resolved module will spawn rather than searching `.pnpm` for one; every other CI install sets `ELECTRON_SKIP_BINARY_DOWNLOAD` — the renderer-only coverage job, the control-plane Node-24 job, the docs-corpus job, and both release jobs, none of which launches Electron (`electron-vite build` needs only the package's `version` field, verified 2026-09-01 by building with `dist/` absent), while the `native-prebuilds` job's scoped `--filter` never reaches the project at all; the `test:smoke` script runs it before the timed spawn; and `apps/desktop/test/launch.smoke.test.ts` refuses BEFORE spawning with `Electron binary not materialized; run pnpm install` instead of paying the 120–160 MB platform download (130 MB `darwin-arm64`, 158 MB `win32-x64` at 44.1.0) inside the spawn deadline and reporting a timeout — re-runs ADR-024's `lifecycle.gc.test.ts` under 44 (its tripwire row names the version bump as its trigger; a failure is a Plan-023 §Risks item, not a skip), re-runs the `Spec-023 §Console Design Research Pass (2026-09-01)` platform-feature probe on the 44 binary and re-grades its two ❌ cells by amendment where they flip, and carries the `Spec-015 §Driver Pin` amendment in-swap (flip-and-restore): the pin sentence moves to 13.0.3 only with the daemon's pragma-readback tests green under 13.0.3 and the `.backup()` contract re-probed against it (the daemon ships no backup caller yet, so Spec-015's backup-policy atomicity contract has no suite until the backup writer lands — the direct probe stands in), re-running on the pinned tree the 2026-09-01 scratch-tree re-evaluation ADR-022's entry already records: suite identical under both pins, #1507 reproduced on 13.0.1 and closed on 13.0.3 under this repo's own worker-terminate shape (so 13.0.2 is the floor beneath the pin), thirty transitive packages dropped, and `@types/better-sqlite3` staying at its pin because no 13.x types exist. **Provenance on every target (Codex closing round):** the check runs in a dedicated `native-prebuilds` CI job over a hosted-runner matrix covering the five ADR-022 targets — `ubuntu-latest` (linux-x64), `ubuntu-24.04-arm` (linux-arm64), `macos-latest` (darwin-arm64), `macos-15-intel` (darwin-x64), `windows-latest` (win32-x64) — each installing the narrowest filter that resolves `better-sqlite3`, asserting the two-conjunct provenance, opening a database, and reading back the WAL-reopen pragma on the 22.14 floor; a target whose hosted label is unavailable is recorded on this task with the closest label used, never dropped silently. **Files:** `apps/desktop/package.json` (EXTEND), `packages/runtime-daemon/package.json` (EXTEND — the one cross-package touch, named in this phase's gate), `pnpm-lock.yaml` (EXTEND), `apps/desktop/src/main/window.ts` + `apps/desktop/electron.vite.config.ts` + `apps/desktop/test/launch.smoke.test.ts` + `apps/desktop/test/lifecycle.gc.test.ts` (EXTEND — comment sweep, and the two spawn harnesses' `ix` / `cmd` / `args` renamed to `probeTagIndex` / `spawnCommand` / `spawnArguments`), `.github/workflows/ci.yml` (EXTEND — comment sweep, the three Node pins, the prebuild assertion, and the Electron-binary materialization the 44 line's dropped install script forces), `apps/desktop/scripts/materialize-electron.ts` (CREATE — the install-time materializer both the `postinstall` and CI call, in `scripts/` because it is invoked BY NAME from a package script rather than run DURING `pnpm build`, and TypeScript under `node --experimental-strip-types` so the package has one script language and no `.mjs` + hand-written `.d.mts` pair), `apps/desktop/test/launch.smoke.test.ts` (EXTEND — the pre-spawn refusal), `.nvmrc` (EXTEND — the last remaining `22.12` pin in the tree; ### Phase 1C — The Console (explicit-label supplement)

**Goal:** Build the whole Meridian console `Spec-023 §Console Design (Meridian)` fixes — the app frame and icon rail, the all-sessions list, the session workspace (cast bar, deck, sidebar), every pane kind of the closed set, the composer at its pinned home, the settings and operator pages, the two auxiliary-window routes, the palette and keybinding grammar, and the fixture bridge with its scenario engine — over the shipped Tier-1 renderer and the Phase-1B origin, against the fixture bridge for every wire the corpus has not yet registered and against the live bridge for every wire it has. The console is a projection over already-registered bridge namespaces inside the approved spec envelope: a task here that would mint a wire method, event type, error code, table, or column is lane 4 and amends first, and a surface whose wire is a `§Console growth slate` row ships fixture-backed with that row's live-status entry `false` until the row lands. Eight tasks, one per delivery PR, the view-family tasks (T-023p-1C-2..7) dispatching in parallel after T-023p-1C-1 and T-023p-1C-8 landing last; every one of them passes `Spec-023 §Console Test Tiers` and the budgets `Spec-023 §Console Design (Meridian)` §Budgets sets **as far as their subjects exist at that PR** (per-task applicability below), and every view-family task answers the jaw-drop gate — _would you screen-record this and post it unprompted?_ — as a binary verdict in its review notes, one fix pass and one re-check, never an open loop. Added 2026-09-01 (NS-97). **Budget and tier applicability is per task (Codex round 3).** Each delivery PR gates on every budget and test tier whose measurement subject is in-tree at that PR and on no other: T-023p-1C-1 lands the budget harness and the budgets JSON and gates on bundle size, recording heap at rest `n/a` (its subject — one real session open with its timeline rendered — arrives with the ledger family and the scenario picker, so T-023p-1C-8 produces it); T-023p-1C-2 adds frame time and first-ledger-row latency (the flagship scenario is their subject); T-023p-1C-7 adds per-terminal memory; T-023p-1C-8 adds the endurance run and binds the full gate, which every later console PR then answers whole. A budget or tier whose subject is absent is recorded `n/a` in the harness output beside the task that will produce it, never skipped silently.

**Gate:** Phase 1B merged (the served origin, the 44.x runtime, the `main-unit` project). No upstream plan gate — nothing here consumes a daemon, control-plane, keystore, or update contract beyond what the shipped Tier-1 bridge types already declare; the live wiring of any surface waits on the `§Console growth slate` row or the Tier-8 phase that registers its wire, and Phase 6 **adopts** the result (T-023r-6-1 EXTEND). The plan-level audit box governs (no `audit_status` entry; authored under the 2026-09-01 targeted readiness-audit delta).

## Tier 8 Remainder PR Sequence

Authored 2026-08-10 by the Tier-8 remainder readiness audit (NS-20, C-023-1). Before this section the remainder existed only as narrative Implementation Steps: no `### Phase N` heading for `walkPhases` to select, no `#### Tasks` block for the declared-task extractor, and no per-phase `preconditions:` YAML to resolve the Plan-007 / Plan-018 / Plan-008 edges §Preconditions names in prose. Phase numbering continues from the Tier-1 partial's Phase 1; the remainder's task ids use the `T-023r-<phase>-<n>` remainder namespace (the Tier-1 partial used `T-023p-1-<n>`).

**Phase ordering is the §Parallelization Notes dependency shape, serialized.** Each phase gates on its predecessor via the intra-plan `plan_phase` chain, and every upstream-plan `## Preconditions` box additionally rides a `precondition_box_checked` entry on **each** phase it gates — not on one phase alone. The auto-walk treats a preconditions-ineligible phase as a soft skip and tries later phases, so a box declared once would let the walk select the skipped phase's successor straight past the unmet dependency; the chain plus the repeated box entry is what closes that. Plan-023 Phase 1 resolves fully-shipped today (declared T-023p-1-1..7 ⊆ the manifest's ten), so the chain's base link is live.

### Phase 2 — IPC Bridge Registry And Per-Surface Handlers

**Goal:** Implementation Step 6 in full — `bridge/index.ts` plus the seven per-surface handler files — and Step 17's Tier-8 ban-list extension; this phase's main-process units execute in the `main-unit` Vitest project Phase 1B registers (T-023p-1B-3). Every preload-bridge method from `Spec-023 §Preload Bridge Contract` gets exactly one `ipcMain.handle`; auth material is attached server-side and stripped from responses; the Tier-1 `NotImplementedAtTier1Error` stubs are replaced by real forwarding.

**Preconditions.** Consumes Plan-007's daemon client and the R3-owned `bridge/daemon-status.ts` (CP-023-3), Plan-018's token API (the DPoP presentation `bridge/control-plane.ts` performs), Plan-008's tRPC + relay-WSS clients, the Plan-007-owned contracts type exports, and the deferred per-subscription request-param channel. All five `**(Tier 8 remainder only.)**` boxes those name gate this phase.

<!-- prettier-ignore -->
```yaml
preconditions:
  - { type: plan_phase, plan: 023, phase: 1, status: merged }
  - { type: external_plan_phase_merged, plan: 007, phase: R3 }
  - { type: precondition_box_checked, box: "(Tier 8 remainder only.)** Plan-007 exposes the" }
  - { type: precondition_box_checked, box: "(Tier 8 remainder only.)** Plan-018 exposes a main-process-consumable API" }
  - { type: precondition_box_checked, box: "(Tier 8 remainder only.)** Plan-008 exposes a tRPC v11 client" }
  - { type: precondition_box_checked, box: "(Tier 8 remainder only.)** `packages/contracts/src/` exports" }
  - { type: precondition_box_checked, box: "(Tier 8 remainder only.)** Plan-007 / Plan-008 resolve the per-subscription request-param channel" }
```

#### Tasks

- **T-023r-2-1 — Bridge registry.** `bridge/index.ts` registers one `ipcMain.handle('<channel>', handler)` per contract method and **imports and registers the Plan-007-owned `bridge/daemon-status.ts` on the `daemon.subscribe` channel without rewriting its body** (CP-023-3). Registration is exhaustive over the contract's method union, so a method added to `SidekicksBridge` without a handler fails the build rather than surfacing at runtime. **Files:** `apps/desktop/src/main/bridge/index.ts` (CREATE), `apps/desktop/src/main/bridge/index.test.ts` (CREATE) **Spec coverage:** Spec-023 §Preload Bridge Contract (one handler per bridge method); Spec-023 §Main Process Responsibilities (IPC handler registration is main-process work) **Verifies invariant:** I-023-1 (the registry exposes no channel whose name or payload carries auth material)
- **T-023r-2-2 — Daemon forwarding handler.** `bridge/daemon.ts` — `daemon.call` / `daemon.subscribe` forwarding to the Plan-007 daemon client with the daemon session token attached as an RPC-level header in the main process. Subscriptions use the parameterized signature the request-param precondition box unblocks. **Files:** `apps/desktop/src/main/bridge/daemon.ts` (CREATE), `apps/desktop/src/main/bridge/daemon.test.ts` (CREATE) **Spec coverage:** Spec-023 §Preload Bridge Contract (`daemon.call` / `daemon.subscribe` shapes; the deferred request-param channel); Spec-023 §Renderer → Shell → Daemon (via Preload Bridge) **Verifies invariant:** I-023-1 (the daemon session token never appears in a value returned across the bridge)
- **T-023r-2-3 — Control-plane forwarding handler.** `bridge/control-plane.ts` — `controlPlane.call` attaches `Authorization: DPoP <paseto-v4.public>` + `DPoP: <signed-proof>` (RFC 9449 §7.1, never `Bearer`); `controlPlane.subscribeRelay` attaches **neither**, authenticating solely via the `Sec-WebSocket-Protocol` subprotocol carrying the main-owned `connectionToken`, which is consumed in the main process and never crosses the bridge. Responses are sanitized — `set-cookie` and every upstream auth-material-carrying header stripped — before the body returns to the renderer. Header attachment is **conditional on holding a credential**, and the anonymous arm is asserted, not assumed: a test drives `invite.preview` with no credential present and asserts the request is **still issued**, carrying neither `Authorization` nor `DPoP`, rather than refused for want of one. The credential-present arm is deliberately unpinned — `Spec-002 §Interfaces And Contracts` makes the procedure anonymous (authentication not required), not unauthenticated (authentication forbidden), and its `Spec-021 §Canonical Endpoint Group Registry` row keys per token-hash rather than per principal, so attaching a held pair is permitted and immaterial to admission. **Files:** `apps/desktop/src/main/bridge/control-plane.ts` (CREATE), `apps/desktop/src/main/bridge/control-plane.test.ts` (CREATE) **Spec coverage:** Spec-023 §Renderer → Shell → Control Plane (via Preload Bridge); Spec-023 §Acceptance Criteria (no auth material on `window.sidekicks`) **Verifies invariant:** I-023-1 (response sanitization is asserted, not assumed — a planted `set-cookie` and a planted bearer header must both be absent from the returned body)
- **T-023r-2-4 — Native-surface handler.** `bridge/native.ts` — dialogs, message boxes, notifications, clipboard, reveal-in-file-explorer, and `openExternal` behind an allowlist. Filesystem paths cross the bridge only as opaque branded `FilePathRef` tokens; dereferencing requires a second round trip through the main process. **Files:** `apps/desktop/src/main/bridge/native.ts` (CREATE), `apps/desktop/src/main/bridge/native.test.ts` (CREATE) **Spec coverage:** Spec-023 §Preload Bridge Contract (`FilePathRef` is opaque; the renderer cannot dereference a path); Spec-023 §Trust Stance (renderer-supplied input is untrusted) **Verifies invariant:** I-023-1 (no native-surface return value carries a raw filesystem path or auth material)
- **T-023r-2-5 — Update, WebAuthn, onboarding, and invite handler surfaces.** `bridge/update.ts` (`getState` / `subscribe` / `requestCheck` / `requestRestart`, forwarding to the Phase-5 state machine behind a seam), `bridge/webauthn.ts` (dispatching to the Phase-4 per-platform strategy behind a seam) — **and, at the Codex round-4 sweep, the contracts-side retirement this narrowing owes**: `packages/contracts/src/desktop-bridge.ts` still declares the shipped Tier-1 `webAuthn` namespace as `createCredential(options)` / `getAssertion(options)` / `deriveKeyMaterial(input)`, the exact three-method shape I-023-16 withdrew, so this task narrows the interface and its `createTier1BridgeStub` arm to `register()` / `signIn()` and drops the now-unreferenced `PrfInput` type and the three DOM stubs that only existed to type the withdrawn parameters. Retiring it here rather than in Phase 4 is deliberate: this is the task that authors the surface's real handlers, and a contract that still advertises a caller-supplied-options method is an invitation to build one, `bridge/onboarding.ts` (**Plan-023 authors the surface and wires the modal window; Plan-026 lands the flow body** — the stubs registered here are the contract Plan-026 consumes), and `bridge/invite.ts` (`subscribePending` / `subscribeOutcome` / `confirmPending` / `retryPending` / `dismissPending`, forwarding to the Phase-5 deep-link handler behind the same seam shape). **This task pins the `invite` wire contract, not just its method names** — Phase 2 lands the public shape before the Phase-5 body fills it, so leaving payloads to that later implementer would let an exhaustive registry enforce presence while interoperability stayed unpinned. `subscribePending` and `subscribeOutcome` each carry a **discriminated union** keyed on `status`. Pending: `ready` (the opaque `reference` plus `{sessionId, joinMode, expiresAt, sessionName, inviterDisplayName}` exactly as `Spec-002 §Interfaces And Contracts` returns them, the two display fields nullable), `refused` (one of `invite.not_found` / `invite.expired` / `invite.revoked` / `invite.already_accepted`, and **no** `reference` member on this arm), `unavailable` (`retryable: true`, no `reference`, and an opaque **non-confirmable** `attempt` handle naming which deep link failed — two protocol URLs can be outstanding at once, and a retry that cannot say which one it means could re-drive or discard the wrong invite). Outcome: `joined` (the `sessionId` the renderer navigates to), `authentication-required` (main is driving the Plan-018 surface; the reference is preserved), `authentication-failed` (`cancelled` — the participant dismissed the passkey prompt — or `failed`; **the preserved reference is released with it**, making this arm terminal, because an `authentication-required` progress state with no terminal counterpart would strand the surface on an event that never arrives), `refused` (the same four codes, the reference now consumed), `reference-invalid` (`unknown` / `consumed` / `expired` — the three reference-side refusals I-023-10 requires), `unavailable` (`retryable: true`). `confirmPending(reference)` and `dismissPending(reference)` take the opaque reference and resolve to `void` — the membership result is a bridge event per `Spec-023 §Deep-Link Invite Flow` steps 6 and 8, never a promise value; `retryPending(attempt)` takes the `attempt` handle from the `unavailable` arm it is retrying. `attempt` is a distinct brand from `reference` and is accepted by no other method, so it can re-drive a preview but can never confirm one. `subscribeOutcome` is **not** a second channel for a fact the corpus already delivers: the in-app accept view (`apps/desktop/src/renderer/src/session-members/invite-accept-view.tsx`) learns its outcome from the return value of the accept call **it issues itself**, while the deep-link renderer never issues that call — main does, after confirmation — so an event is the only way the result can reach it. The two legs differ by which process holds the token, the same split `Spec-023 §Deep-Link Invite Flow` draws. **Reference TTL is `min(mint + 10 minutes, expiresAt)`** — human-scaled for a modal decision, never outliving the invite it references. The `invite` namespace must exist at this phase even though its body lands at Phase 5, because T-023r-2-1's registration is exhaustive over the `SidekicksBridge` method union — a namespace added to the interface without a registered handler fails the build. Extending the interface and its `createTier1Bridge()` per-namespace stubs is part of this task, under the §Target Areas Contracts authoring rule for Tier-8 namespaces. The `invite` preload arm ships in this same task, because the main-side registration is only half a reachable path: without it a renderer call to `window.sidekicks.invite.*` resolves to the `createTier1Bridge()` throwing stub and never reaches the handler registered here. `subscribePending` mounts as an `ipcRenderer` event subscription returning the contracts-declared `Unsubscribe`, matching the shipped `daemon.subscribe` / `update.subscribe` shape, rather than as an `invoke`. The `onboarding` preload arm is deliberately **not** in scope here — Plan-026 T5.1 owns `apps/desktop/src/preload/onboarding.ts` and its `index.ts` spread, so this task authors only that namespace's main-side stubs and its interface member. **Files:** `apps/desktop/src/main/bridge/update.ts` (CREATE), `apps/desktop/src/main/bridge/webauthn.ts` (CREATE), `apps/desktop/src/main/bridge/onboarding.ts` (CREATE), `apps/desktop/src/main/bridge/invite.ts` (CREATE), `apps/desktop/src/preload/invite.ts` (CREATE), `apps/desktop/src/preload/index.ts` (EXTEND — one import + spread), `packages/contracts/src/desktop-bridge.ts` (EXTEND — **the WebAuthn surface narrows here**: `register()` / `signIn()` replace `createCredential(options)` / `getAssertion(options)` / `deriveKeyMaterial(input)`, and the `PublicKeyCredentialCreationOptions` / `PublicKeyCredentialRequestOptions` / `PublicKeyCredential` / `PrfInput` Tier-1 stubs are retired with them, since nothing on the narrowed surface references a DOM WebAuthn type — which also retires the Tier-8 `lib: ["dom"]` decision those stubs deferred) **Spec coverage:** Spec-023 §Preload Bridge Contract (the update / WebAuthn / onboarding method groups, and the Tier-8 namespace rule for `invite`); Spec-023 §Deep-Link Invite Flow (the opaque-reference handoff the `invite` surface carries) **Verifies invariant:** I-023-1 (the `invite` surface exposes the pending invite by opaque reference, never by token), I-023-10 (the preload arm forwards the reference and never resolves it), I-023-16 (the negative test is two-armed and both arms are needed: a type-level assertion in `desktop-bridge.test-d.ts` that neither `register` nor `signIn` accepts an argument — so a renderer passing options fails to compile — and a runtime assertion that the main-side handler refuses an IPC frame carrying a payload, since the compile-time arm binds only callers this repository builds)
- **T-023r-2-6 — Renderer import-ban extension (Step 17, Tier-8 leg).** Extends the Tier-1 `no-restricted-imports` list with `keytar`, `@napi-rs/keyring`, `@sentry/electron`, `koffi`, the `main/keystore.ts` / `main/webauthn/**` modules, and the `apps/desktop/native/**` addon tree now that they exist — the last of these because it is a trusted-side sibling of `src/`, which the Tier-1 `src/main/**` and `src/preload/**` patterns do not reach. The rule is a CI gate, not advice: a renderer file importing any banned specifier fails the build. **Files:** `apps/desktop/eslint.config.mjs` (EXTEND) **Spec coverage:** Spec-023 §Trust Stance (renderer-untrusted enforcement at the import boundary); Spec-023 §Renderer Responsibilities (the renderer is a projection, never an authority) **Verifies invariant:** I-023-8 (the extended ban list is asserted by a fixture file that must fail lint)
- **T-023r-2-7 — Bridge adversarial-boundary arms.** The intake-validation-parity and mutation-isolation-symmetry arms of §Adversarial-Tampering Boundary: each handler's own validation is asserted at least as strict as the contract schema (a payload the schema rejects must not pass the handler), and structured-clone discipline is asserted in both directions — a renderer-held reference cannot mutate main-process state, and a renderer-supplied object is cloned on the way in. **Files:** `apps/desktop/src/main/bridge/adversarial-boundary.test.ts` (CREATE) **Spec coverage:** Spec-023 §Trust Stance (every renderer-originated payload is untrusted input); Spec-023 §Preload Bridge Contract (the contract schema is the validation floor) **Verifies invariant:** I-023-1 (the parity arm is what keeps a lax handler from re-admitting a shape the contract forbids)

### Phase 3 — Daemon Supervisor And Crash Reporter

**Goal:** Implementation Steps 7 and 12, plus the `main/index.ts` startup composition that orders them. Crash reporting initializes first; daemon supervision starts after `app.whenReady()`.

**Preconditions.** Phase 2's registry must exist before the supervisor's lifecycle events have a bridge surface to reach. Consumes Plan-007's daemon-control surface and its `DaemonHello` readiness signal — the box that names it gates this phase as well as Phase 2. Phase 1B must also have merged: T-023r-3-4's composition-only EXTEND orders the `protocol.ts`, `menu.ts`, and auxiliary-window factory that supplement creates, so the dependency is a formal precondition entry rather than an assumption that the files are in-tree (Codex round 3) — spelled `external_plan_phase_merged`, the one resolver that performs the declared-task-set lookup for a supplement label; the numeric `plan_phase` resolver matches the manifest's integer phase key and would report every supplement task missing forever (Codex closing round).

<!-- prettier-ignore -->
```yaml
preconditions:
  - { type: plan_phase, plan: 023, phase: 2, status: merged }
  - { type: external_plan_phase_merged, plan: 023, phase: 1B }
  - { type: precondition_box_checked, box: "(Tier 8 remainder only.)** Plan-007 exposes the" }
```

#### Tasks

- **T-023r-3-1 — Daemon supervisor.** `daemon-supervisor.ts` — `utilityProcess.fork(daemonEntryPath, args, { serviceName: 'ai-sidekicks-daemon' })` invoked after `app.whenReady()`, never `child_process.fork`. Exit-reason switch: a `"memory-eviction"` exit restarts **without** incrementing the failure counter; any other non-zero exit increments and backs off along the `100ms, 300ms, 1s, 3s, 10s` ladder, surfacing persistent-error state after five attempts. Version-negotiation gate on `DaemonHello`: a `protocol_version` below the shell's pinned minimum blocks mutating bridge calls and permits read-only subscriptions only. Publishes `daemon.process.*` into the Plan-007 R3 status projector (CP-023-2). **Files:** `apps/desktop/src/main/daemon-supervisor.ts` (CREATE), `apps/desktop/src/main/daemon-supervisor.test.ts` (CREATE) **Spec coverage:** Spec-023 §Daemon Supervision Lifecycle (the five-step backoff ladder; persistent-error state after five consecutive crashes; the `"memory-eviction"` special case); Spec-023 §Utility Process Vs Child Process (`utilityProcess.fork()` after `app.whenReady()`) **Verifies invariant:** I-023-4 (the exit-reason switch never increments the failure counter on a memory-eviction exit, and the counter is the only input to the persistent-error transition)
- **T-023r-3-2 — Crash reporter and per-process Sentry init.** `crash-reporter.ts` initializing `@sentry/electron/main` v7.11.0 with the `beforeSend` scrubber, called first among the startup steps in `main/index.ts` — ahead of the single-instance lock, the protocol handler install, every window, and the daemon supervisor — with one named exception that precedes it at module top level: Phase 1B's `registerRendererScheme()`, which Electron requires before `app.ready` and which touches no network, no file, and no crash-relevant state, so a fault inside it is a startup-order regression `startup-order.test.ts` catches, not a report the reporter would have caught (2026-09-01); `renderer/src/sentry.ts` and the utility-process init, because the v7 SDK does not auto-initialize renderers. The scrubber deletes `token` / `dpop` / `session_token` / `prf_output` top-level keys, replaces session ids with stable SHA-256 hashes, truncates file paths to their extension, and elides content payloads. **Files:** `apps/desktop/src/main/crash-reporter.ts` (CREATE), `apps/desktop/src/renderer/src/sentry.ts` (CREATE), `apps/desktop/src/main/crash-reporter.test.ts` (CREATE) **Spec coverage:** Spec-023 §Crash Reporting (per-process `init()`; `SentryMinidump` from Crashpad; main-process `beforeSend` scrubbing); Spec-023 §Acceptance Criteria (crash reports carry no tokens, session ids, file paths, or content payloads) **Verifies invariant:** I-023-1 (the scrubber is the bridge invariant's egress-side twin — auth material must not leave by the crash channel either)
- **T-023r-3-3 — Crash-report exfiltration arm.** The §Adversarial-Tampering Boundary crash-exfiltration arm: a synthetic event is planted with a PASETO token, a DPoP proof, a session id, an absolute file path, and a content payload; the test asserts every one is absent from the post-`beforeSend` event. Records the known residual explicitly — `beforeSend` does not reach raw minidump bytes, which are covered by the server-side scrubbing rules and the opt-in upload gate, not by this test. **Files:** `apps/desktop/src/main/crash-scrubber-exfiltration.test.ts` (CREATE) **Spec coverage:** Spec-023 §Crash Reporting (the scrubber is the sole client-side barrier); Spec-023 §Pitfalls To Avoid (heap-resident secrets reaching an upload endpoint) **Verifies invariant:** I-023-1 (a negative test, so the barrier is proven rather than asserted)
- **T-023r-3-4 — Main startup composition.** Grows `main/index.ts` into the full startup sequence: scheme registration at module top level (Phase 1B, the one step Electron pins before ready), then crash reporter, single-instance lock, protocol handler install, application menu, main window creation, daemon supervisor start. **Composes, never replaces, the Plan-001-owned `sidecar-lifecycle.ts` will-quit drain** (CP-023-1) — a diff that removes the drain wiring is a review rejection. Window construction and retention follow ADR-024's reachability mechanism so the window is not collected mid-session. **Files:** `apps/desktop/src/main/index.ts` (EXTEND), `apps/desktop/src/main/startup-composition.test.ts` (CREATE) **Spec coverage:** Spec-023 §Main Process Responsibilities (single-instance lock; startup ordering; protocol registration); Spec-023 §Acceptance Criteria (the main-process window-retention bullet, whose mechanism ADR-024 supplies) **Verifies invariant:** I-023-2 (every window this sequence creates carries the locked `webPreferences` block and loads over the custom protocol)
- **T-023r-3-4 EXTEND (2026-09-01 — composing the Phase 1B renderer-load legs).** The privileged scheme registration, the bundle handler, the main window's load, the auxiliary-window factory, and the application menu ship on Tier 1 at T-023p-1B-1 / T-023p-1B-2 (re-homed there the same day they were first written here, because none of them consumes a daemon contract). This composition **inherits** them and adds nothing to them: the full order is scheme registration (module top level, ahead of every `whenReady()` consumer — the one exception T-023r-3-2's crash-first rule names) → crash reporter → single-instance lock → `installRendererProtocol` → application menu → main window → daemon supervisor, and `startup-composition.test.ts` asserts that the registration still precedes ready and the handler still precedes the first window after the Phase-3 growth — a diff that reorders either, or re-authors `protocol.ts` / `window.ts` / `menu.ts` here, is a review rejection. No renderer-initiated detach exists until the window-control namespace on `§Console growth slate` lands. **Files:** `apps/desktop/src/main/index.ts` (EXTEND) and `startup-composition.test.ts` (EXTEND) only. **Verifies invariant:** I-023-11 (the order survives composition), I-023-12 (the auxiliary factory is the one Phase 1B shipped).

### Phase 4 — Keystore, WebAuthn Dispatcher, And DAG Fallback

**Goal:** Implementation Steps 8, 9, and 10 — the credential-at-rest and credential-ceremony surfaces.

**Preconditions.** Phase 3 must have merged (the keystore's degradation event rides the bridge surface the supervisor phase completes). Consumes Plan-018's token API: the keystore stores what Plan-018 issues, and the DAG fallback exchanges an authorization code for it. **And Plan-018 Phase 6 must have merged (2026-09-01, Codex round 4)** — it is the relying party's half of the ceremony this phase's bindings perform: without it `register()` and `signIn()` reach a native authenticator with nothing to issue a challenge, nothing to verify the response, and nothing to serve the server-held user-verification mode guard 4 compares against (CP-023-10 ⇄ Plan-018 CP-018-14). The gate is mechanical rather than a born-unchecked box, on the Plan-026 Phase-7 precedent.

<!-- prettier-ignore -->
```yaml
preconditions:
  - { type: plan_phase, plan: 023, phase: 3, status: merged }
  - { type: precondition_box_checked, box: "(Tier 8 remainder only.)** Plan-018 exposes a main-process-consumable API" }
  - { type: external_plan_phase_merged, plan: 018, phase: 6 }
```

#### Tasks

- **T-023r-4-1 — Keystore abstraction.** `keystore.ts` exporting `getSecret` / `setSecret` / `deleteSecret` over `@napi-rs/keyring` v1.2.0 primary with Electron `safeStorage` as the one-shot-blob fallback. At startup `safeStorage.isEncryptionAvailable()` must be true, and on Linux `safeStorage.getSelectedStorageBackend()` must be `gnome_libsecret` or `kwallet[456]` — `basic_text` and `unknown` are refused. On refusal a `keystoreUnavailable` bridge event is emitted and `setSecret` throws `KeystoreUnavailableError` thereafter. **Files:** `apps/desktop/src/main/keystore.ts` (CREATE), `apps/desktop/src/main/keystore.test.ts` (CREATE) **Spec coverage:** Spec-023 §Native Keystore (the `@napi-rs/keyring` primary path; the Linux plaintext-fallback refusal); Spec-023 §Shell ↔ OS Keystore (the per-platform backends, the custody-root entries keyed by credential id, and the access discipline) **Verifies invariant:** I-023-6 (a `basic_text` or `unknown` backend is refused and persistence degrades to memory-only rather than writing plaintext)
- **T-023r-4-2 — Keystore refusal-bypass arm.** The §Adversarial-Tampering Boundary keystore arm: after a refusal, a subsequent `setSecret` must still throw rather than silently falling through to an in-memory or on-disk path, and no later call may re-enable persistence without a fresh successful backend check. Refusal is sticky by construction, not by convention. **Files:** `apps/desktop/src/main/keystore-refusal.test.ts` (CREATE) **Spec coverage:** Spec-023 §Native Keystore (refusal is terminal for the session); Spec-023 §Fallback Behavior (memory-only degradation is the declared fallback) **Verifies invariant:** I-023-6 (the sticky-refusal property is what makes the invariant hold across the whole session, not just at startup)
- **T-023r-4-3 — WebAuthn strategy dispatcher and the macOS binding.** `webauthn/index.ts` selecting the binding module from `process.platform` — `darwin` to `mac.ts`, `win32` to `windows.ts`, `linux` to `linux.ts` — each behind a guarded dynamic `import()` so no host evaluates a binding it cannot use, all four exporting identical signatures. `mac.ts` binds the own-built `native/webauthn-darwin/` addon and supplies the **caller's** challenge, which is why the Vault12 package is the reference implementation and not the dependency. The addon drives **two** providers on independent arms — `ASAuthorizationPlatformPublicKeyCredentialProvider` for Touch ID / iCloud Keychain and `ASAuthorizationSecurityKeyPublicKeyCredentialProvider` for an attached FIDO2 key — with PRF attached as the directly-named `prf` request property rather than through an `extensions` dictionary, which the framework does not have, and with its two floors honoured separately at runtime: platform PRF from macOS 15.0, security-key PRF only from macOS 26.4, so a roaming key on 15.0–26.3 completes sign-in and yields no PRF, taking the custody leg rather than a refusal. Both arms are **attempted unconditionally**: macOS exposes no direct platform-authenticator availability call, only `LAContext.canEvaluatePolicy`, which the addon reads on the `.deviceOwnerAuthentication` policy and records as an advisory diagnostic that suppresses nothing. The narrower `.deviceOwnerAuthenticationWithBiometrics` this task first named is documented false on a Mac with no Touch ID hardware, with no enrolled biometric, and system-wide after repeated biometric failures — states in which a platform passkey ceremony still completes through iCloud Keychain, a paired device, or the user's password — so gating on it would have refused the platform provider on hosts that can serve it (corrected 2026-09-01, Codex round 2). PRF output arrives as a CryptoKit `SymmetricKey`, so the addon extracts bytes rather than casting. **This leg needs no off-thread machinery (Codex round 4):** `AuthenticationServices` is asynchronous by construction — the ceremony is started and its result arrives on an `ASAuthorizationController` delegate callback — so the addon bridges that callback to a resolved promise and the main event loop is never blocked; the two `koffi` legs are the ones that need the library's asynchronous call path, and they take it in T-023r-4-5 and T-023r-4-6. **Guard 4 is enforced on the response (Codex round 3):** `ASAuthorizationPublicKeyCredentialUserVerificationPreference` is a preference, so the addon surfaces `rawAuthenticatorData` and `mac.ts` reads bit 2 (`0x04`) of its flags byte against the mode the **verification reply** returns for the credential that signed (re-homed off the options reply at Codex round 5, since a discoverable sign-in names no credential in advance), deriving nothing and taking the fallback on a mismatch. **The dispatcher owns the DPoP proof the verify request carries (Codex round 7):** the verification procedure issues a sender-bound token pair, so the request it answers has to prove possession of a key, and nothing in this plan supplied one. `index.ts` resolves a DPoP key pair before submitting — generating it on first use and persisting it through `keystore.ts` (T-023r-4-1) as its own entry under the [ADR-021](../decisions/021-cli-identity-key-storage-custody.md) ladder, **not** under the custody root, since the proof is signed before the reply that would let a root be minted — and signs the **token-request** proof form over that request's method and URI, carrying **no** `ath` because no access token exists yet to hash ([RFC 9449 §5](https://datatracker.ietf.org/doc/html/rfc9449#section-5)). The relying party derives the issued pair's `cnf.jkt` from that proof's public key (Plan-018 T6.3, I-018-20), so the same key must be reused on every later request the pair authenticates; a key regenerated per ceremony would bind a pair the shell immediately cannot use. Tested for reuse across a restart and for the absent-`ath` shape. **The dispatcher owns the verify-before-derive ordering (Codex round 5, I-023-17):** every leg returns its assertion and PRF bytes to `index.ts`, which submits the assertion to `Spec-018`'s authentication-verify procedure and treats the bytes as **provisional** until that verdict — zeroizing them on every refusal arm before anything is cached, derived, or unwrapped. Placing the ordering in the dispatcher rather than in each leg is deliberate: three legs each implementing it is three chances to derive first, and the bytes reach one place anyway. The dispatcher settles nothing about capability: it routes to `fallback-dag.ts` only when every arm this host offers has been attempted and none produced a PRF result the leg is willing to derive from. **A cancellation is not one of those cases (Codex round 5, I-023-18):** a participant who dismisses the OS dialog has answered, so the dispatcher stops — no second provider arm on a host that offers two, no DAG loopback (which would present a browser sign-in to someone who just declined to sign in), and the outcome rides `WebAuthnCeremonyOutcome`'s refused arm rather than the fallback-required one. The macOS leg is where this is most concrete, since it is the only host with two providers to fall through to. **The relying-party identifier is the paired control-plane origin (Codex round 5):** the dispatcher validates against the origin main pinned at pairing, never a build constant, so a self-hosted deployment is not refused by its own `rpId`; on such a deployment the platform provider is additionally unavailable to a stock build, because the `webcredentials:` associated-domain entitlement is compiled into the signed application, and this leg degrades to the security-key arm with the reason surfaced rather than reported as a failed ceremony. **Two dispatcher-level rules land here (Codex round 3).** A binding that will not load — a rejected dynamic `import()`, a missing library, an unresolved symbol, a `.node` without this architecture's slice — is caught at the load boundary, recorded as a diagnostic naming the library and the symbol, and treated as a **negative capability result**, never rethrown and never reported as a failed ceremony. And the derived-key surface leaves the main process nowhere: `index.ts` additionally exports `PrfKeyDerivationService`, a single-method main-owned port (`deriveKek`) that runs the resolved binding's ceremony and returns bytes to a main-process caller only, is named in no `SidekicksBridge` type, and is the producer CP-023-9 registers for Plan-022's forward-declared Tier-8 KEK branch. **This task also builds the addon**, which no task previously owned: `apps/desktop/native/webauthn-darwin/` gains a `swift build` invocation per architecture joined with `lipo -create` into one universal `.node`, wired as an `apps/desktop/package.json` build script that the desktop build depends on so a `pnpm build` on macOS cannot silently produce a bundle with no addon in it, run on the macOS CI leg of `.github/workflows/ci.yml` and emitted into the path `electron-builder.yml` already `asarUnpack`s. The toolchain floor is **Xcode 26.4** (Swift 6.3, macOS 26.4 SDK, itself requiring macOS 26.2+) because the security-key `prf` property is a 26.4 SDK symbol; the deployment target stays macOS 12.0 and the two later floors are honoured through availability annotations rather than by raising it. **Files:** `apps/desktop/src/main/webauthn/index.ts` (CREATE), `apps/desktop/src/main/webauthn/dpop-key.ts` (CREATE — the generate-on-first-use / persist / reuse coordinator for the proof key, and the token-request proof signer), `apps/desktop/src/main/webauthn/dpop-key.test.ts` (CREATE), `apps/desktop/src/main/webauthn/mac.ts` (CREATE), `apps/desktop/native/webauthn-darwin/` (CREATE — sources plus the universal-build script), `apps/desktop/src/main/webauthn/index.test.ts` (CREATE), `apps/desktop/package.json` (EXTEND — the addon build script the desktop build depends on), `.github/workflows/ci.yml` (EXTEND — the macOS addon build step), `apps/desktop/electron-builder.yml` (EXTEND — the addon output path, beside the `asarUnpack` entry T-023r-4-5 adds) **Spec coverage:** Spec-023 §WebAuthn Credential Flow (the ceremony the main process orchestrates); Spec-023 §WebAuthn Platform-Authenticator Native Module (the per-platform strategy, the macOS own-build rationale, and the per-host probe rule) **Verifies invariant:** I-023-1 (PRF output never crosses the bridge; the ceremony resolves an outcome, the derived key stays in main, and `PrfKeyDerivationService` is reachable from no bridge namespace); I-023-15 (capability is probed per host, never inferred from `process.platform`, a platform-probe negative suppresses one arm rather than the ceremony, and a binding that will not load is a negative capability result rather than a crash); I-023-16 (the dispatcher receives control-plane-issued, RP-validated options from the bridge handler and accepts none from a caller); I-023-17 (the dispatcher holds every leg's PRF bytes provisional until the relying party's verdict and zeroizes them on refusal); I-023-18 (a participant cancellation stops the dispatcher rather than advancing to a second arm or the loopback); I-023-20 (one persistent DPoP proof key per installation, generated on first use, held outside the custody root, and reused on every request the issued pair authenticates)
- **T-023r-4-5 — Windows `webauthn.dll` binding.** `webauthn/windows.ts` — the `koffi` declarations for the eight call sites and the seven structs the ceremony needs, `dwVersion` set from the **explicit API-version → maximum-struct-version table** of `Spec-023 §WebAuthn Platform-Authenticator Native Module` — one row per API version 1..9 for `MAKE_CREDENTIAL_OPTIONS`, `GET_ASSERTION_OPTIONS`, `CREDENTIAL_ATTESTATION`, and `WEBAUTHN_ASSERTION`, read from the pinned `webauthn.h` version-history block (`webauthn.h` lines 36–154, the only place in the header where an API version and a struct version appear together) and carried forward per the header's own delta wording. **The `min(desired, supported)` formula this task previously specified is withdrawn (Codex round 4):** it compares numbers drawn from two unrelated sequences, so on a host reporting API 4 or 5 it would send `GET_ASSERTION_OPTIONS` version 4 or 5 — below the version 6 that carries `pHmacSecretSaltValues` — and Windows would silently ignore the PRF request on exactly the builds that can serve it. The table is transcribed into the binding as a frozen lookup with a **positive API-4 fixture** asserting that an API-4 host sends `GET_ASSERTION_OPTIONS` version 6 and reads `WEBAUTHN_ASSERTION` version 3, plus a negative fixture asserting no version below the API-1 baseline row (3 / 4 / 3 / 1) is ever sent. The three PRF floors are read off that table rather than restated, and each is checked against the response member that returns its result at the same API version: assertion-time PRF is `pHmacSecretSaltValues` (`GET_ASSERTION_OPTIONS` v6) with its output on `WEBAUTHN_ASSERTION.pHmacSecret` (v3), **both API 4**; `bEnablePrf` (`MAKE_CREDENTIAL_OPTIONS` v6) with its acknowledgement on `WEBAUTHN_CREDENTIAL_ATTESTATION.bPrfEnabled` (v5), **both API 6**; `pPRFGlobalEval` (`MAKE_CREDENTIAL_OPTIONS` v8), **API 8**. The salt-values count member is `WEBAUTHN_HMAC_SECRET_SALT_VALUES.cCredWithHmacSecretSaltList`, one level down — there is no `cbHmacSecretSaltValues` in the header. Registration requests a **discoverable** credential whenever PRF is wanted, because `webauthn.dll` silently drops `hmac-secret` otherwise and the defect surfaces only at the next assertion. Registration also persists nothing locally: the PRF evaluation input this leg sends is the contract-fixed constant registered in [api-payload-contracts.md](../architecture/contracts/api-payload-contracts.md) (Plan-018 I-018-18, restated at Codex round 6), never a value generated per ceremony and never one taken from a reply, which is guard 3's one-salt-value half. `WEBAUTHN_AUTHENTICATOR_HMAC_SECRET_VALUES_FLAG` is left **unset**, so the OS applies the WebAuthn PRF transform and all three legs share one salt domain. A `FALSE` from `WebAuthNIsUserVerifyingPlatformAuthenticatorAvailable` gates the **platform** arm only and never the ceremony: that call reports on an authenticator built into the device and takes no transport or attachment parameter, so it says nothing about an attached security key, and the roaming attempt proceeds with `dwAuthenticatorAttachment = WEBAUTHN_AUTHENTICATOR_ATTACHMENT_CROSS_PLATFORM`. It is also not an error channel — a negative returns `S_OK` and writes `FALSE`, so the `HRESULT` and the `BOOL` are read separately. **The blocking calls run off the main thread (Codex round 4).** `WebAuthNAuthenticatorMakeCredential` and `WebAuthNAuthenticatorGetAssertion` block until the user answers the OS dialog, so both are invoked through `koffi`'s asynchronous call path — the wrapped function's `async` member, `fn.async(...args, (err, result) => …)`, promisified at the call site because koffi documents no promise form; the native call runs on a worker thread and settles the callback back on the event loop — and never synchronously from Electron main, which would freeze every window for the length of a human interaction and leave no thread to deliver the cancellation on. Cancellation is bound as the two-call pair: `WebAuthNGetCancellationId` before each ceremony, its GUID on the options struct's `pCancellationId`, and `WebAuthNCancelCurrentOperation` taking that GUID, **issued from main while the async call is still pending**. **The binding holds one in-flight slot (Codex round 5, I-023-18)**, taken at dispatch and released when the native call settles — the timeout arm included and the caller's cancellation excluded — so a cancel-then-immediate-retry takes a typed **busy** refusal rather than putting a second call behind the first; the rule is uniform across both `koffi` legs even though only Linux can be harmed by breaking it, because a rule that holds on one leg and not the other is a rule nobody checks. A cancellation is **terminal** here as everywhere: the leg reports the refused arm and the dispatcher stops, rather than routing to `fallback-dag.ts`. Tested with a deliberately blocking stub: main stays responsive while the call is outstanding (a main-process turn completes during it), the cancel settles the pending promise as a refusal rather than hanging to the timeout, and a retry issued while the first call is still outstanding takes the busy refusal and then succeeds once the slot releases. `HRESULT` is read as `int32_t`, `PCWSTR` as `str16`, and the `HWND` is the value inside `getNativeWindowHandle()`; out-structs are allocated with `koffi.alloc()` rather than as JS `Buffer`s — koffi documents only an `alloc`-backed pointer as safe for memory the native side may write later or from another thread, which an async call makes the ordinary case — then copied with `koffi.decode()` and released with `WebAuthNFreeCredentialAttestation` / `WebAuthNFreeAssertion`. **Guard 4 is enforced on the response, not the request (Codex round 3):** `WEBAUTHN_AUTHENTICATOR_GET_ASSERTION_OPTIONS.dwUserVerificationRequirement` is a preference the OS may exceed, so the leg reads bit 2 (`0x04`) of the flags byte in `WEBAUTHN_ASSERTION.pbAuthenticatorData` and compares it with the mode the verification reply returns (re-homed off the options reply at Codex round 5), deriving nothing and taking the fallback on a mismatch — and it returns its PRF bytes to the dispatcher **provisional**, never deriving from them itself (I-023-17); it does **not** demand `WEBAUTHN_USER_VERIFICATION_REQUIREMENT_REQUIRED` universally, which would refuse every non-UV key. **Load failure is a capability result (Codex round 3):** an absent `webauthn.dll` or a `koffi` symbol that will not resolve is caught at the binding's own load boundary, recorded with the library and symbol names, and routed to `fallback-dag.ts` — never rethrown, and never reported as a failed ceremony. This task also **owns the packaging side of the FFI**, because a binding that resolves in development and throws in the packaged app is the same silent failure one layer down: `electron-builder.yml` gains the `asarUnpack` entries for `**/node_modules/@koromix/**` (the koffi platform binary, which lives in a sibling package the smart-unpack heuristic does not reach) and for the in-tree `native/webauthn-darwin/` addon output, and a packaged-build test asserts that **all three** native artifacts resolve from `app.asar.unpacked` rather than from inside the archive — both koffi-backed bindings and the macOS addon, each on its own platform.
