The test runs against a real packaged output, since the failure mode it exists to catch does not reproduce from source. **Files:** `apps/desktop/src/main/webauthn/windows.ts` (CREATE), `apps/desktop/src/main/webauthn/windows.test.ts` (CREATE), `apps/desktop/electron-builder.yml` (EXTEND — the `asarUnpack` entries), `apps/desktop/build/asar-unpack.test.ts` (CREATE — the packaged-load assertion; a `build/**` unit rather than a twelfth E2E spec file, so the §Test count does not move) **Spec coverage:** Spec-023 §WebAuthn Platform-Authenticator Native Module (the Windows leg, guards 1, 3, 4, and 5, the `asarUnpack` constraint, and the per-leg verification protocol) **Verifies invariant:** I-023-15 (capability is probed per host, never inferred from `process.platform`); I-023-17 (PRF bytes leave this leg provisional and are derived from nowhere inside it); I-023-18 (one in-flight slot per binding, released on settlement and not on cancellation; a cancellation is terminal)
- **T-023r-4-6 — Linux `libfido2` binding.** `webauthn/linux.ts` — the `koffi` declarations for the enumeration, capability, and assertion chains against the distribution's `libfido2.so.1`, is **never bundled**. The round-1 fold corrected the rationale this amendment first gave: no `libfido2` package ships udev rules and no supported Debian or Ubuntu release ships `70-u2f.rules` at all, so device access comes from the host's `systemd` / `udev` (≥ 244) descriptor autodetection, which the packaged `.deb` / `.rpm` declares — and that `uaccess` grant is a **seat ACL**, reaching no headless or non-seat session. It builds **both** chains, since a credential must be created before it can be asserted and the round-1 text specified only the assertion half: make-credential is `fido_cred_new` → `set_type` → `set_clientdata_hash` → `set_rp` → `set_user` → `set_extensions(FIDO_EXT_HMAC_SECRET)` — an OR-able `0x01` bitmask, and deliberately **not** `FIDO_EXT_HMAC_SECRET_MC` (`0x20`), which is a different CTAP 2.2 extension — → `set_rk(FIDO_OPT_TRUE)` → `set_uv(<the pinned mode>)` → `fido_dev_make_cred`. **The read-back is the whole attestation response, not a credential id (Codex round 4).** A credential id is not a server-verifiable registration, and `Spec-023 §Main Process Responsibilities` has main submit an attestation for relying-party verification — so this leg composes the WebAuthn `attestationObject` itself, as the three-key CBOR map `{fmt, attStmt, authData}` from `fido_cred_fmt()`, `fido_cred_attstmt_ptr()` / `_len` (already a CBOR map, spliced in as an encoded item), and `fido_cred_authdata_raw_ptr()` / `_raw_len`. **`_raw_` and not `_ptr`:** `fido_cred_authdata_ptr` returns the authenticator data still wrapped in a CBOR byte-string header, which Yubico's own manual states and which the server verifier rejects, while `_raw_` returns the decoded bytes WebAuthn defines. `libfido2` offers no getter for a whole attestation object at all — `fido_cred_set_attobj` is a setter only and no `fido_cred_attobj_ptr` exists — so composing it is the only route. `fido_cred_sig_ptr` / `_len` supplies the signature, `fido_cred_id_ptr` / `_len` the credential id, and a full certificate chain comes from `fido_cred_x5c_list_count()` with `fido_cred_x5c_list_ptr` / `_len` per index — `fido_cred_x5c_ptr` is the **leaf only** and would truncate a chain silently. Client data goes in through `fido_cred_set_clientdata`, which takes the **unhashed** `clientDataJSON` bytes and hashes internally (the form Yubico's manual directs applications to prefer over `_set_clientdata_hash`); the leg keeps its own copy for the server payload, because the library exposes a getter for the hash and none for the client data. Every returned pointer is valid only until the next non-`const` call on the same object, so each is copied immediately. The task's test asserts the serialized registration payload verifies against the T6.2 server verifier, and the serialized assertion payload — `fido_assert_authdata_raw_ptr`, `fido_assert_sig_ptr`, `fido_assert_user_id_ptr`, `fido_assert_id_ptr`, each indexed by `idx` across `fido_assert_count()`, plus the client-data bytes main retained — against T6.3's; the legacy `fido_cred_set_options` / `fido_assert_set_options` pair is exported from `fido.h` but absent from the man pages and is not used. Capability is `fido_dev_get_cbor_info` plus a scan of `fido_cbor_info_extensions_ptr` for the literal `"hmac-secret"`; enumeration alone establishes nothing. **Device access is declared, not assumed.** No `libfido2` package ships udev rules and no supported Debian / Ubuntu release ships `70-u2f.rules` at all, so the packaged `.deb` and `.rpm` declare a dependency on the host stack that actually grants access — `udev (>= 244)` / `systemd-udev` — and never on a rules package; `libu2f-udev` is an empty transitional shell and Fedora's `u2f-hidraw-policy` is retired. **This task owns that declaration, not just the sentence about it (Codex round 6).** Round 5 stated the dependency as a fact about the packaged artifacts and assigned it to no file, which is how a packaging requirement ends up in prose and out of the build: the `deb.depends` / `rpm.depends` entries land in `electron-builder.yml` here, and a packaging-side unit asserts them **out of the built artifacts' own metadata** — `dpkg-deb --field <artifact> Depends` and `rpm -qp --requires <artifact>`, or the equivalent read of the control / header data — rather than by re-reading the YAML the build was configured from, which would assert only that the file says what the file says. The test sits with the other `build/*.test.ts` packaging units and fails on a declaration that is absent, empty, or names a rules package instead of the udev stack. Because that access is a **seat ACL** rather than a permission bit, the leg's zero-devices diagnostic has to name the remedy without over-claiming, and it cannot get the distinction from libfido2: `fido_dev_info_manifest` always returns `FIDO_OK`, and a key the process cannot open is enumerated and then silently discarded when `is_fido()`'s `O_RDWR` open returns `EACCES`. The task therefore builds the discriminator outside the library — enumerate `hidraw` nodes tagged `ID_SECURITY_TOKEN` and test `access(path, R_OK|W_OK)` — so "no key attached" and "key attached, not readable from this session" are different messages, the second naming an inactive or non-seat session (SSH, container, system service) as the cause. The leg applies `SHA-256("WebAuthn PRF" || 0x00 || value)` itself before `fido_assert_set_hmac_salt`, since that call takes the raw CTAP2 salt while Windows and macOS apply the transform for us — one salt domain across all three, or a credential enrolled on one host derives a different key on another. The `value` transformed is the contract-fixed constant registered in [api-payload-contracts.md](../architecture/contracts/api-payload-contracts.md) (Plan-018 I-018-18, restated at Codex round 6 from the round-5 server-minted per-credential form), never one this leg generates and never one taken from a reply: guard 3 makes both the transform and the value identical across legs, and only both together make the derived key reproducible. **Guard 4 is enforced on the response (Codex round 3):** `fido_assert_set_uv` states what the platform asked for, so after `fido_dev_get_assert` the leg reads bit 2 (`0x04`) of the flags byte at `fido_assert_authdata_raw_ptr` — **`_raw_`, corrected at Codex round 4**, since `fido_assert_authdata_ptr` returns the CBOR-byte-string-wrapped form and the bit offset would be wrong by the width of that header — and compares it with the **server-held** mode the T6.3 authentication-**verify** reply carries (re-homed off the options reply at Codex round 5, since a discoverable sign-in names no credential in advance), deriving nothing and routing to `fallback-dag.ts` on a mismatch rather than under the other `CredRandom`. Its PRF bytes leave the leg **provisional** and are derived from nowhere inside it (I-023-17). **Load failure is a capability result (Codex round 3):** a host with no `libfido2.so.1`, or one whose library resolves none of the declared symbols, is caught at the load boundary, recorded with the library and symbol names, and routed to the fallback — this is the most likely of the three legs to hit it, since the library is the distribution's and is never bundled. **Transports are stated exactly, not implied:** `fido_dev_info_manifest` runs the HID backend unconditionally and the NFC / PC/SC backends under `NFC_LINUX` and `USE_PCSC`, which both default ON for Linux, so a default host build reaches USB HID and NFC; there is **no BLE backend under any flag**, so a BLE-only key routes to `fallback-dag.ts` and this task adds no BLE transport (Codex round 2). **The blocking call runs off the main thread, and cancellation is bounded rather than interrupted (Codex round 4).** `fido_dev_make_cred` and `fido_dev_get_assert` are documented synchronous and blocking, so both are invoked through `koffi`'s asynchronous call path (`fn.async(...args, callback)`, promisified at the call site) and never synchronously from Electron main. Cancellation is deliberately **not** the Windows shape: `fido_dev_cancel` exists and is exported, but its whole documentation is one sentence on a page it shares with `fido_dev_open`, it is omitted from that page's own return-value section despite returning `int`, `libfido2` publishes no thread-safety statement anywhere in its manual pages or README, and its only in-tree call site is a single-threaded cleanup loop over devices other than the one in use — so cancelling a call blocked on the same device handle from another thread is undocumented behaviour and this task does not adopt it. The leg instead calls `fido_dev_set_timeout` before dispatch, which is documented to stop the library blocking longer than the given milliseconds and to fail with `FIDO_ERR_RX`; a participant cancel settles the ceremony at the main-process boundary and the bounded native call is left to reach its own end, with the device handle closed only after it settles and never while it is in flight. The residual is stated rather than hidden: a Linux cancel is immediate for the caller and the key may keep waiting for touch until the bound expires. **That residual is exactly why this leg holds a single-flight slot (Codex round 5, I-023-18).** Because the caller settles before the native call does, a cancel-then-immediate-retry would otherwise dispatch a second `fido_dev_get_assert` against a `fido_dev_t` the first call still owns — two concurrent calls on one device handle, which the same absent thread-safety statement that ruled out `fido_dev_cancel` rules out here too. The binding takes a per-binding slot at dispatch and releases it **only when the native callback settles, the timeout arm included and the participant's cancellation excluded**; a ceremony requested while the slot is held is refused with a typed **busy** result, which is neither a probe result nor a failed ceremony, so the shell can say the key is still finishing the previous request. A cancellation is also **terminal**: this leg reports the refused arm and the dispatcher stops, rather than falling through to `fallback-dag.ts`. Tested with a deliberately blocking stub — main stays responsive while the call is outstanding, the bound settles it, no `fido_dev_cancel` is issued against an in-flight call, and, driving the arm rather than only the timeout, a **cancel followed immediately by a retry** takes the busy refusal while the first call is still outstanding and then succeeds once the slot releases. Roaming FIDO2 keys only: Linux exposes no platform authenticator, and this task claims none. **Files:** `apps/desktop/src/main/webauthn/linux.ts` (CREATE), `apps/desktop/src/main/webauthn/linux.test.ts` (CREATE), `apps/desktop/electron-builder.yml` (EXTEND — the `deb.depends` / `rpm.depends` entry declaring `udev (>= 244)` on Debian-family and `systemd-udev` on RPM-family, the third task to extend this file after T-023r-4-3's addon output path and T-023r-4-5's `asarUnpack` entry), `apps/desktop/build/linux-package-deps.test.ts` (CREATE — the artifact-metadata assertion) **Spec coverage:** Spec-023 §WebAuthn Platform-Authenticator Native Module (the Linux leg, guards 2, 3, and 4, and the per-leg verification protocol) **Verifies invariant:** I-023-15 (capability is probed per host, never inferred from `process.platform`); I-023-17 (PRF bytes leave this leg provisional and are derived from nowhere inside it); I-023-18 (the per-binding slot is held to the native callback, the busy refusal is typed, and a cancellation is terminal)
- **T-023r-4-7 — Password-derived KEK custody on the no-PRF path.** The DAG loopback reauthenticates the participant; it does **not** reproduce a wrapping key, and `ADR-010 §Consequences` requires the desktop's long-term Ed25519 identity key to be derived or wrapped from a PRF ceremony. Without this leg a host that reaches §Fallback Behavior signs in and then cannot unwrap its identity key, so every E2EE session it holds becomes unjoinable — a silent capability loss rather than a refusal. `webauthn/kek-fallback.ts` implements the disposition ADR-010's own risk row already decided — a **password-derived KEK for unsupported devices** — reusing `ADR-021`'s ratified primitives rather than minting a second scheme: Argon2id at the OWASP 2026 parameters through `@noble/hashes`, XChaCha20-Poly1305 through `@noble/ciphers`, the wrapped blob in the OS keystore beside the refresh token, and the passphrase never persisted and never logged. **The task also owns how main obtains the passphrase, because nothing in the corpus gave it a way (Codex round 2).** The main process has no text-entry surface and Electron's native dialogs have none either — `dialog.showMessageBox` offers buttons and a checkbox and no input field — so without this leg the KEK path is specified and unreachable. Main opens a **secret-entry window** on the I-023-12 pattern: its own hardened `BrowserWindow` built through the same private `constructLockedWindow` that already owns the locked `webPreferences` literal (so `assert-webprefs.ts` covers it with no second block), its own preload and its own bridge instance, no shared in-memory store, loaded from the packaged bundle over the renderer scheme at a `secret-entry` window route. That bridge exposes **exactly one write-only operation**, which delivers the entered passphrase to main once and resolves `void` — no echo, no read accessor, no derived key, no getter of any kind — after which main closes the window, derives the KEK, and zeroizes the transferred buffer. It is deliberately **not** a `SidekicksBridge` namespace, since a namespace on the shared object would be callable from every session renderer, and deliberately **not** one of the two auxiliary windows: it hosts no pane, subscribes to nothing, is opened by main rather than from the menu bar, and takes no entry in the auxiliary route registry, so that set stays at two (`Spec-023 §Preload Bridge Contract`, the secret-entry paragraph). **No E2EE feature is refused on this path**; the host is a full participant whose KEK has a different provenance, and that provenance is surfaced so the degradation is visible rather than silent. **What this KEK wraps is a copy of the custody root, not the envelopes (Codex round 6, I-023-19):** the passphrase path is one more wrapper over the same installation-scoped key that every credential's KEK wraps, which is what makes a host that later gains PRF a re-wrap of one 32-byte copy rather than a re-encryption of every envelope it holds. A host that declines to set a passphrase is the one case that does degrade, and it degrades to the `Spec-023 §Fallback Behavior` keystore rule already written. **It also builds the reverse-RPC transport and registers the `shell.deriveKek` handler (Codex round 4; the transport assigned to files at round 5).** Round 4 named the method and left every module that carries it unowned, so this task takes them: `packages/contracts/src/desktop/shell-rpc.ts` holds the envelope plus the `ShellDeriveKekRequest` / `ShellDeriveKekResponse` schemas both processes parse from; `apps/desktop/src/main/shell-rpc.ts` is the main-side dispatcher — a method table keyed by registered name, request-id correlation with a pending map and a bounded timeout that settles an orphaned request instead of leaking it, the supervised-child port-identity assertion, and refusal of any unregistered name; and `daemon-supervisor.ts` is extended to hand the `MessagePortMain` to that dispatcher at fork and revoke it on exit, so no dispatcher outlives its child. The daemon-side caller is Plan-022's own `packages/runtime-daemon/src/crypto/shell-rpc-client.ts`, named on that plan's T22.1.3. The single registered method's implementation is the injected `PrfKeyDerivationService` (CP-023-9): the daemon's PRF branch requests a KEK carrying **only** Plan-022's HKDF salt (the round-5 second member, the server-minted PRF evaluation input, is removed at round 6 — it is a contract-fixed constant the shell already holds, and the daemon never had a credential row to read a per-credential one from), **main runs the ceremony and the HKDF-SHA-256 itself** under the contract-fixed info string `"ai-sidekicks/daemon-master/v1"`, and only the finished 32-byte KEK crosses that one private channel — no authenticator-derived secret rides the RPC, which would hand it to the process that does not own it. **The HKDF input is the custody root of T-023r-4-8, not raw PRF output (Codex round 6):** a KEK derived straight from one credential's PRF bytes would change the moment the participant enrolled or removed an authenticator, and the daemon cannot re-wrap what it can no longer decrypt, so deriving from the installation-scoped root is what makes this key survive an enrolment at all. The handler asserts the receiving port is the supervised child's and drops anything else — a local assertion, not a wire refusal, so no error code is minted. **Tests:** a duplex round-trip over a real `MessagePort` pair — request, correlation, response, an unmatched response id settling nothing, and an unregistered method name refused — driven end to end rather than against a mocked channel, since a correlation defect is invisible to a test that stubs the transport; a cross-process **wrap → restart → unwrap** vector proving a blob wrapped under a KEK derived through `shell.deriveKek` reopens after the daemon restarts, and a second arm proving it still reopens after a **second authenticator is enrolled** — the pair is what makes the custody root and the fixed info string load-bearing rather than incidental, since a derivation taken straight from PRF output passes the first arm and fails the second; the one-op bridge shape (the exposed object has exactly one own key, it is a function, and no accessor or method on it returns the value back — asserted against the built preload rather than against a hand-written stand-in); the no-echo guarantee (a second delivery after the window closes is refused, and neither the passphrase nor any prefix of it appears in the window's IPC replies, in the main-process log sink, or in a crash-report payload); and zeroization (the transferred buffer is overwritten once the KEK is derived, asserted on the buffer main actually holds). **Files:** `apps/desktop/src/main/webauthn/kek-fallback.ts` (CREATE), `apps/desktop/src/main/webauthn/kek-fallback.test.ts` (CREATE), `apps/desktop/src/main/shell-rpc.ts` (CREATE — the main-side dispatcher), `apps/desktop/src/main/shell-rpc.test.ts` (CREATE — the duplex round-trip and the wrap/restart/unwrap vector), `packages/contracts/src/desktop/shell-rpc.ts` (CREATE — the envelope and the `ShellDeriveKek` request/response schemas), `apps/desktop/src/main/daemon-supervisor.ts` (EXTEND — hand the `MessagePortMain` to the dispatcher at fork, revoke on exit), `apps/desktop/src/main/webauthn/passphrase-prompt.ts` (CREATE — the open / await-single-delivery / close / zeroize coordinator), `apps/desktop/src/main/webauthn/passphrase-prompt.test.ts` (CREATE), `apps/desktop/src/main/window.ts` (EXTEND — `createSecretEntryWindow()` beside the existing two factories, through the same private `constructLockedWindow`), `apps/desktop/src/preload/secret-entry.ts` (CREATE — the one-op preload, a separate entry point and not a `SidekicksBridge` namespace), `apps/desktop/src/renderer/src/secret-entry/` (CREATE — the route body), `apps/desktop/electron.vite.config.ts` (EXTEND — the second preload entry) **Spec coverage:** Spec-023 §Fallback Behavior (the no-PRF host's identity-key custody and where the passphrase comes from); Spec-023 §Preload Bridge Contract (the secret-entry window and its single write-only operation); Spec-023 §Trust Stance (the passphrase and the derived KEK are main-process-only) **Verifies invariant:** I-023-1 (neither the passphrase nor the derived KEK crosses the bridge in the returning direction, and no session-window bridge can reach the operation at all); I-023-2 (the secret-entry window carries the locked `webPreferences` block and loads over the custom protocol); I-023-12 (it shares no store with any other window); I-023-19 (the passphrase-derived KEK wraps a copy of the custody root and never an envelope, so a host that later gains PRF re-wraps one copy)
- **T-023r-4-8 — Custody root key.** The installation-scoped key every wrapping path wraps a copy of, rather than each path wrapping the envelopes directly. `webauthn/custody-root.ts` owns its whole lifecycle: **mint** 32 bytes from `crypto.randomBytes` the first time a **verified** ceremony on this installation finds no local root — enrolment or sign-in, checked on every verified ceremony and performed at most once (Codex round 7). Scoping the mint to enrolment, as the round-6 text did, left the ordinary fresh-install case rootless: a participant whose discoverable credential is already synced to the machine signs in without ever enrolling locally, so an enrolment-only trigger never fires and the issued token pair has nothing to be wrapped under. Then: **wrap** one copy per wrapping key — each enrolled credential's PRF-derived KEK and, on a host that took T-023r-4-7's path, the passphrase-derived one — through the same `ADR-021` XChaCha20-Poly1305 primitives that plan already uses, minting no second scheme; **store** each wrapped copy through `keystore.ts` (T-023r-4-1) under a `custody-root/credential/<credentialId>` name, with the fallback path's copy at `custody-root/passphrase`; **unwrap** on sign-in with whichever KEK the ceremony produced; and **revoke** by calling the relying party's `WebAuthnCredentialRevoke` procedure (Plan-018 T6.5) **and** deleting that credential's local copy, in that order. **The server call is the load-bearing half (Codex round 7):** deleting the local copy only stops _this_ installation from deriving through the lost authenticator, while the WebAuthn routes are otherwise reachable without a session — so a credential that remains live in `webauthn_credentials` can still complete the unauthenticated verification from any other installation and be issued a fresh token pair. Local deletion alone is therefore not revocation; it is cleanup. If the server call fails the local copy is kept and the removal is reported as failed, because a UI that says "removed" over a credential the relying party still honours is the worse of the two outcomes. Nothing else in the desktop holds a wrapping key that touches an envelope: the refresh-token envelope and the long-term Ed25519 identity-key envelope are wrapped under the root exactly once, at mint time, and are never rewritten by an enrolment or a removal. **Why the indirection exists (Codex round 6).** A KEK derived from one credential's PRF output is that credential's alone, so wrapping the envelopes directly under it means a second enrolled authenticator opens nothing — and the obvious repair, re-wrapping every envelope when the second is enrolled, needs a live ceremony on the **old** credential at exactly the moment a participant enrolling a backup key is trying to stop depending on it. Wrapping a copy of one root instead makes enrolment a 32-byte write and removal a 32-byte delete, both touching no envelope. **What it does not do**, stated because the absence is the design and not an oversight: there is no server-side escrow copy, because an escrow the server can open is a key the server holds and `Spec-023 §Trust Stance` puts none there — so losing every enrolled credential loses the root and everything under it. And the root is **per installation**: a reinstall mints a new one and re-enrols its identity key through the ordinary `verified → bound` flow, which is why `Spec-023 §WebAuthn Credential Flow` step 6 has the ceremony's verification reply **issue** a token pair rather than unwrap a stored one. **Tests:** a mint-once assertion (a second successful ceremony on an installation that already has a root wraps a copy and does not mint a second root); a **synced-credential cold-install** vector — a verified sign-in through an already-enrolled credential, with no local enrolment anywhere in the run, mints the root and wraps the issued refresh token under it (Codex round 7, the case the enrolment-only trigger missed); a **revocation** vector — removing a credential calls `WebAuthnCredentialRevoke` before deleting the local copy, and a failed server call leaves the copy in place and reports the removal as failed; an **enrol-second-authenticator** vector — wrap an envelope, enrol a second credential, and open that same envelope through the second credential's KEK with the ciphertext byte-unchanged, which is the property the whole task exists for and the one a direct-wrap design fails; a **remove-a-credential** vector — deleting one credential's copy leaves the other credential's sign-in working and the envelopes untouched; a **lose-everything** negative control asserting that with no copy present the root is unrecoverable and the failure is a typed refusal rather than a silent empty key (the guard-list shape); and a fallback-path arm proving the passphrase-derived KEK wraps a copy of the _same_ root, so a host that later gains PRF re-wraps one copy rather than re-encrypting anything. **Files:** `apps/desktop/src/main/webauthn/custody-root.ts` (CREATE), `apps/desktop/src/main/webauthn/custody-root.test.ts` (CREATE) **Spec coverage:** Spec-023 §WebAuthn Platform-Authenticator Native Module (the custody-root rule and its two stated consequences); Spec-023 §Shell ↔ OS Keystore (the custody-root entries and where they live) **Verifies invariant:** I-023-19 (envelopes are wrapped under the root and never under a credential's KEK; each credential's KEK wraps only a copy; enrolment and removal touch no envelope)
- **T-023r-4-4 — Device Authorization Grant fallback.** `webauthn/fallback-dag.ts` — an `http.createServer()` bound to `'127.0.0.1'` (explicitly not `'localhost'`, avoiding the IPv6 `::1` DNS ambiguity RFC 8252 §7.3 scopes) on OS-assigned port `0`; one-shot callback acceptance; `AbortSignal.timeout(300_000)`; PKCE S256 via `oauth4webapi` v3.8.5, with `plain` rejected at authorize-URL construction time. **Files:** `apps/desktop/src/main/webauthn/fallback-dag.ts` (CREATE), `apps/desktop/src/main/webauthn/fallback-dag.test.ts` (CREATE) **Spec coverage:** Spec-023 §Fallback Behavior (the DAG loopback is the per-host degradation for a probe that finds no usable authenticator or no PRF, on any of the three platforms — never a per-platform assignment); Spec-023 §Trust Stance (the ceremony is main-process work; the renderer never sees the code or verifier) **Verifies invariant:** I-023-1 (neither the code verifier nor the exchanged token crosses the bridge)

### Phase 5 — Auto-Updater And Deep-Link Handler

**Goal:** Implementation Steps 11 and 13 — the two surfaces that mutate the installed application or admit an external URL.

**Preconditions.** Phase 4 must have merged (the updater persists its state through the keystore-adjacent config path and the deep-link exchange presents Plan-018 credentials). Consumes Plan-008's control-plane client for both `invite.preview` and `acceptInvite`, and is gated on Plan-002 Phase 2: the non-consuming `invite.preview` read the confirmation surface calls before acceptance is declared at T2.6–T2.8 and not yet shipped, and the deep-link flow must not ship a confirmation UI that burns a single-use invite to render it. That `plan_phase` entry replaces the `{ type: bl_closed, ref: 133 }` entry this phase carried until 2026-08-25 and is strictly stronger: it fails closed on the unshipped producing tasks rather than on a backlog status.

<!-- prettier-ignore -->
```yaml
preconditions:
  - { type: plan_phase, plan: 023, phase: 4, status: merged }
  - { type: precondition_box_checked, box: "(Tier 8 remainder only.)** Plan-008 exposes a tRPC v11 client" }
  - { type: precondition_box_checked, box: "(Tier 8 remainder only.)** Spec-002 / Plan-002 expose the" }
  - { type: plan_phase, plan: 002, phase: 2, status: merged }
```

#### Tasks

- **T-023r-5-1 — Auto-update state machine and rollback.** `updater.ts` — `electron-updater` v6.8.9 on a 4-hour cadence plus startup plus explicit request. Before a verified staged update applies, retain the prior version: copy the running version's rollback source — per platform, the previously-downloaded artifact for the running version from the shell-owned update-artifact cache when present, else the installed application itself (macOS `.app` bundle; Windows installed directory; Linux `AppImage`) — into `<app-user-data>/retained-versions/` (bounded to one prior version) and record its version, retained-copy path, integrity hash in `updater-state.json`. Retention failure refuses the update fail-closed — apply aborted, installed version keeps running, refusal logged and re-surfaced — because an in-place apply overwrites what the live executable path points to, so a rollback source recorded as that path would already be the new version by the time rollback needs it. After relaunch, wait for `DaemonHello`; on 10s timeout or version mismatch, verify the retained copy against its recorded integrity hash and re-install from it — the retained copy is the sole rollback source; the live executable path never is. A confirmed-healthy handshake deletes the retained copy and completes the update; the rollback path never deletes it. Rollback is a one-way ratchet — the failed version enters `rollback_blacklist` and is not re-attempted automatically. The rollback machine is **authored here**, not an `electron-updater` feature. **Files:** `apps/desktop/src/main/updater.ts` (CREATE), `apps/desktop/src/main/updater.test.ts` (CREATE) **Spec coverage:** Spec-023 §Auto-Update Flow (staged apply; post-apply handshake; rollback on failure); Spec-023 §Auto-Updater (rollback is architecture this plan must build; prior-version artifact retention is the mechanism selected from that section's named options) **Verifies invariant:** I-023-1 (updater state persisted to disk — the `updater-state.json` retained-version record and the retained copy itself — carries no auth material)
- **T-023r-5-2 — Update trust-chain verification.** `updater-public-key.ts` committing the Ed25519 verification key as a compile-time `Uint8Array` constant, plus the two-leg verification chain in `updater.ts`. Leg 1, at check time: fetch the platform's `latest-*.yml` together with its sibling `manifest.sig` (both produced by T-023r-7-4) and verify the Ed25519 signature over the exact manifest bytes against the pinned key — an unverifiable manifest is discarded and logged, the cycle is skipped, and nothing from it (versions, URLs, hashes) is honored. Leg 2, on `update-downloaded`: match the downloaded artifact's hash against the authenticated manifest's per-file artifact-hash entry (`sha512` in the `electron-updater` v6.8.9 manifest schema) before staging — a mismatch discards the artifact, logs, and refuses the update. The trust chain is pinned key → signed manifest → hash-bound artifact: no per-artifact signature exists, and `electron-updater`'s own internal checksum pass is not the security boundary, because its manifest fetch is not authenticated against this key. Both legs are fail-closed with no state mutation on refusal. The verifier is authorable and unit-testable in this phase against test manifests and test key material; the live signed feed rides Phase 7 (T-023r-7-4). Key rotation is a build-time change. This key is deliberately distinct from the code-signing certificate, conflating them being a named rotation-mishap vector. **Files:** `apps/desktop/src/main/updater-public-key.ts` (CREATE), `apps/desktop/src/main/updater.ts` (EXTEND), `apps/desktop/src/main/update-signature.test.ts` (CREATE) **Spec coverage:** Spec-023 §Shell ↔ Auto-Update Feed (the manifest carries the artifact hash and signature; Ed25519 signature algorithm); Spec-023 §Auto-Update Flow (verification precedes staging; a failed verification discards, logs, and re-attempts next cycle); Spec-023 §Pitfalls To Avoid (update-signing key and code-signing cert are distinct) **Verifies invariant:** I-023-3 (the signing-key separation is the same discipline the packaging order enforces on the other side of the release)
- **T-023r-5-3 — Deep-link handler.** `deep-link.ts` — `app.setAsDefaultProtocolClient('sidekicks')`; `open-url` on macOS and `second-instance` on Windows / Linux. URL parse, token extraction, the `invite.preview` metadata read, and the `controlPlane.call('acceptInvite', { token })` exchange all happen in the main process; the renderer receives only the display metadata, the opaque pending-invite reference, and the post-exchange session capability, and the raw token is memory-discarded after exchange. The handler **never** reaches the exchange without an intervening confirmation: it calls the non-consuming preview first (so displaying invite details never burns the single-use invite), hands off to T-023r-5-5's pending-reference surface, and issues `acceptInvite` only on a confirmation resolving a live reference. **Files:** `apps/desktop/src/main/deep-link.ts` (CREATE), `apps/desktop/src/main/deep-link.test.ts` (CREATE) **Spec coverage:** Spec-023 §Deep-Link Invite Flow (main-process-only token handling; the non-consuming preview before acceptance); Spec-023 §Acceptance Criteria (the raw invite token never crosses the bridge) **Verifies invariant:** I-023-5 (the token appears in no bridge-transcript event and in no renderer-visible value), I-023-9 (the handler's own control flow is what makes the preview-then-confirm ordering hold — there is no path from parse to exchange that skips the confirmation)
- **T-023r-5-4 — Deep-link parser adversarial arms.** The §Adversarial-Tampering Boundary canonicalization-round-trip and empty-segment arms, on the plan's only untrusted-input parser: a `sidekicks://` URL must survive parse → normalize → re-serialize with no action or argument reinterpretation, and `sidekicks://invite/` (empty token) plus `sidekicks://invite//<token>` (empty leading segment) must both be **rejected at the parser boundary**, never coerced into a valid action. Percent-encoded separators and trailing separators are covered by the same arm. **Files:** `apps/desktop/src/main/deep-link-parser-boundary.test.ts` (CREATE) **Spec coverage:** Spec-023 §Deep-Link Invite Flow (the `sidekicks://<action>/<...args>` grammar the parser must hold exactly); Spec-023 §Trust Stance (an inbound URL is untrusted input regardless of which OS event delivered it) **Verifies invariant:** I-023-5 (a reinterpreted or empty-segment URL must not reach the exchange path at all, which is what keeps the token-containment guarantee total)
- **T-023r-5-5 — Pending-invite preview, reference lifecycle, and confirmation handoff.** The main-process half of `Spec-023 §Deep-Link Invite Flow` steps 3–6, split from T-023r-5-3's parsing half. After the parse, `deep-link.ts` issues the anonymous non-consuming `invite.preview` over the same main-process control-plane client `bridge/control-plane.ts` uses for `acceptInvite` (CP-023-5) — never the daemon gateway, never a local token decode. **On success** it mints a pending-invite reference — an opaque, single-use, TTL-bounded handle carrying no token-derived material, held in a main-process map that is the only place it resolves — and emits it through `bridge/invite.ts`'s `subscribePending` channel beside `{sessionId, joinMode, expiresAt, sessionName, inviterDisplayName}`. **On a typed refusal** (`invite.not_found`, `invite.expired`, `invite.revoked`, `invite.already_accepted` — the last three at their `error-contracts §Invite` statuses, `expired` and `revoked` lifted to 410 by the contracts-level status-override map) it mints **no reference at all** and emits a refusal-shaped prompt, so no confirmable state exists for an invite that cannot be accepted. **A transport failure is a third, distinct outcome** — an unreachable or erroring control plane is neither a valid invite nor a typed refusal, so it surfaces a retryable could-not-verify state and must never fall through to the exchange. `confirmPending` resolves the reference and drives the accept; `dismissPending` releases it; an unknown, consumed, or expired reference is refused rather than re-resolved through the `reference-invalid` outcome arm, and the TTL sweep discards the confined token with the entry. **The reference is consumed when acceptance is issued, not when confirmation is requested** — which is what makes the authentication detour possible. That gap is closed by an in-flight state rather than left open: `confirmPending` is **atomically single-shot**, transitioning the entry to `authenticating` in the same step that resolves it, and a second `confirmPending` against an entry already in flight is refused through `reference-invalid`'s `consumed` arm rather than starting a second ceremony. Without that transition a double click — or a compromised renderer replaying the handle it holds — would launch concurrent WebAuthn ceremonies and race two acceptances against one invite, which is exactly the single-use posture main exists to enforce. The entry keeps its confined token and its original deadline across the transition. `invite.preview` is anonymous, but `acceptInvite` is authenticated: when main holds no access credential at confirm time, it emits the `authentication-required` outcome, drives the Plan-018 authentication surface (`Spec-002 §Invite Delivery` requires prompting the recipient before acceptance), and **preserves the pending reference across that transition** carrying its **original absolute deadline, never re-armed** — `min(mint + 10 minutes, expiresAt)` is the bound T-023r-2-5 pins and I-023-10 promises, so extending it to cover a slow authentication would hold the confined token past both and make the lifecycle contradict its own wire contract. A recipient whose deadline lapses mid-authentication returns to the `reference-invalid` `expired` arm rather than a silently extended handle; abandoning authentication releases the reference exactly as `dismissPending` would **and emits the terminal `authentication-failed` outcome in the same step** — releasing without emitting is the stranding case, so the release and the emission are one action, not two. `retryPending(attempt)` re-drives the preview for the URL its `attempt` handle names — main keys each failed deep link by that handle precisely so a retry cannot select the wrong one when two protocol URLs are outstanding. It mints no reference of its own and **cannot confirm**: `attempt` is a separate brand that `confirmPending` does not accept, and every path out of a retry re-enters this same three-outcome emission. **One `already_accepted` is not a refusal: the ambiguous accept.** If an acceptance was issued for a reference and its response was lost in transit, the membership may already exist server-side; a re-driven preview then answers `invite.already_accepted`, terminal by the `Spec-002 §Interfaces And Contracts` ladder yet wrong for this caller, who is the very participant that accepted. Main therefore records that it issued the acceptance for a reference, and on that one code from that one reference it **reconciles rather than refuses**: it reads the target session's membership for its own principal over the same control-plane client, emits `joined` when the membership is present, and the terminal refusal only when it is not. A reference this process never issued an acceptance against is unaffected — `already_accepted` stays a refusal there, since it means somebody else spent the invite. All terminal results reach the renderer through `subscribeOutcome`. **Files:** `apps/desktop/src/main/deep-link.ts` (EXTEND), `apps/desktop/src/main/bridge/invite.ts` (EXTEND), `apps/desktop/src/main/pending-invite.test.ts` (CREATE) **Spec coverage:** Spec-023 §Deep-Link Invite Flow (steps 3-6: the non-consuming resolution, the opaque-reference emission, the confirmation signal, the membership event); Spec-023 §Trust Stance (the renderer drives confirmation while holding no token and no authority) **Verifies invariant:** I-023-9 (the refusal arm is what makes no-acceptance-without-confirmation total rather than incidental — there is no reference to confirm), I-023-10 (the reference's opacity, single use, TTL bound, and main-only resolution), I-023-5 (the token stays confined across all three outcomes) **Consumes:** `invite.preview({token})` ← Plan-002 Phase 2 (T2.6/T2.7, over the main-process control-plane client per CP-023-5); `acceptInvite(token)` ← Plan-008 Phase 3 (the shipped authenticated accept this leg drives after confirmation); the Plan-018 authentication surface ← Plan-018 (the `authentication-required` detour)

### Phase 6 — Renderer Shell, Router, And Composer

**Goal:** Implementation Step 14 as reduced by §Renderer — `App.tsx` routing plus the `renderer/src/shell/` layout and composer subtree. This phase authors **no feature-view body**; each Signature Feature view is created by its owning plan in that plan's own subtree and mounted here.

**Preconditions.** Phase 5 must have merged. The router's parameterized subscriptions need the deferred request-param channel, and the composer's typing producer leg needs Plan-002 T3.5's daemon presence update path — the two boxes those name gate this phase. The invite-confirmation surface T-023r-6-3 mounts consumes the same `invite.preview` producer Phase 5 does, so the invite-metadata box and its `plan_phase` pair are carried here too: the preflight auto-walk soft-skips ineligible phases, so a box entry gates only the phases that declare it. Phase 1C must also have merged: T-023r-6-1 adopts the console by mount-and-move, and a phase cannot adopt a tree that does not exist, so the supplement is a formal `external_plan_phase_merged` entry here too (Codex round 3; the label-aware spelling per the Phase-3 note).

```yaml
  - { type: precondition_box_checked, box: "(Tier 8 remainder only.)** Spec-002 / Plan-002 expose the" }

```

#### Tasks

- **T-023r-6-1 — Router and shell chrome.** `App.tsx` grows from the Tier-1 placeholder into the top-level router plus layout shell; `renderer/src/shell/` holds the chrome. Each Signature Feature view is mounted as a route against the owning plan's own subtree — this task creates a mount point and a route, never a view body, and a diff that adds a `renderer/src/features/` tree is a review rejection. Where an owning plan's view has not landed, the route renders a declared not-yet-available placeholder rather than a local reimplementation. **Files:** `apps/desktop/src/renderer/src/App.tsx` (EXTEND), `apps/desktop/src/renderer/src/shell/AppShell.tsx` (CREATE), `apps/desktop/src/renderer/src/shell/index.ts` (CREATE), `apps/desktop/src/renderer/src/shell/__tests__/AppShell.test.tsx` (CREATE) **Spec coverage:** Spec-023 §Signature Feature Composition Sketches (each sketch names its owning plan; this plan composes rather than implements); Spec-023 §Renderer Responsibilities (the renderer is a thin projection over the bridge) **Verifies invariant:** I-023-8 (the shell subtree imports nothing under `src/main/` or `src/preload/` and no Node built-in)
- **T-023r-6-1 EXTEND (2026-09-01 — adopting the console).** By the time this task dispatches, the console subtree §Target Areas Renderer registers is in-tree from Phase 1C and is already the app frame `App.tsx` mounts. This task therefore **adopts rather than re-authors**: the router is the console's route table (the three rail destinations, the session workspace, the settings pages, and the two auxiliary-window routes), the layout chrome is the console's icon rail plus workspace, and `shell/AppShell.tsx` becomes the thin host that mounts the console and the plan-owned subtrees into it — a diff that re-implements a console surface under `shell/`, or moves a console file to re-own it, is a review rejection, as is a `renderer/src/features/` tree. Where an owning plan's view has not landed, the console renders that pane kind's declared not-yet-available state (`Spec-023 §Console Design (Meridian)`, the _not loaded_ kind of nothing), never a local reimplementation. **Files:** the same, with `AppShell.tsx` reduced to the host. **Tests:** the console's architecture tier (no second mount door per pane kind; no import into a plan-owned subtree except the absorbed components) runs against the adopted tree, and the scenario manifest's live-status test passes with every row of `§Console growth slate` that has since landed marked live.
- **T-023r-6-2 — Session composer and typing producer leg.** `shell/MessageComposer.tsx` — the composer's pinned home, in this plan's shell subtree and never inside a feature-view tree owned by Plan-013 or Plan-016. It drives `activity.typing` set / refresh / clear **through the daemon presence handler surface** Plan-002 T3.5 ships, never by direct Awareness injection (CP-023-4), and it does not route around the daemon-side membership-restricted-channel suppression — a composer that publishes for a restricted channel is a review rejection even if the daemon would drop it. The composer is **authored at this pinned home by Phase 1C** (T-023p-1C-3, 2026-09-01) — the send router, target chip, posture chip, meters slot, and discovery-only autocomplete `Spec-023 §Signature Feature Composition Sketches` specifies, mounted into the console workspace's composer slot, the console never carrying a second composer — and this task is its **live-wiring leg**: it wires the router's run-queue create and steer-intervention calls and the typing producer (CP-023-4) live at that home and re-authors nothing. **Files:** `apps/desktop/src/renderer/src/shell/MessageComposer.tsx` (EXTEND — created by T-023p-1C-3), `apps/desktop/src/renderer/src/shell/__tests__/MessageComposer.test.tsx` (CREATE) **Spec coverage:** Spec-023 §Renderer Responsibilities (composition surfaces call the bridge; they hold no authority); Spec-023 §Signature Feature Composition Sketches (the composer is shell chrome shared across views, not a view body) **Verifies invariant:** I-023-8 (the composer reaches presence only through `window.sidekicks`, which is what makes the never-inject-Awareness-directly rule mechanically true rather than a convention)
- **T-023r-6-2 EXTEND (2026-08-29 — the console-parity composer affordances).** The same composer additionally hosts the Session Composer surface under `Spec-023 §Signature Feature Composition Sketches`: the provider command-and-skill autocomplete it **authors** (reading `driver.listProviderCommands` per CP-023-8, keyed on the target agent's `(driverName, providerAccountId)` and re-read rather than filtered when the target changes), and the composer-area **mount points** for the context-window meter, the rate-limit indicator, and the participant-triggered compaction control — all three authored by Plan-013 in its own `usage-meters/` subtree, so the shell mounts and never authors, exactly as it already does for the meter. **Two round-3 rules bind this surface (2026-08-29).** The declared state is **absent until observed** — the handshake carrying it is turn-bearing, so a freshly spawned binding has none — and the composer renders that as **not yet observed**, never as the mode being off and never by falling back to the requested value, which would reproduce at the surface exactly the false success the split exists to prevent. And `listProviderCommands` returns **one group per live binding**, each naming its run and `(driverName, providerAccountId)`: the composer selects the group for the binding its target agent is composing against and **never flattens the groups into one list**, since a flattened list is a list whose entries have lost the routing invariant that says which agent may be offered them. **The output-speed control is authored here** (round-2 fold, 2026-08-29), unlike those three, because the Session Composer sub-sketch is this plan's own and the control composes only surfaces already registered elsewhere: it reads the target agent's `outputSpeed` and the live-scoped `observedOutputSpeed` from `agent.list`, offers the choices from that driver's published `outputSpeedLevels` on the `driver.listCapabilities` read the other affordances already take, and dispatches `agent.configUpdate` — no new verb, no new subtree, and no obligation minted, the members and the vocabulary being Plan-016's and Plan-005's to define. It renders **both** values, never one folded into the other, with the provider's own reason beside them where the provider supplied one; where no declared state has been read it shows the requested value as requested rather than as achieved, and it synthesizes neither, on the same fail-closed-projection discipline the other affordances follow. Every capability-gated affordance renders **absent** rather than disabled where its driver flag is `false`. **The autocomplete is discovery-only and dispatches nothing** (2026-08-29 review fold): selecting an entry inserts no text into the message box and starts no turn, and this task registers no code path that sends an enumerated entry. Two contracts require that and neither is the shell's to relax — [Spec-017 §Command interception — the reserved `/` prefix (C-18)](../specs/017-workflow-authoring-and-execution.md#command-interception--the-reserved--prefix-c-18) refuses leading-`/` input outright on provider-bound composer paths, so inserted command text would compose a message the send path rejects; and `Spec-005 §The provider command and skill surface` admits the tripwire-exempt `driver_command` arm only against post-dispatch typed evidence, which the one dispatched entry — the compaction command, reached through the compaction control's own wire call rather than through this list — is the only entry that supplies. **Files:** the same two files, EXTENDed. **Tests:** an enumeration read under one binding is not offered in a composer re-addressed to an agent of another binding — asserted on the re-read call rather than on the rendered list, since a filtered stale list would pass a render assertion; each capability-gated affordance is absent, not disabled, when its flag is `false`; and the composer issues no compaction call of its own on any usage threshold. Added at the same fold: selecting any autocomplete entry leaves the message-box value **byte-identical** and issues no send and no driver call, asserted on the input value and the call counts together — the assertion that would fail if an insert-then-send affordance were added later; and the compaction control's in-progress state clears on the compaction call's own settlement rather than on a `usage.context_compacted` row arriving, so a settled-`failed` compaction leaves no control spinning. Added at the round-2 fold: an agent row whose `observedOutputSpeed` differs from its `outputSpeed` renders both values and the provider's reason — asserted on the rendered output rather than on the request, since showing only the requested value is exactly the false success the control exists to prevent — and a row carrying no `observedOutputSpeed` renders the requested value as requested with no achieved-state claim and no synthesized `off`. Added at the round-3 fold: a row on a **live, declaring, not-yet-observed** binding renders **not yet observed** rather than off and rather than the requested value restated as achieved — the arm every freshly spawned binding passes through, so a test that only covered the disposed-binding absence would have passed while the ordinary case rendered a false reading; and an enumeration reply carrying **two** binding groups offers only the group matching the composer's target agent, asserted on the offered set rather than on the request, since a composer that concatenated both groups would still have issued exactly one correct call.
- **T-023r-6-3 — Deep-link invite confirmation surface.** `shell/InviteConfirmation.tsx` — the renderer half of `Spec-023 §Deep-Link Invite Flow` steps 4-7, in this plan's own shell subtree because a deep link can fire before any session view is mounted and the surface therefore belongs to the shell, not to a feature view. It subscribes through `invite.subscribePending`, renders the supplied display metadata (`sessionName` and `inviterDisplayName` are nullable by contract — the surface identifies the session by `sessionId` when they are null, never by fabricating a label), and requires an explicit affirmative action before calling `invite.confirmPending`. **Acceptance is never the default outcome:** the affirmative control is not auto-focused and is never triggered by a bare Enter; dismissal by escape, backdrop, or the decline control routes to `invite.dismissPending`, never to confirm. A refusal-shaped prompt renders the typed reason terminally with no confirm control at all — the renderer cannot manufacture a reference the main process did not mint — and a could-not-verify transport state offers retry through `invite.retryPending`, passing back the `attempt` handle that arm supplied so the retry re-drives the deep link the surface is actually showing, never acceptance. It subscribes to `invite.subscribeOutcome` for the terminal result: **navigation happens on the `joined` outcome event**, not on `confirmPending` returning (which resolves to `void`); an `authentication-required` outcome holds the surface open in a progress state rather than closing it or reporting failure, because the reference survives that transition and the participant is mid-authentication, and `authentication-failed` is the arm that releases it from that state — rendering the cancellation or failure terminally, with no confirm control, since its reference is gone; `reference-invalid` and `refused` render terminally with no confirm control. **Files:** `apps/desktop/src/renderer/src/shell/InviteConfirmation.tsx` (CREATE), `apps/desktop/src/renderer/src/shell/__tests__/InviteConfirmation.test.tsx` (CREATE) **Spec coverage:** Spec-023 §Deep-Link Invite Flow (step 4's explicit confirmation with no auto-accept; step 7's navigation on membership); Spec-023 §Renderer Responsibilities (the surface projects main-supplied state and holds no authority) **Verifies invariant:** I-023-9 (the explicit-action requirement and the no-confirm-control refusal arm are the renderer-side half of the no-auto-accept guarantee), I-023-10 (the renderer holds only the opaque reference and can neither mint nor re-resolve one) **Consumes:** `invite.subscribePending` / `invite.subscribeOutcome` / `invite.confirmPending` / `invite.retryPending` / `invite.dismissPending` ← this plan's Phase 2 (T-023r-2-5, which pins the namespace and both discriminated unions before this body renders them)

**Goal:** Implementation Steps 15 and 16 — fuse-flip, ASAR digest, per-platform signing with the notarization submit-and-poll design, release-manifest signing, and the bundle-size gate.

**Preconditions.** Phase 6 must have merged; the pipeline packages the complete application. No upstream-plan box gates this phase — it consumes only this plan's own output and release-engineering credentials, which §Risks And Blockers tracks separately.

#### Tasks

- **T-023r-7-1 — Fuse flip and ASAR integrity digest.** `build/fuses.ts` applies the nine-fuse posture via `@electron/fuses`' `flipFuses()` and ships a `--verify` mode that reads the packaged binary's fuse wire; `build/asar-digest.ts` runs `asar integrity-digest` via `@electron/asar` v4.1.0+. Both run after packaging and **before** signing, because each invalidates any prior signature. **Files:** `apps/desktop/build/fuses.ts` (CREATE), `apps/desktop/build/asar-digest.ts` (CREATE), `apps/desktop/build/fuses.test.ts` (CREATE) **Spec coverage:** Spec-023 §Electron Fuses (the nine-fuse posture); Spec-023 §Build And Packaging (fuse-flip and digest-embed precede signing) **Verifies invariant:** I-023-3 (the pipeline's step order is asserted by the pipeline itself, not left to a runbook)
- **T-023r-7-2 — Per-platform signing and notarization.** `build/code-sign.ts` implementing §Notarization Submission And Poll for macOS — detached `notarytool submit --no-wait -f json`, persisted submission id, bounded resumable `wait` / `info` polls under the 168-hour overall abandonment ceiling, `stapler staple` on `Accepted`, `notarytool log` captured on `Invalid` / `Rejected` — plus Azure Artifact Signing or `signtool` EV fallback on Windows and GPG signing on Linux. **Files:** `apps/desktop/build/code-sign.ts` (CREATE), `apps/desktop/build/code-sign.test.ts` (CREATE) **Spec coverage:** Spec-023 §Code Signing And Notarization (timeout + retry rather than synchronous blocking; the documented 24–120+ hour queue-delay range this plan's ceiling sits above); Spec-023 §macOS (Developer ID Application cert plus `notarytool` submission and stapling) **Verifies invariant:** I-023-3 (signing runs last, and the notarization poll never re-enters the pipeline ahead of it)
- **T-023r-7-3 — Sign-order tamper arm.** The §Adversarial-Tampering Boundary sign-order arm: a pipeline configuration that signs **before** fuse-flip or before digest-embed must fail the build, not merely warn. The ordering is currently prose in §Build / Config; this task makes violating it a build failure with a named error. **Files:** `apps/desktop/build/sign-order-boundary.test.ts` (CREATE) **Spec coverage:** Spec-023 §Build And Packaging (the ordering constraint and why each preceding step invalidates a signature); Spec-023 §Pitfalls To Avoid (signing before fuse-flip is a named packaging pitfall) **Verifies invariant:** I-023-3 (an executable assertion of the ordering, which is what promotes it from documentation to a gate)
- **T-023r-7-4 — Release manifest signing and bundle-size gate.** `release/manifest-sign.ts` producing `manifest.sig` over each `latest-*.yml` with the release-engineering Ed25519 key (held out-of-band, distinct from every code-signing cert), and `build/size-check.ts` failing CI when the post-asar post-compression bundle exceeds the ADR-016 150 MB ceiling. **Files:** `apps/desktop/release/manifest-sign.ts` (CREATE), `apps/desktop/build/size-check.ts` (CREATE), `apps/desktop/build/size-check.test.ts` (CREATE) **Spec coverage:** Spec-023 §Shell ↔ Auto-Update Feed (the signed manifest the updater verifies against); Spec-023 §Build And Packaging (packaged-artifact constraints) **Verifies invariant:** I-023-3 (manifest signing is the release chain's last step for the same reason binary signing is)

These assert pipeline behavior (fuse wire, notarization poll-and-resume, step ordering, bundle ceiling, probe elimination, and the Linux `.deb` / `.rpm` udev dependency declaration read out of the artifacts' own metadata) without packaging a real release.
- **E2E tests** (Playwright `_electron`, `apps/desktop/test/e2e/*.spec.ts`):

T-023r-8-5 extends that same file with the two-client run — client A invites, client B deep-links in, and both observe the resulting membership. The mock is generated from the `SidekicksBridge` contract type rather than hand-written, so a leak added to the real surface fails the harness too.
