### Adversarial-Tampering Boundary

Added 2026-08-10 by the Tier-8 remainder readiness audit (NS-20, M-023-2). The remainder is almost entirely a tampering surface — an untrusted renderer, an untrusted inbound URL, an untrusted update feed, a credential store, a crash-upload channel, and a signing pipeline — so the `000-plan-template.md` threat classes are enumerated here with the task that owns each arm. Every arm lands inside a phase; none is a floating aspiration.

| Threat class | Arm | Owning task |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Canonicalization round-trip | A `sidekicks://` URL survives parse → normalize → re-serialize with no action or argument reinterpretation. Deep-link parsing is the plan's only untrusted-input parser. | T-023r-5-4 |
| Empty-segment / trailing-separator rejection | `sidekicks://invite/` (empty token) and `sidekicks://invite//<token>` (empty leading segment) are rejected **at the parser boundary**, never coerced into a valid action; percent-encoded and trailing separators ride the same arm. | T-023r-5-4 |
| Preview-refusal fall-through | A typed `invite.preview` refusal (`invite.not_found` / `invite.expired` / `invite.revoked` / `invite.already_accepted`) and a control-plane transport failure each terminate the deep-link path: neither mints a pending reference, and neither reaches `acceptInvite`. A forged or replayed reference from the renderer resolves to nothing. This is the arm that keeps the no-auto-accept property total rather than dependent on the renderer behaving. | T-023r-5-5 |
| Intake validation parity | Each IPC handler's own validation is at least as strict as the contract schema — a payload the schema rejects must not pass the handler. `Spec-023 §Trust Stance` makes every renderer-originated payload untrusted, so handler-level leniency re-admits what the contract forbids. | T-023r-2-7 |
| Mutation-isolation symmetry | Structured-clone discipline in both directions: a renderer-held reference cannot mutate main-process state, and a renderer-supplied object is cloned on the way in. | T-023r-2-7 |
| Crash-report exfiltration | A synthetic event planted with a PASETO token, a DPoP proof, a session id, an absolute file path, and a content payload has every one stripped by `beforeSend`. Named residual: `beforeSend` does not reach raw minidump bytes — those are covered by the server-side scrubbing rules plus the opt-in upload gate, not by this arm. | T-023r-3-3 |
| Keystore refusal bypass | After a `keystoreUnavailable` refusal, a subsequent `setSecret` still throws rather than falling through to an in-memory or on-disk path, and persistence cannot re-enable without a fresh successful backend check. Refusal is sticky by construction. | T-023r-4-2 |
| Sign-order tamper | A pipeline configuration that signs **before** fuse-flip or before digest-embed fails the build with a named error rather than warning. `fuses.ts --verify` checks the resulting wire; this arm checks the ordering that produced it. | T-023r-7-3 |
| Update-feed tamper | A `latest-*.yml` altered after signing fails `manifest.sig` verification over its exact bytes — the check cycle is refused with nothing from the manifest honored; a downloaded artifact whose hash does not match the authenticated manifest's entry is discarded before staging. No per-artifact signature exists: artifact authenticity derives solely from the hash binding to the signed manifest, and both refusals are fail-closed with no state mutation. | T-023r-5-2 |

### Testing posture (BL-118 closure, 2026-05-20 via PR #81)

The `@ai-sidekicks/desktop` package's test surface is split into two disjoint Vitest projects gated by Turbo:

- **`test:renderer`** — happy-dom environment, includes `src/renderer/**/__tests__/**`. Exercises React components and renderer unit logic against plain TypeScript imports. Does NOT invoke `build:smoke`; ~3 s feedback loop. `dependsOn: ["^build"]` in `apps/desktop/turbo.json` covers workspace upstreams only.
- **`test:smoke`** — node environment, includes `test/**/*.test.ts` (currently the sole `launch.smoke.test.ts`). Spawns the BUILT `electron-vite --mode=smoke` bundle and asserts Spec-023 §webPreferences-lock runtime invariants (`window.sidekicks` defined; `window.require`, `process`, `global` all `undefined`). `dependsOn: ["build:smoke"]` same-package — Turbo's content-hash cache restores `out/**` when `src/**` + `build/**` + `electron.vite.config.ts` are unchanged, so the build runs only when sources actually changed. Cache miss costs the full ~25-30 s electron-vite build + `assert-webprefs.ts` gate; cache hit is near-instant (Turbo restores `out/**` from `.turbo/cache/` without spawning electron-vite).

The package-level `test` script (the entry point `pnpm --filter @ai-sidekicks/desktop test` resolves to) routes through `turbo run test:renderer test:smoke --filter=@ai-sidekicks/desktop` so the outer task DAG re-resolves `build:smoke` on direct invocations — without that indirection, a bare `vitest run` script would skip the rebuild and `launch.smoke.test.ts` would fail its `existsSync` precondition checks on a cold checkout. The outer `test` task in `apps/desktop/turbo.json` carries `cache: false` to suppress Turborepo's silent input-inheritance from the root `test` task (whose `inputs` declare only `src/**` + `vitest.config.*` + `tsconfig*.json` + `package.json` — excluding both `test/**` and `build/**`); without that opt-out, edits to `apps/desktop/test/**` would cache-hit the orchestrator and skip the inner smoke suite, producing false-green CI on the very surface this posture protects.

The build-time `assert-webprefs.ts` gate (`Spec-023 §Security Hardening Baseline`) remains the load-bearing security check — it runs as part of every `build:smoke` invocation. The runtime smoke-test assertions in `launch.smoke.test.ts` run against the BUILT bundle; running smoke against unbuilt sources is not a supported configuration. The split only changes WHEN the build runs (gated by Turbo's content-hash cache), not the smoke gate's authority.

At Tier 8, when the Playwright `_electron` E2E suite ships per Implementation Step 18, it should land as its own project (`test:e2e`) with `dependsOn: ["build:smoke"]` or `dependsOn: ["build"]` (depending on whether E2E runs against the smoke bundle or the release bundle); the renderer + smoke split established here is the substrate Playwright extends.

**Amended 2026-08-10 by the Tier-8 remainder readiness audit (NS-20).** The two globs above are disjoint by design, but they are also **incomplete for the Tier-8 remainder**: `renderer` matches `src/renderer/**/__tests__/**` and `main` matches `test/**/*.test.ts`, so **no project's glob reaches them at all** — `src/main/**` and `build/**`, precisely where the remainder's main-process and build-script units live (§Test And Verification Plan has always declared them at `apps/desktop/src/main/**/*.test.ts`). Under the two-project posture those files would be authored, typechecked, linted, and never executed. T-023p-1B-3 closes this by registering a `main-unit` project in Phase 1B, in the same PR as the first such unit; the paragraph above therefore describes `test:e2e` as its own project rather than "the third". The four plan-registered projects remain mutually disjoint: `src/renderer/**/__tests__/**/*.test.{ts,tsx}`, `src/main/**/*.test.ts` + `build/**/*.test.ts`, `test/**/*.test.ts`, and E2E's `*.spec.ts`. `Spec-023 §Console Test Tiers` registers the console's own projects beside these four (T-023p-1C-1), and the no-double-discovery property is re-asserted per added project rather than inherited from this sentence: console unit tests live at `src/renderer/src/console/**/*.test.{ts,tsx}` with no `__tests__/` directory, the `renderer` project's glob gains `!src/renderer/src/console/**` in the same change, and the browser-mode, screenshot, accessibility, budget, and end-to-end tiers take `apps/desktop/test/console/<tier>/**` globs that no other project reaches — a console project whose glob intersects any other is a build failure asserted by the console's architecture tier. Note that the harness self-test at `apps/desktop/test/harness/mock-bridge.test.ts` (T-023r-8-2) matches `test/**/*.test.ts` and so runs in the existing smoke project — deliberate, not incidental.

## Rollout Order

1. Scaffold `apps/desktop/` + bridge type + vite config + eslint config (steps 1, 2, 3, 17).
2. Land main-window factory + preload + IPC handler skeletons (steps 4, 5, 6).
3. Land crash reporter (step 12) — must be wired early so it catches startup crashes during subsequent step landings.
4. Land daemon supervisor + keystore + WebAuthn dispatch (steps 7, 8, 9). Gate: Plan-007 `DaemonHello` signal must be live.
5. Land DAG fallback + auto-updater + deep-link (steps 10, 11, 13).
6. Land renderer shell — router, layout chrome, composer (step 14). Gate: the bridge types from step 2 must be stable. Feature-view bodies are **not** landed here; each owning plan lands its own view into its own subtree and this step mounts it.
7. Land build pipeline (step 15) + release-manifest signing (step 16). First packaged release candidate emits from this step.
8. Land E2E suite (step 18) + CI gate (step 19) + release runbook (step 20).
9. Manual per-platform smoke on macOS arm64 + x64, Windows 11, Ubuntu 24.04 LTS, Fedora 41. First-run UX exercise against Plan-026.
10. Ship internal beta to dogfood cohort. Monitor Sentry crash rate, daemon-supervisor restart rate, update-apply success rate for 2 weeks.
11. Public V1 release once beta metrics are green.

## Rollback Or Fallback

- **Renderer-level bug post-release**: auto-update ships a patched binary within 24 hours of detection; users receive it on next update-check cycle (max 4 hours) or explicit "check for updates" action.
- **Main-process crash loop post-update**: users receive the auto-rollback on daemon-handshake failure — a re-install from the integrity-verified retained copy, never a swap from the live executable path. No user action required. An update whose prior-version retention failed was refused before apply, so no installed state exists without a rollback source.
- **Keystore unavailable at runtime**: session proceeds memory-only; auth material is not persisted; user must re-authenticate on every shell restart. Degradation is surfaced via a persistent banner.
- **Daemon fails to start after 5 backoff attempts**: shell enters offline read-only mode; user can view the last-synced session state but cannot issue new runs, approvals, or invites. Manual retry button surfaced.
- **Auto-update verification failure — manifest signature or artifact hash**: an unverifiable `latest-*.yml` is discarded with nothing from it honored; a downloaded artifact whose hash does not match the authenticated manifest's entry is discarded before staging. Either failure is logged to `updater-state.json`; next update-check cycle re-attempts. Signed-key compromise is a release-engineering incident requiring out-of-band communication.
- **Notarization queue delay (macOS)**: the release pipeline's detached-submit + resumable-poll shape (§Notarization Submission And Poll) lets a delayed release candidate complete asynchronously — the submission id is persisted, Apple's Notary service keeps processing across CI-job boundaries, and no single job is held open. Only the 168-hour overall ceiling abandons a submission, and abandonment is a manual release-engineering decision, not an automatic resubmit. If notarization fails outright (rare), `notarytool log` is captured and the artifact is not distributed.

## Risks And Blockers

- **`electron-vite` v6 stability.** v5.0.0 stable is 16 months old (released 2024-12-07); v6.0.0-beta.1 shipped 2026-04-12 but is pre-GA. **Decision: pin to v5.0.0 for V1.** Revisit v6 for V1.1 once the beta stabilizes. Alternative considered: direct Vite + `electron-builder` wiring without a wrapper (gives maximum control; loses HMR convenience and source-code-protection baked-in). Alternative rejected because V1 desktop is a greenfield Electron app and `electron-vite` is the de-facto production pick for 2026 greenfield projects per the Apr-2026 research pass. Alternative also considered: `@electron-forge/plugin-vite` — still marked experimental in v7.11.1 (2026-01-12) per [Electron Forge Vite plugin docs](https://www.electronforge.io/config/plugins/vite). Rejected: an experimental plugin is not a V1-appropriate foundation.
- **Playwright `_electron` experimental API.** Still officially marked experimental in v1.58.x per [Playwright class-electron docs](https://playwright.dev/docs/api/class-electron). Accepted: this is the only viable option (Spectron archived since 2022 with no revival confirmed in the Apr-2026 research pass). Mitigation: design test suites to tolerate minor API churn between Playwright releases; pin Playwright in `package.json` and review changelogs on every bump.
- **WebAuthn platform coverage — settled 2026-09-01, and what remains is verification rather than design.** The 2026-04 entry recorded a cross-platform gap and accepted a macOS-only PRF posture. That is superseded: no cross-platform Electron WebAuthn module exists (`@electron-webauthn/native` is deprecated in favour of `electron-webauthn`, whose sole implementation dependency declares `"os": ["darwin"]`), and neither non-macOS leg needs one, because Windows exposes `webauthn.dll` to any process holding an `HWND` and Linux's only authenticator class is the roaming FIDO2 key `libfido2` already drives. **The residual risks are three, each named rather than accepted wholesale.** (a) **The macOS end-to-end PRF verification is [BL-108](../backlog.md)-gated**: passkey operations on native macOS require a code-signed `.app` carrying the application-identifier and associated-domains entitlements plus an RP-hosted `apple-app-site-association` file, with no localhost exemption, so that leg cannot be exercised until signing infrastructure lands. (b) **No hosted CI runner can produce a PRF byte on any platform** — no Touch ID, Hello, TPM, or key — so end-to-end PRF is a recorded manual matrix and CI verifies only load, layout, prototype resolution, and the credential-free capability reads. (c) **The Windows Hello PRF build floor is graded `Source`, not Primary**: the widely-cited 25H2 build 26200.7840 figure originates in a vendor blog and is corroborated only by a third-party issue thread; the KB release notes for that exact build mention neither WebAuthn nor PRF. **No gate encodes that build number** — the Windows leg reads `WebAuthNGetApiVersionNumber()` and probes, which is correct whether or not the figure is right. **One named re-evaluation trigger** for the Linux leg: `linux-credentials/credentialsd` landing with a TPM backend, and the XDG credential portal it proposes, would give Linux a platform authenticator for the first time; that is an ordinary focused edit to this plan when it ships, not a V1 gate.
- **Minidump PII scrubbing gap.** `@sentry/electron` v7.11.0's `beforeSend` hook does not apply to raw minidump bytes; minidumps go through **server-side** scrubbing rules per [Sentry Electron minidump docs](https://docs.sentry.io/platforms/javascript/guides/electron/configuration/integrations/electronminidump/). Minidumps contain heap memory, which includes PASETO tokens, DPoP keys, PRF output. **Mitigation path**: (a) `crashReporter.start({ uploadToServer: true })` is gated on explicit user opt-in (default off) in the first-run telemetry prompt owned by Plan-026; (b) Sentry server-side data-scrubbing rules are configured to strip PASETO token patterns (`v4.public.`, `v4.local.`) and DPoP JWT patterns before the minidump is persisted. Self-hosted symbolication (Option B in `Spec-023 §Open Questions`) is the long-term answer; V1 ships the Sentry path with the dual gate.
- **Azure Artifact Signing regional eligibility.** `Spec-023 §Open Questions`: USA / Canada / EU / UK organizations + US / Canada individuals only. If the issuing org is outside eligibility, the Windows-signing path falls back to a traditional EV cert (DigiCert / Sectigo / SSL.com), now subject to CSC-31's 15-month validity (not the pre-2026 3-year) per [CA/Browser Forum CSC-31 ballot](https://cabforum.org/working-groups/code-signing/requirements/). Plan-023 documents both paths; lock-in decision is release-engineering-owned, pre-V1 RC.
- **Smart App Control reputation cold-start on Windows.** A freshly-signed binary has low reputation in Windows Defender's Intelligent Security Graph. First-launch UX on Windows 11 will be rough until reputation accumulates. Accepted for V1. Mitigation: Artifact Signing's Public Trust chain is documented as the preferred path specifically because it chains to a CA in the Microsoft Trusted Root Program; the first-run onboarding copy in Plan-026 includes a "Run anyway" guidance for SmartScreen.
- **Notarization queue delays on macOS.** Per `Spec-023 §Code Signing And Notarization` — Apple notarization queues have been reporting 24–120+ hour delays in January 2026 per [Apple Developer forum thread 813441](https://developer.apple.com/forums/thread/813441). The release pipeline's per-platform signing step therefore does **not** hold a job open on a synchronous wait: it submits detached, persists the submission id, and polls in bounded increments under a 168-hour overall abandonment ceiling set above the documented 120-hour upper bound (§Notarization Submission And Poll). Accepted as a release-latency risk; does not block V1 if release engineering is staffed for async release cutting. A timeout shorter than the documented delay range would not be a timeout at all — it would be a guaranteed resubmission cycle, and Apple's queue treats each resubmission as new work.
- **Electron 44 pin and the branch cadence.** Electron 41 is end-of-life (2026-08-25), so the 44.x target is not optional; the branch itself is supported until 2027-03-02 and V1 must expect at least one further major bump before then. The console's `browser` pane kind depends on the detached-`WebContentsView` bounds fix, which landed on 44 and reached 42 / 43 only as late backports, so V1 admits 44.x alone and carries no per-branch fix floor. **Mitigation:** the pin is exact in `apps/desktop/package.json`, a bump is a lane-3 change that re-reads `Spec-023 §Electron Version And Support Window`, and `ADR-016 §Decision`'s supported-branch subset is amended by the same PR that moves the pin. **Two legs ride the pin move (2026-09-01).** (i) Electron 44 is Node ABI 149 and no `better-sqlite3` 12.x release ships a prebuild for it, so the daemon's binding under `utilityProcess` would compile from source on every machine; `ADR-022 §Decision Log` therefore moves `packages/runtime-daemon` to `better-sqlite3` 13.0.3 (Node-API, platform prebuilds bundled) **in or before** the PR that moves the Electron pin — the pin may not move first. (ii) [ADR-024](../decisions/024-electron-main-process-window-retention.md)'s window-retention mechanism was verified empirically on Electron 41.6.1; the shipped `lifecycle.gc.test.ts` re-verifies it on 44 in the same PR, and a divergence is an ADR-024 revisit trigger, not a test to loosen. The shipped Tier-1 comments and the `package.json` description that name Electron 41 are swept in that PR.
- **Console built ahead of Phase 6 (Phase 1C).** The console lands against the fixture bridge before the Tier-8 phases that give several of its surfaces live wires, so the risk is a surface whose fixture shape drifts from the wire that eventually lands. **Mitigation:** the fixture bridge is typed from the same `packages/contracts` desktop-bridge types the live bridge uses, so a wire change breaks the fixture at compile time; the scenario manifest's per-method live status is checked against `§Console growth slate` by a test; and a console PR that wires a surface live against an unregistered wire is a review rejection. Adoption at Phase 6 is a mount-and-move, never a rewrite (T-023r-6-1 EXTEND). The console's shell-side floor is Phase 1B, not Phase 3: until T-023p-1B-1 / T-023p-1B-2 merge the renderer has no served origin, so the first console PR follows the Phase 1B PR rather than racing it.
- **Rollback state-machine complexity.** Electron's built-in `autoUpdater` does not ship rollback; `electron-updater` 6.8.x does not ship rollback. Plan-023 authors the rollback state machine from scratch (step 11). Accepted: this is first-class code with unit-test coverage. Alternative considered: dual-slot A/B update (Android-style); rejected for V1 because dual-slot is a disk-space + launcher-complexity doubling that is inappropriate before we have load-bearing evidence the single-slot-with-rollback approach is insufficient. The retained-copy design's cost is deliberately narrower than dual-slot's: one retained prior version, held only from apply until the confirmed-healthy handshake deletes it — transient disk headroom, not a permanent second install plus launcher-based version selection.
- **`@napi-rs/keyring` ecosystem maturity.** `@napi-rs/keyring` v1.2.0 is the keytar successor path per `Spec-023 §Implementation Notes` §Native Keystore. It has not yet seen the battle-testing of `node-keytar` (archived 2022). **Mitigation**: the `keystore.ts` abstraction is backend-agnostic; if `@napi-rs/keyring` regresses, the abstraction can swap to `safeStorage` for all keys (with the accepted usability regression that `safeStorage` is a one-shot-blob API without per-key metadata). This swap is a code-only change, no schema migration.
- **Renderer clipboard deprecation pitfall.** Electron 40+ deprecated direct `navigator.clipboard.*` from the renderer per `Spec-023 §Implementation Notes` §Renderer Bundle. The ESLint `no-restricted-imports` rule is the CI gate; a renderer file that imports `@capacitor/clipboard` or similar cross-platform clipboard library bypasses the rule. **Mitigation**: document in `apps/desktop/README.md` that clipboard access must route through `window.sidekicks.native.copyToClipboard`; add a runtime assertion (`expect(navigator.clipboard).toBeUndefined()` in renderer boot) that fails fast if a transitive dep re-introduces renderer-clipboard access.
- **Single-instance-lock race with deep-link.** `Spec-023 §Main Process Responsibilities` requires `app.requestSingleInstanceLock()`. Without the lock, a `sidekicks://invite/<token>` URL arriving at a second instance would race with the first instance's daemon state. The lock + `second-instance` event handler is the correct pattern, but it introduces a subtle bug: if the first instance is mid-crash during URL arrival, the second instance gets the lock and may re-exchange the invite token. Accepted: Plan-007's invite-redemption API is idempotent on double-submission (same token → same session membership), so the race is a UX oddity not a security issue. Documented in `apps/desktop/RELEASE.md`.

    verifies_invariant: [I-023-11, I-023-13, I-023-14]
    spec_coverage:
      [
        "Spec-023 §Console Design (Meridian)",
        "Spec-023 §Console Libraries",
        "Spec-023 §Console Test Tiers",
      ]
    notes: |
      T-023p-1C-1, first of two PRs. The console substrate: the app frame, icon rail, and
      hash router; the Meridian tokens, hue system, and primitives; the session stores and
      the apply chokepoint; the persistence layer with both adapters; the fixture bridge,
      growth port, scenario manifest, and scenario engine; the palette and keybinding
      grammar; the error boundaries; the tripwires; and the console test projects (unit,
      browser-mode, screenshot, accessibility, budget, endurance, end-to-end, architecture,
      assets, bundle). `App.tsx` mounts `ConsoleRoot` from this PR on; a release bundle
      compiles the fixture out and binds the live preload bridge through the single
      `bridge/live-bridge.ts` reader. Five Codex rounds folded through substrate lanes
      (registry growth, the scripted-reply seam, RouteSurface agent context, tokens before
      bridge failure, fixture stores on the scenario clock). `phase: 1` for the same reason
      the Phase 1B row records: the supplement label `1C` can never be a manifest phase key,
      so the task id carries the assertion. The task is not fully shipped by this row alone;
      PR #422 (the next row) completes it.

      `ConsolePaneAddress` union with its runtime parser, sidebar section ids, inline-card
      seats, pane-kind scope table), the run-stream and queue projections through the
      registered payload schemas, the fixture session directory derived from the
      `SessionState` census, the scenario wire-truth walk split by axis (beat shape,
      run-and-queue semantics, beat order, reply walk, membership), late-subscriber prefix
      replay in the scenario engine, session-scoped fixture relay, the `accent-ink` token
      and filled-accent primitive, the store selectors for stamped execution posture and
      membership role, and the two dependency-cruiser rules (view-family isolation, no
      barrel chain) with their architecture tests. Thirteen Codex rounds: rounds 1–12 folded
      in full through seats lanes; the code-review cap (five) was exceeded at round 6 and the
      cycle closed at round 13 under the cap policy, whose four findings (beat `atMs`
      finiteness, a present-but-malformed `channelId` normalized rather than refused, the
      448-line `run-stream-projection.ts` queue-arm split, and the `resultFor` session-read
      arm in the fixture directory) are answered on the PR threads and owed first by the
      T-023p-1C-8 structure audit on develop. With this row T-023p-1C-1 is fully shipped;
      T-023p-1C-2..7 ride PRs #423–#428, each a family branch merged onto this squash.

### Notes

<!-- Per-PR human commentary (round-trips, learnings, partial-ship details). Append-only. -->

**2026-09-02 — `apps/desktop` structure enforcement: the dead-code and layering gates (§6 node NS-100); Status `approved → review → approved` in one swap.** Widens **I-023-14** in place — the budgets and test tiers it already gates are joined by `structure:dead-code` (knip) and `structure:layering` (dependency-cruiser), so a console PR now also proves reachability and layering — and registers the four files that carry them at §Target Areas Build / Config. Widening a named invariant is a §Status Flip Rule behavior-change trigger, so this plan flipped and was restored in the same diff by the targeted readiness-audit delta riding it; `Spec-023 §Console Libraries` takes the paired nineteenth axis and `§Console Test Tiers` the normative console-family-DAG paragraph. **No invariant is minted** — an existing one already says these gates run in CI, and a second id would split one obligation in two — so the Gate-4 invariant-reference census pin does not move. The gate wiring itself is lane-3 tooling and adds no Shipment Manifest row. Two design points are recorded because they cost a rewrite: `console-not-plan-subtree` bans only the subtrees whose owners **mount into** the console, never the three Tier-1 components §Target Areas has it absorb by import, because a gate stricter than its own plan is a defect; and the layering pass runs with an explicit resolver extension list rather than `--ts-config`, because dependency-cruiser resolves a tsconfig's `extends` against the process directory, so a per-tsconfig pass would pin the cruise's working directory and this tree, declaring no path aliases, loses nothing without it. Both gates were proven by planted negative control before landing.

**2026-09-01 — Zero-copy enforcement withdrawn: the mechanical string sweep is removed and the rule it enforced is kept; Status `approved → review → approved` in one swap.** `Spec-023 §Console Design (Meridian)`'s Zero-copy bar had carried a second sentence requiring a mechanical sweep against a fixed list of forbidden strings on every console PR with a known-bad negative control, `§Console Test Tiers`' architecture row listed it among the tripwires, and **T-023p-1C-1** carried it as a CI-check deliverable. All three are struck, along with the checker, its list file, the architecture-tier test that drove it, the package script, the turbo task, and the CI step. **The reason is structural, not a cost judgement.** A tracked checker can forbid a string only by carrying it, so the gate could not exist without shipping the very material the rule exists to keep out of this repository; and the one escape — committing digests instead of terms — trades that for a list no reader can audit, correct, or extend, whose negative control has to be minted from the same unreadable source. The rule constrains what an author is told before writing console code, which lives in the instructions an author is given and not in any artifact the repository can hold. **The rule itself is untouched** — mechanics are adopted from the field; skin, names, and copy are ours — and review enforces it, as it already does for the other three product bars. `§Console Test Tiers` still registers **nine** tiers and the architecture tier keeps every other tripwire and its each-asserting-at-least-one-site rule, so no count in either document moves. Both this plan's paired-spec box and its plan-readiness-audit box are re-opened and re-checked in this same diff. No preload-bridge capability, wire method, event type, error code, table, or column is minted; no census moves, and the restore being in-swap, the README plan-status census and its CLAUDE.md counterpart do not move either. **No [cross-plan-dependencies.md §6](../architecture/cross-plan-dependencies.md) node is minted:** the swap registers no cross-plan obligation and no §6 entry described the withdrawn sweep, so there is nothing for a node to record and nothing stale for one to correct. **One erratum rides the same PR**, recorded here because it corrects a registration rather than a decision: §Target Areas Renderer listed the console's family directories as they were first drafted, naming `tripwires/` as a family of its own and omitting `core/` and `routing/` entirely. The tree T-023p-1C-1 ships folds the runtime tripwire registry into `core/` — the DAG floor the layering gate already anchors there — and carries `routing/` as the family between `tokens/` and `primitives/`, so the registration and its **Files:** cell are re-derived from the shipped tree. Erratum-class: no family is added or removed from the plan's scope, no task, invariant, or obligation changes, and the Status does not move.

**2026-09-01 — WebAuthn per-platform strategy: the platform-coverage open question settled and the accepted scope degradation withdrawn ([cross-plan-dependencies.md §6](../architecture/cross-plan-dependencies.md) node NS-99); Status `approved → review → approved` in one swap.** `Spec-023 §Open Questions` had carried since 2026-04 whether any Electron WebAuthn module covers Windows and Linux, and this plan's §Scope recorded the consequence as an accepted scope degradation. The answer is negative — no cross-platform Electron WebAuthn module exists — and **the degradation is withdrawn rather than accepted**, because neither non-macOS leg needs one: Windows exposes `webauthn.dll` to any process holding an `HWND`, and Linux's only authenticator class is the roaming FIDO2 key `libfido2` already drives, from the distribution package rather than bundled. V1 ships all three legs native, with macOS an **own-built** universal Swift N-API addon over `AuthenticationServices` (`apps/desktop/native/webauthn-darwin/`, registered in the §2 ownership map) — Vault12 `electron-webauthn-mac` is the reference implementation and is rejected because it generates the WebAuthn challenge internally, so no assertion it produces is server-verifiable, and its binary is arm64-only with no arch guard. Adds **I-023-15** (capability is probed per host at ceremony time, never inferred from `process.platform`), **T-023r-4-5** (the Windows binding, with the three runtime API-version floors, the discoverable-credential requirement, and the shared salt domain) and **T-023r-4-6** (the Linux binding), rewrites **T-023r-4-3** onto the own-built addon, pins **`koffi`** as the FFI (exact, floor `3.1.6`, MIT — needs no `onlyBuiltDependencies` entry, and `asarUnpack` must name `**/node_modules/@koromix/**`), and adds two E2E spec files while re-scoping two more — `webauthn-mac.spec.ts` onto the own-built addon and `webauthn-fallback.spec.ts` from `(win32 + linux)` to all three platforms (nine → eleven). **I-023-8** is re-derived in the same swap: its renderer import-ban enumeration named `src/main/` and `src/preload/`, and the native addon tree is a sibling of `src/` that neither prefix reached, so both the invariant and T-023r-2-6's ban list now name it (and `koffi`). The Device Authorization Grant loopback is restored to the per-host fallback `Spec-023 §Fallback Behavior` always made it and stops being "the Windows / Linux path". A new invariant plus new tasks is a [§Status Flip Rule](../operations/plan-implementation-readiness-audit-runbook.md#status-flip-rule) row-3 trigger, so both §Preconditions boxes carry the Re-opened/Delivered record and the restoring targeted readiness-audit delta rides this same diff. No phase gate moves; the macOS end-to-end PRF verification stays [BL-108](../backlog.md)-gated exactly as the signing work already was. ADR-016 takes three dated engine-scoped narrowings in the same swap with its Status unmoved at `accepted`; ADR-010 and the Security Architecture need no edit. No census moves. **Codex round 1 fold (2026-09-01, same swap).** Fourteen threads, all adjudicated into normative content. Four were internal contradictions this branch's own round-0 text left standing — T-023r-4-4's Spec-coverage line still calling the DAG the declared Windows / Linux path, two `apps/desktop/package.json` dependency enumerations still naming `electron-webauthn-mac`, and the hosted macOS E2E row claiming an assertion a credential-free runner cannot produce. The rest changed what the plan builds. **I-023-16** is minted for the trust boundary the bridge contract had inverted: `webAuthn.createCredential(options)` / `getAssertion(options)` / `deriveKeyMaterial(input)` let the untrusted renderer choose the challenge, the `rpId`, and the PRF salt, and handed derived key material back across the bridge — the surface narrows to `register()` / `signIn()` taking no argument, with main fetching the server-issued options from the control plane and validating them against the build-pinned RP — **superseded at Codex round 5**, which re-derived the accepted `rpId` from the **pairing-pinned** control-plane origin, since a compiled-in identifier refuses every self-hosted deployment — and T-023r-2-5 carrying the two-armed negative test and the `packages/contracts/src/desktop-bridge.ts` narrowing. **T-023r-4-7** is added for the custody leg `ADR-010 §Consequences` already decided and this plan had never carried: a host that reaches the DAG fallback is reauthenticated but cannot reproduce the KEK that wraps its Ed25519 identity key, so it takes ADR-010's password-derived KEK on ADR-021's ratified primitives — no E2EE feature is refused. **Guard 4** is added for the CTAP 2.1 rule that hmac-secret output differs by user-verification mode (two per-credential secrets, selected on the `uv` bit in the _response_), which makes libfido2's `FIDO_OPT_OMIT` default a non-deterministic key derivation. Three vendor facts were verified against primary sources and corrected: the Windows cancellation surface is a **two-call pair** (`WebAuthNGetCancellationId` plus a `pCancellationId` member, both v1 baseline), taking the Windows call sites seven → **eight**; `WebAuthNIsUserVerifyingPlatformAuthenticatorAvailable` returning `FALSE` gates the **platform arm only** and never the ceremony, the same rule now stated for macOS's two providers; and macOS carries **two** PRF floors, 15.0 for the platform authenticator but **26.4** for security keys. Two round-0 claims were **falsified by this round's own research and are corrected rather than dropped**: the macOS probe was specified as `getClientCapabilities()` + `isUserVerifyingPlatformAuthenticatorAvailable()`, which are Web IDL members with no main-process existence, so the leg probes `LAContext.canEvaluatePolicy` and records it as an explicit **proxy**; and the Linux `uaccess` rules were said to ship with the `libfido2` package, which is false in every supported release — access comes from `systemd` / `udev` ≥ 244 descriptor autodetection, so the packaged build declares that instead, and the `uaccess` grant is a **seat ACL** rather than a permission bit. Counts re-derived by direct count: invariants **16**, Phase-4 tasks **7**, silent-failure guards **4**, Windows call sites **8**, E2E spec files **11** (unmoved — the two added tests are a `build/**` unit and a `src/main/**` unit, neither a spec file). ADR-016's narrowings re-derive two → **three** with the §Alternatives Option A steel man. No census moves. **Codex round 2 fold (2026-09-01, same swap).** Five threads, all adjudicated into normative content, and two of them correct round 1's own text rather than the branch's round-0 text. **ADR-016's deciding set is re-derived by listing rather than narrowed again.** Round 1 held that ADR's Status at `accepted` on the ground that WebKitGTK carries no engine-level WebAuthn, and once every ceremony is shell-native that ground is gone — a Rust or Go host binds `AuthenticationServices` / `webauthn.dll` / `libfido2` as readily as a Node one — so the WebAuthn constraint is **struck** from the deciding set, §Synthesis's "the one constraint that cannot be engineered around" is corrected (this amendment is the engineering-around), and the §Decision Log entry enumerates what still decides with each item's evidence: the single Chromium renderer the console's single-number budgets and `@xterm/xterm` WebGL tier are measured against, the `browser` pane's need for an in-process `WebContentsView` with a `webContents.debugger` CDP channel on all three platforms, `utilityProcess` over a Node main hosting the `better-sqlite3`-bearing daemon and its PTY-sidecar supervision, and the reversal cost of Tier-1 code already bound to `app` / `BrowserWindow` / `contextBridge`. The Status outcome is stated rather than assumed — the remaining set still decides, so `accepted` holds; had it emptied, the row would have moved the ADR to `proposed`. The sweep carries to §Thesis point 1, the §Consequences positive bullet, Option A's steel man, Options B and C point 1 (the WebAuthn gap **withdrawn** as a rejection reason), Assumption 1's what-breaks cell, and Revisit Trigger 1, so the ADR carries no site still calling WebAuthn a decider. **The macOS probe becomes advisory and gates nothing**, correcting round 1 rather than restating it: `canEvaluatePolicy(.deviceOwnerAuthenticationWithBiometrics)` is documented false on a Mac with no Touch ID hardware, with no enrolled biometric, and system-wide after repeated failures, and a platform passkey ceremony still completes in every one of those states through iCloud Keychain, a paired device, or the user's password — so the platform provider is attempted on every host and the probe, if kept, reads `.deviceOwnerAuthentication` as a diagnostic. The asymmetry with Windows is now the rule: a **direct** first-party availability answer may gate its own arm, and a proxy that is not that answer may gate nothing. **T-023r-4-7 gains the surface that makes it reachable.** Main has no text-entry affordance and Electron's native dialogs carry none, so the KEK leg was specified and unreachable; it now opens a **secret-entry window** on the I-023-12 pattern through the same private `constructLockedWindow`, with its own preload exposing exactly one write-only operation that delivers the passphrase once and resolves `void`, after which main closes the window, derives the KEK, and zeroizes the buffer — with tests for the one-op shape, the no-echo guarantee, and zeroization. It is not a `SidekicksBridge` namespace (a namespace would be callable from every session renderer) and not an auxiliary window (it hosts no pane and takes no route-registry entry), so the auxiliary set stays at **two**. **Two remaining corrections.** `Spec-023 §Main Process Responsibilities` still said "Return the authenticator assertion through the bridge", contradicting I-023-16 and the bridge contract's own prohibition three subsections above it — it now resolves the typed `WebAuthnCeremonyOutcome`, with main submitting the assertion over its own authenticated control-plane channel. And the Linux transport set said USB / NFC / BLE: `libfido2` has **no BLE backend under any flag** (its own scope sentence is "a FIDO device over USB or NFC", and `fido_dev_info_manifest` runs `hid` unconditionally with `nfc` / `pcsc` under flags that default ON for Linux), so the row states HID plus NFC exactly and a BLE-only key takes the fallback path; no BLE transport is added. Counts re-derived by direct count and unmoved by this round: invariants **16**, Phase-4 tasks **7**, silent-failure guards **4**, Windows call sites **8**, E2E spec files **11** — the three files T-023r-4-7 adds are a `src/main/**` unit, a preload entry, and a renderer route body, none of them a spec file. No census moves. **Codex round 3 fold (2026-09-01, same swap — under the three-round documentation cap).** Five threads, all adjudicated into normative content; three were P1. **Guard 4 moves from the request to the response**, which is where CTAP actually decides: a `userVerification` preference does not bind an authenticator (`discouraged` does not forbid verification, `preferred` does not establish it), so pinning the request left the derivation exactly as non-deterministic as guard 4 was minted to prevent. Every leg now reads bit 2 (`0x04`) of the flags byte in the returned authenticator data — `WEBAUTHN_ASSERTION.pbAuthenticatorData`, `fido_assert_authdata_ptr`, `ASAuthorizationPublicKeyCredentialAssertion.rawAuthenticatorData` — compares it against the recorded mode, and derives nothing on a mismatch, with a mismatched-bit fixture on each leg; the rule is deliberately **not** "require UV everywhere", which would refuse every non-UV key and strand the hosts the Linux leg exists for. **A third detection rule** makes a binding that will not load a **negative capability result**: a rejected dynamic `import()`, an absent `webauthn.dll` or `libfido2.so.1`, an addon missing this architecture's slice — each caught at its own load boundary, recorded with the library and symbol names, and routed to the fallback, never rethrown and never reported as a failed ceremony, because a host that never had a binding did not fail one. Each leg carries a resolution-failure fixture. **T-023r-4-3 takes the macOS addon build**, which no task owned — `swift build` per architecture joined by `lipo -create` into one universal `.node`, an `apps/desktop/package.json` script the desktop build depends on so a macOS `pnpm build` cannot silently ship a bundle with no addon, a macOS CI step, and the `electron-builder.yml` output path — at the **Xcode 26.4** floor (Swift 6.3, macOS 26.4 SDK, itself needing macOS 26.2+), set by the SDK rather than the language because the security-key `prf` property is a 26.4 symbol, with the deployment target still macOS 12.0 behind availability annotations. **Implementation Step 9 is rewritten**: it still threw `WebAuthnPrfUnsupportedError` and reauthenticated on the refresh token alone, contradicting both the typed outcome and the custody leg the round-1 fold landed; it now resolves the `fallback-required` arm and runs the DAG for authentication and `kek-fallback.ts` for custody, in that order. And **CP-023-9** gives `Plan-022` CP-022-3 its producer back: retiring `webAuthn.deriveKeyMaterial` left that approved reciprocal naming a deleted method, so the main-owned `PrfKeyDerivationService` port — in no `SidekicksBridge` type, reachable from no bridge namespace, injected rather than imported — is registered as the producer, with Plan-022's obligation row, Tier-2 ladder pointer, and Tier-5 `Consumes:` line re-pointed and that plan taking an in-swap flip-and-restore, since a cross-plan-obligation row changing its named producer is a contract-row change. Counts: cross-plan obligations eight → **nine**; invariants **16**, Phase-4 tasks **7**, guards **4**, Windows call sites **8**, and E2E spec files **11** all unmoved. No census moves. **Codex round 4 — (2026-09-01).** Seven threads, all material, all folded. **The ceremony's server half is given an owner.** A repo-wide search established that no route, task, credential store, or obligation anywhere in the corpus issues a WebAuthn challenge or verifies a response, so this plan's bindings reached an authenticator with nothing to call — [Plan-018](./018-identity-and-participant-state.md) takes it as a new **Phase 6** (T6.1–T6.4, extended to T6.5 at round 7) with `webauthn_credentials` + `webauthn_challenges`, four ceremony routes on the shipped Plan-008 tRPC `sessionRouter` skeleton — five once round 7 adds the revocation route — and a single-use challenge fence, registered here as **CP-023-10** ⇄ Plan-018 CP-018-14 and gated mechanically by `{ type: external_plan_phase_merged, plan: 018, phase: 6 }` on Phase 4 rather than by a born-unchecked box. That phase also settles what guard 4 compares against: the **user-verification mode is server-held**, recorded on the credential row from the verified registration response and returned on the assertion-options reply, because a mode the client merely remembers is gone after a reinstall and absent on a second desktop using a synced credential. **The Windows version arithmetic is replaced by a table.** `dwVersion = min(desired, supported)` compared numbers from two unrelated sequences: `pHmacSecretSaltValues` sits on `GET_ASSERTION_OPTIONS` **version 6** but ships in the `WEBAUTHN_API_VERSION_4` delta block, so an API-4 host would have been sent struct version 4 and Windows would have ignored the PRF request on exactly the builds that can serve it. `Spec-023 §WebAuthn Platform-Authenticator Native Module` now carries an explicit API-version → maximum-struct-version table for all four structs, read from the pinned header's version-history block — the **only** place an API version and a struct version appear together, since the header carries no `added in WEBAUTHN_API_VERSION_N` comment inside any struct body — with a positive API-4 fixture, and each PRF floor cross-checked against the response member that returns its result at the same API version (`pHmacSecretSaltValues` v6 with `WEBAUTHN_ASSERTION.pHmacSecret` v3, both API 4; `bEnablePrf` v6 with `bPrfEnabled` v5, both API 6; `pPRFGlobalEval` v8, API 8). **The Linux leg returns a verifiable attestation.** It read back only a credential id, which is not a registration a relying party can verify — it now composes the `attestationObject` as `{fmt, attStmt, authData}` from `fido_cred_fmt` / `fido_cred_attstmt_ptr` / `fido_cred_authdata_raw_ptr`, since `libfido2` exposes `fido_cred_set_attobj` as a **setter only** and no getter for the whole object, with `fido_cred_sig_ptr`, `fido_cred_id_ptr`, and the `_x5c_list_` family for a full chain rather than `fido_cred_x5c_ptr`, which is the leaf alone. The `_raw_` choice is corrected everywhere it matters, guard 4 included: `fido_cred_authdata_ptr` and `fido_assert_authdata_ptr` return the **CBOR-byte-string-wrapped** form, so a flags-byte read at that pointer is off by the header's width. Client data enters through `fido_cred_set_clientdata` (unhashed, hashed internally — the form Yubico's manual directs applications to prefer), and the leg keeps its own copy because the library offers a getter for the hash and none for the bytes; both serialized payloads are asserted against the Plan-018 T6.2 / T6.3 verifiers. **Both FFI legs stop blocking the main thread.** `WebAuthNAuthenticatorGetAssertion` and `fido_dev_get_assert` block for a human interaction, so each is invoked through `koffi`'s asynchronous call path; the macOS leg needs none, `AuthenticationServices` being asynchronous by construction. Cancellation is stated **asymmetrically rather than smoothed over**: Windows has the documented `WebAuthNGetCancellationId` / `WebAuthNCancelCurrentOperation` pair, issued from main while the call is pending; Linux does not, because `fido_dev_cancel`'s entire documentation is one sentence, it is absent from its own page's return-value section, `libfido2` publishes no thread-safety statement anywhere, and its only in-tree call site is single-threaded — so the leg bounds the wait with the documented `fido_dev_set_timeout` instead, closes the device handle only after the call settles, and names the residual (a Linux cancel is immediate for the caller; the key may keep waiting until the bound expires). **And the `deriveKeyMaterial` sweep is completed rather than declared:** `Plan-022`'s reciprocal-obligations bullet still required this plan to expose it, CP-023-9's transport is corrected off "injection over a process boundary" onto the typed `shell.deriveKek` request registered in `api-payload-contracts.md` §Shell-Hosted Method Registry, the per-platform module export is renamed `deriveKek`, and T-023r-2-5 takes the contracts-side retirement — `packages/contracts/src/desktop-bridge.ts` still declares the withdrawn three-method shape, and a contract advertising a caller-supplied-options method is an invitation to build one. Counts after round 4: cross-plan obligations nine → **ten**; invariants **16**, Phase-4 tasks **7**, guards **4**, Windows call sites **8**, and E2E spec files **11** all unmoved. **One census moves and it is Plan-018's**: Postgres **26 → 28**.

**2026-09-01 — Console design (Meridian) lands in Spec-023; the console subtree, its invariants, and its growth slate land here (§6 node NS-97); Status `approved → review → approved` in one swap.** The design track `Spec-023 §Non-Goals` had deferred to since authoring has run — a whole-corpus surface survey, a reference-mechanics study adopting mechanics and never skin, and a cited library-leverage pass — and its result is `Spec-023 §Console Design (Meridian)` plus `§Console Libraries` and `§Console Test Tiers`. This plan takes the parts that are a plan's to hold: the `console/` subtree registered at §Target Areas Renderer built on **lane 1** as the Tier-1 explicit-label supplement **Phase 1C** (T-023p-1C-1..8, gated only on Phase 1B merged and dispatched by label; the console is a projection over already-registered bridge namespaces inside the approved spec envelope, and anything that would mint a wire is lane 4 and amends first), the absorb-by-import rule for the shipped Tier-1 components and the never-touch rule for the other plans' subtrees `timeline/`, `usage-meters/`, and `run-controls/`, the adoption path at Phase 6 (T-023r-6-1 EXTEND: mount and move, never re-author), invariants **I-023-11** (renderer scheme privileged before ready — without it the console's persistence has no IndexedDB and the design's persisted layouts are a fiction) and **I-023-12** (auxiliary windows are hardened windows with their own bridge instance and no shared store), the auxiliary-window and scheme legs on Phase 1B (T-023p-1B-1 / T-023p-1B-2, composed by T-023r-3-4 EXTEND), and `§Console growth slate` — the ledger of every wire the console builds against the fixture and does not yet have, each row naming its owning document, so nothing the console shows is a claim the corpus has not made. **The Electron floor moves:** 41 reached end-of-support 2026-08-25 with 44.0.0, the `browser` pane kind's `WebContentsView` bounds fix is on 42+ only, so the target is 44.x (44.1.1 at authoring), `ADR-016 §Decision` is amended in the same swap to the 42 / 43 / 44 subset, and the packaging pins are re-derived from the registry (`electron-builder` 26.15.x, `electron-updater` 6.8.9). **Three conflict resolutions, each for the owning spec:** notification mute global-only (Spec-019), the Invites View's decline a local hide (Spec-002 mints no verb), and no renderer detach control (Spec-009). **Errata riding the sweep:** Spec-023's workflow non-goal still called the engine V1.1; two Spec-007 cites named headings that do not exist; the "sixth run control" phrasing is made count-free across Spec-004 / Plan-004 / Spec-016 / Spec-023 and the payload-contract comment because Spec-004's own V1 control enumeration had omitted `cancel`, which is corrected there. `axe-core`'s MPL-2.0 is admitted as a never-distributed test dependency in `ADR-020 §Decision Log`. **No wire method, event type, error code, table, or column is minted; no census moves.** The restore is in-swap, so the README plan-status census and its CLAUDE.md counterpart do not move either. The one thing this swap deliberately does **not** do is pre-register the `browser.*` namespace, the terminal pane's Spec-003 obligations, or the embedded-browser ADR: those are the first rows of the growth slate and land as their own amendment batch before the surfaces that need them go live. **Phases 1B and 1C** are the two structural additions beyond the console registration. The renderer-load legs first written onto Phase 3's T-023r-3-4 in this same swap were re-homed the same day to the Tier-1 explicit-label supplement Phase 1B (T-023p-1B-1..4, gated only on Phase 1 merged — the Electron-44 / `better-sqlite3`-13.0.3 pin move joining it as T-023p-1B-4 so ADR-022's ordering rule holds in one task), because the shipped window loads nothing, none of those legs consumes a daemon contract, and a console that cannot run in the shell until Plan-007 R3 would be built blind; T-023r-2-8 is retired into T-023p-1B-3 and T-023r-3-4 EXTEND becomes composition-only. The console itself ships on **lane 1** as Phase 1C (T-023p-1C-1..8, gated only on Phase 1B merged) rather than on the enhancement lane the design track had proposed: [CONTRIBUTING.md §How Code Lands: Work Classification](../../CONTRIBUTING.md#how-code-lands-work-classification)'s lane 2 is defined over code a shipped phase introduced, which new authorship is not, and widening that definition to admit one tree would weaken the boundary for every plan — a supplement phase with manifest rows, a title token, and a by-label preflight is the honest shape and costs nothing the console was not already going to pay. The two ripple audits this swap ran folded twelve cross-document and seven set-quantifier findings into normative content rather than cataloguing them: the window-control namespace joins the growth slate (twenty-four rows); the composer sketch now defines the send router, target chip, and posture chip it hosts; `Spec-023 §State And Data Implications` carries the renderer-scheme persistence tier and the widened settings set; the research pass's ceiling row names the 44.x target and demotes the 41.6.1 probe to provenance; the pane-routing rule states where repo, worktree, invite, and member entities open; the invite family's scope is stated exactly; `Spec-015 §Driver Pin` takes a landed-fact annotation naming T-023p-1B-4 as the PR its upgrade-policy gate binds, with ADR-022's residual two-ABI sites rewritten and the two architecture-doc pin cells re-derived; `Spec-019 §Fallback Behavior` states the global-only mute it already decided; Plan-026's a11y deferral and menu-ownership residuals are closed against the landed tier and the Phase-1B menu; and I-023-13 / I-023-14 make the fixture-bridge coherence and the budgets invariants rather than prose.

**2026-08-29 — Desktop-console parity: the session composer and the MCP-servers operator page (§6 node NS-93); Status `approved → review → approved` in one swap.** Registers **CP-023-7**, the reciprocal of [Plan-028](./028-mcp-server-configuration-and-governance.md) CP-028-8 (Plan-028's MCP-governance page mounts into this plan's settings surface; the CP-023-6 / NS-73 owner-authors-host-mounts split unchanged), and **CP-023-8**, this plan's consumption of Plan-005's `driver.listProviderCommands` under the target agent's `(driverName, providerAccountId)` binding. `Spec-023` **is** touched this time — two sub-sketches join §Signature Feature Composition Sketches (six → **eight**, both carrying the operator-plane / shell-chrome `Note` the section's own rule requires, so the Signature Feature enumerations do not move) and the Timeline View gains its input-ask bullet — so the paired-spec box moves as well as the audit box, and both are re-checked in this same diff. T-023r-6-2 takes an EXTEND clause for the composer's autocomplete and its three capability-gated mount points; no new task and no new invariant is minted, because the composer already exists as a task and the fail-closed-projection rule the new surfaces obey is `I-004-24`, already stated.
