**2026-08-25 — Deep-link invite-confirmation amendment + restoring targeted readiness-audit delta (§6 node NS-81); Status `approved → review → approved` in one swap.** Authors the Tier-8 consumption leg of the non-consuming invite-metadata read, the last open leg of [BL-133](../backlog.md#bl-133-non-consuming-invite-metadata-endpoint-for-the-deep-link-confirmation-surface), which **stays open and is corrected in place**: criterion (c) is written as a two-client smoke that _passes_ end-to-end, so no doc-only amendment can meet it — closing it here would have retired the criterion by reinterpreting it. It is narrowed instead onto **T-023r-8-5** as its named exit vehicle, and the gate substitution below is what makes leaving it open cost-free. What landed: tasks **T-023r-5-5** (main-side preview, opaque pending-invite reference lifecycle, confirmation handoff) and **T-023r-6-3** (the renderer confirmation surface in this plan's own shell subtree), invariants **I-023-9** (no acceptance without explicit confirmation; the preview is the only pre-confirmation call and a typed refusal mints no reference) and **I-023-10** (the reference is opaque, single-use, TTL-bounded, main-resolved), obligation **CP-023-5** (consume Plan-002's `invite.preview` verbatim), the `invite` namespace registered end-to-end at T-023r-2-5 behind a Phase-5 seam (contracts member plus `createTier1Bridge()` stub, `preload/invite.ts` plus its one-line `preload/index.ts` spread, and the `main/bridge/invite.ts` handler — the three arms §Target Areas Preload now names as the reachability rule), and one §Adversarial-Tampering Boundary arm. **The load-bearing edit is the gate substitution:** Phase 5's `{ type: bl_closed, ref: 133 }` entry became `{ type: plan_phase, plan: 002, phase: 2, status: merged }` paired with the invite-metadata box, carried onto Phase 6 as well. That entry is strictly stronger — Plan-002 Phase 2 declares T2.1–T2.8 and has shipped T2.1–T2.5, so `classifyPhaseShipment` reads it as unshipped and the gate fails closed on the missing code, where the retired entry would have opened the moment the backlog item closed. Closing BL-133 without that swap would have opened Phase 5 against an endpoint that exists only on paper. **Three count claims re-derived by direct count over §Target Areas Main process, not by arithmetic:** the §Preconditions gate-entry sentence (six → all seven `precondition_box_checked` entries), the Phase-2 goal (six → seven per-surface handler files), and Implementation Step 6 ("five" → seven — pre-existing drift dating to PR #6, predating both the `onboarding` and `invite` namespaces). **Codex round 1 (seven findings) folded in, not catalogued.** The `invite` namespace was registered with method names but no wire shape, so T-023r-2-5 now pins both event unions, the reference TTL (`min(mint + 10 minutes, expiresAt)`), and the three reference-side refusal codes; `subscribeOutcome` was added because `Spec-023 §Deep-Link Invite Flow` steps 6 and 8 require the membership result to arrive as a **bridge event**, which a promise value from `confirmPending` would not satisfy; `retryPending()` was added because T-023r-5-5 mints no reference on a transport failure, leaving T-023r-6-3's advertised retry with nothing callable — it takes no argument, so it cannot be aimed at a reference the participant never saw. The **authentication transition** was the substantive gap: `invite.preview` is anonymous while `acceptInvite` is authenticated, so a signed-out recipient could see a confirmation he could not complete; the reference is now consumed at acceptance-issue rather than at confirm-request, which is what lets it survive the Plan-018 detour (I-023-10 restated accordingly). Criterion (c)'s smoke had no manifest owner, so **T-023r-8-5** now runs it across two clients. **Spec-023 is flipped `approved → review → approved` in this same swap**, its restoring targeted readiness-audit delta riding the same diff. Its §Deep-Link Invite Flow pin ended by assigning the shell's consumption leg to Plan-008's Tier-5 daemon-side handoff — the opposite transport from what steps 2 and 5 of that same flow require and CP-023-5 registers — and the sentence now splits the in-app leg (Plan-008 / CP-002-9) from the deep-link leg (CP-023-5). That **reassigns a mechanism** rather than repairing a typo: an implementer reading the spec builds a different client path before and after, which is the fact that clears the audit runbook's flip bar, on the §6 node NS-73 precedent that flipped this same spec for materially re-derived interaction enumerations — a lighter class than reassigning a leg's transport. No Required Behavior row moves, no contract is minted, and the Status ends `approved`, so no census moves. The line-delta constraint this edit was first written to respect is **stale, and was verified stale rather than recalled**: NS-73 itself converted the last inbound `Spec-023 line NNN` anchors to the §-anchor-plus-descriptor form, and a fresh repo-wide sweep over the `.md` corpus and the code tree finds zero surviving numeric line cites into this spec, so the amendment blockquote lands without renumbering any citer. **Two further adjudications from the pre-push ripple audit, folded in as normative text rather than catalogued.** (i) `Spec-023 §Example Flows` still narrated deep-link auto-accept — the sibling of the contradiction the transport split repaired, 77 lines away — so it is rewritten to preview-then-confirm and its stale "emits a `session:joined` event on the bridge" is corrected to the `invite.subscribeOutcome` delivery this plan pins; it rides the same flip, not a second one. (ii) The **anonymous-transport arm** was missing: §Target Areas and §API And Transport Changes both described `controlPlane.call` as attaching `Authorization: DPoP` + `DPoP` to _every_ outbound request with only the relay WSS carved out, while `invite.preview` is anonymous by construction — Plan-008 I-008-4 gates `invite.create` / `invite.accept` / `invite.revoke` and excludes `preview` precisely because the previewer may hold no identity yet. Routing an anonymous procedure through an unconditionally credential-attaching client would have re-introduced, one layer down, the regression I-008-4 exists to prevent, and would have made T-023r-5-5's `authentication-required` outcome unreachable. Header attachment is now stated as conditional on holding a credential, with `invite.preview` header-free, and T-023r-2-3 asserts it in both the credential-absent and credential-present cases rather than leaving it to the implementer. The remainder of the amendment discharges a delegation — §Deep-Link Invite Flow already mandates preview-then-confirm and assigns the reference shape, lifecycle, and error semantics to this plan at Tier 8; §Target Areas Contracts now states that Tier-8-namespace rule explicitly, which also makes the pre-existing `onboarding` namespace coherent against the Tier-1 interface block.

**PR #70 in-PR task additions (T-023p-1-8 … T-023p-1-10).** The Phase-1 `#### Tasks` block declares T-023p-1-1 … T-023p-1-7; the shipment manifest records ten. The three extras were introduced inside the PR as load-bearing governance work — a citation-correction sweep, the Phase B.5 amendments to this plan and Spec-023, and the Phase D round-trip cite fixes — not as pre-declared DAG tasks. Recorded here so the manifest is self-describing; the declared set is unchanged and the declared-⊆-shipped relation holds.

**Tier-8 remainder readiness audit (NS-20, PR #318, 2026-08-10).** The audit found the remainder undispatchable — no `### Phase N` headings, no `#### Tasks` blocks, no per-phase `preconditions:` YAML, no `## Invariants`, no `## Cross-Plan Obligations`, and no plan-level audit checkbox for the audit gate to resolve. Nineteen findings (4 critical / 8 major / 5 minor / 1 nit / 1 verification no-op). Every Plan-023-owned finding is amended in this swap: §Tier 8 Remainder PR Sequence (Phases 2–8), the audit checkbox, `I-023-1..8`, `CP-023-1..4`, the ADR-024 requirement, the Plan-002 header dependency and its composer pin, the request-param precondition box, the `features/*View.tsx` ownership correction and the stale bridge method strings it disposed of, the corrected Phase-1 AC-coverage paragraph, the notarization design, the §Adversarial-Tampering Boundary arms, the BL-131 harness, this manifest note, and the Done Checklist tier split. Plan-023 flipped `approved → review` on four `§Status Flip Rule` row-3 triggers and was restored `approved` in the same swap.

**Items routed out of this swap, or closed here by a narrower action than the finding proposed.** Recorded so the audit record is complete and nothing reads as silently dropped. The first two bullets are the only findings **not** closed in this file; the rest are closed here and recorded for their reasoning:

- **Runbook half of M-023-1.** The readiness-audit runbook's `§Per-Phase Audit Semantics` presents Plan-023 Phase 1 as the canonical `substrate_exempt` criterion-(3) example, quoting the empty-coverage sentence this swap corrected. Correcting the plan without touching the runbook leaves the false precedent teaching the pattern to every future audit. The runbook is the methodology surface and is not edited from a plan swap; the fix belongs to a runbook-owning change and must quote this plan's corrected paragraph without drift.
- **M-023-7 (a) and (b), and m-023-5 — dependency-map edits.** (a) The §2 rows for `apps/desktop/src/main/` and `apps/desktop/src/preload/` owe a per-file content-ownership line for the `bridge/` tree, recording that `bridge/daemon-status.ts` is Plan-007 R3 content inside a Plan-023-owned namespace — the same shape as `main/sidecar-lifecycle.ts`. CP-023-3 is this plan's side of that edge. (b) The audit's recommendation is to **leave** the Plan-007 R3-PR-b/c tier placement at Tier 4: the projector builds and runs against Plan-007's own gateway, so there is no build-order cycle, and moving R3 to Tier 8 would create the back-edge that row is at pains to avoid; the residual is runtime pill content only, and CP-023-2 plus CP-023-3 are the closing edges. (m-023-5) The §3 Plan-007 row mis-numbers this audit as NS-21; §6 is unambiguous that NS-20 is the Tier-8 audit and NS-21 is Tier 9. All three are dependency-map edits and land with that file's owner.
- **m-023-6 — Spec-023 acceptance-criteria numbering (closed here, narrower).** The finding's proposed fix — numbering the Spec-023 bullets — is declined; the authoring rule below is adopted in its place and is in force across this file, which is why the audit checkbox lists m-023-6 as amended. `Spec-023 §Acceptance Criteria` is unnumbered bullets, so a Tier-8 task under time pressure would invent an `AC-N` anchor — a cite-fabrication failure. Numbering the bullets in Spec-023 would be the durable fix but is a spec amendment with its own promotion lifecycle. **Authoring rule adopted here instead:** until and unless Spec-023 numbers its acceptance criteria, every Tier-8 `Spec coverage:` marker uses the heading-plus-behavior-name form and never a synthesized id — `Spec coverage: Spec-023 §Acceptance Criteria (no auth material on window.sidekicks); Spec-023 §Preload Bridge Contract`. Every marker in §Tier 8 Remainder PR Sequence follows it.
- **Anchor caution carried forward.** The **eight** `###` sub-sketch headings under `Spec-023 §Signature Feature Composition Sketches` — five Signature Feature views, the operator-plane Provider Accounts And Cost View NS-74 added, and the shell-chrome Session Composer plus the operator-plane MCP Servers View NS-93 added (2026-08-29; the count is re-derived by heading count, not incremented) — carry parenthetical owner suffixes and em-dashes, so `§Approvals View` is **not** a valid anchor. The corpus gate normalize-matches and will not catch a truncated one; any future amendment citing a sub-sketch rather than the parent section must byte-compare against the real heading first.
- **Census siblings.** A Plan-023 status flip re-derives the README plan-status census and its CLAUDE.md counterpart. Those surfaces are not Plan-023-owned and are not edited from this file; they ride the same PR under their own owner.
- **Open source check.** §Scope attributes seven CVE ids to the 2026-04-02 Electron batch and the 39.8.5 / 40.8.5 / 41.1.0 fixed-branch floor. The audit could not reach the upstream advisory feed to confirm the attribution; it is carried as a source check for the swap PR, not asserted as a defect.

**PR #318 Codex review round — update trust chain and rollback retention.** Two review findings against the Phase-5 updater tasks are amended before merge. T-023r-5-2 as first authored verified a per-artifact Ed25519 signature no task produces — T-023r-7-4 signs only the `latest-*.yml` manifest — so verification could only fail or be skipped; it now verifies `manifest.sig` over the exact manifest bytes against the pinned key and hash-binds each downloaded artifact to the authenticated manifest. T-023r-5-1 recorded the live executable path as its rollback source — a path the in-place apply itself overwrites; it now retains the prior version under `<app-user-data>/retained-versions/` before apply, refuses the update fail-closed when retention fails, and rolls back only from the integrity-verified retained copy. §Scope, §Target Areas, §Data And Storage Changes, §API And Transport Changes, Implementation Step 11, §Test And Verification Plan, §Adversarial-Tampering Boundary, §Rollback Or Fallback, §Risks And Blockers, and the Done Checklist carry the same correction.

**2026-09-01 — WebAuthn per-platform strategy, Codex round 5 (§6 node NS-99).** Twelve findings, all folded. Four of them are corrections to this plan rather than additions to it. **The ceremony was ordered wrong.** The client derived from PRF output before the relying party had accepted the assertion it came from, and guard 4 compared against a mode carried on the **options** reply — which cannot work, because guard 2 requires discoverable credentials and a discoverable sign-in offers no credential list, so the options leg does not know which credential will sign. The flow is re-ordered — options → assertion → verify → derive — with PRF bytes **provisional** until the verdict and zeroized on every refusal arm (new **I-023-17**), and the mode re-homed onto the verification reply. **Cancellation was conflated with incapacity.** A participant who dismisses the OS dialog was reaching the same fallback as a host with no authenticator, which would present a browser sign-in to someone who had just declined to sign in. It is now terminal on its own arm: no second provider arm, no DAG loopback (new **I-023-18**, which also carries the per-binding single-flight slot — held to the native callback and released on timeout but never on the caller's cancellation, so a cancel-then-retry takes a typed busy refusal rather than putting a second `fido_dev_get_assert` on a device handle the first call still owns). **The relying-party identifier was a build constant**, which would have failed the origin check on every self-hosted control plane; it now derives from the origin main pinned at install-to-control-plane pairing, with the macOS **platform** provider's `webcredentials:` associated-domain entitlement named as an [ADR-020](../decisions/020-v1-deployment-model-and-oss-license.md) self-hosting residual — unavailable to a stock build, degrading honestly to the security-key arm and then the loopback. **And the transport registered at round 4 had no modules on either side**: CP-023-9 now names `packages/contracts/src/desktop/shell-rpc.ts`, `apps/desktop/src/main/shell-rpc.ts`, and the `daemon-supervisor.ts` extension here, with the daemon-side caller named on Plan-022 T22.1.3, plus a duplex round-trip test over a real `MessagePort` pair — and the **HKDF is pinned to the shell side**, so the request carries the PRF evaluation input and Plan-022's salt and the response carries a finished 32-byte KEK, raw PRF output never crossing the RPC into the process that does not own the authenticator. `Spec-023`'s silent-failure guard list moves four → **five** for the minted-once PRF evaluation input, without which a fresh input each ceremony derives a fresh key and every previously wrapped envelope stops opening at the next restart. **Census:** no wire method, error code, table, or column is minted by this plan; the counts that move are inside its own closed sets — invariants sixteen → **eighteen**, and three files join §Target Areas and T-023r-4-7.

**2026-09-01 — WebAuthn per-platform strategy, Codex round 6 (§6 node NS-99, the last fold).** Eight findings, taken as one redesign rather than eight patches, because three of them are the same hole seen from three sides. **The PRF evaluation input could not have worked.** Round 5 had the relying party mint one per credential and serve it on the authentication-options reply, and that reply is composed **before** a discoverable sign-in names a credential — there is nothing to look one up for, and the only way to serve one anyway is to return every candidate's, which publishes the participant's enrolled-credential set on a reply that is unauthenticated on purpose. The daemon could not have supplied it either. It is now a **contract-fixed public constant**, `Spec-023`'s guard list goes four → five → **four** again, guard 3 absorbing the reproducibility obligation, and the `shell.deriveKek` request loses that member and carries only Plan-022's salt. Security is unmoved: the secret is the authenticator's per-credential `CredRandom` and the input only selects which evaluation to take. Yubico's own PRF guide does recommend a unique per-credential salt — that recommendation is **recorded in §References and declined with its reason** rather than quietly contradicted, since it presumes an `allowCredentials` flow this deployment does not have. **What the per-credential salt was actually being asked to do now has a mechanism.** Two enrolled authenticators derive two unrelated KEKs, so wrapping the refresh-token and identity-key envelopes directly under one means the other opens nothing — and re-wrapping every envelope at each enrolment needs a live ceremony on the **old** credential, at exactly the moment a participant enrolling a backup key is trying to stop depending on it. New **T-023r-4-8** owns a per-installation **custody root key** (new **I-023-19**): the envelopes are wrapped under it once, every wrapping key wraps only a 32-byte **copy** of it, copies live in the OS keystore keyed by credential id, enrolment writes one copy and removal deletes one, and the passphrase-derived fallback KEK wraps a copy under the same rule — which is what makes a host that later gains PRF a re-wrap rather than a re-encryption. Two consequences are stated rather than discovered: no server escrow, so losing every credential loses the root; and the root is per **installation**, so a reinstall mints a new one. **Which is why sign-in on a cold install had to change too.** Main was unwrapping a stored refresh token it would not have — the flow was unreachable exactly after a reinstall. `Spec-023 §WebAuthn Credential Flow` step 6 now takes a freshly issued, DPoP-bound PASETO pair off the verification reply (Plan-018 T6.3) and wraps it under the newly minted root; unwrapping is the path for an installation that already holds one. **Three smaller corrections ride along.** Guard 1's surviving `dwVersion = min(desired, supported)` sentence is deleted — the explicit API-version → struct-version table is the only rule, and computing `dwVersion` by comparing two unrelated sequences is the mistake the table exists to prevent. Guard 4's Linux read is `fido_assert_authdata_raw_ptr` and never `fido_assert_authdata_ptr`, whose CBOR byte-string header shifts the flags byte. And T-023r-4-6 now **owns** the `.deb` / `.rpm` udev dependency it had only described, in `electron-builder.yml` with a packaging unit that asserts it out of the built artifacts' own metadata rather than out of the YAML the build was configured from. The E2E spec-file count is corrected to **eleven** in the second statement that still said nine. **Census:** no wire method, error code, table, or column is minted by this plan; the counts that move are inside its own closed sets — invariants eighteen → **nineteen**, Phase-4 tasks seven → **eight**, and one packaging unit joins the `build/*.test.ts` project.

**2026-09-01 — WebAuthn per-platform strategy, Codex round 7 (§6 node NS-99, the closing round).** Five findings, all real gaps rather than style, and three of them are consequences round 6 created and did not follow. **The custody root could never be minted on the path that needs it most.** Round 6 minted it at an installation's _first enrolment_; a cold install after a reinstall reaches this host by **sign-in**, performs no enrolment, and would have wrapped its freshly issued token pair under a root that does not exist. The trigger widens to the first time a **verified** ceremony on this installation finds no local root — enrolment or sign-in, whichever came first — which is also the only formulation that stays honest about what a per-installation root means. **The DPoP proof key could not live where round 6 implied.** Main signs the token-request proof _before_ the verification reply arrives, so a key sealed under the custody root is unreachable at exactly the moment it is used; the key takes its own [ADR-021](../decisions/021-cli-identity-key-storage-custody.md)-ladder keystore entry, explicitly **not** wrapped under the root, and T-023r-4-3 owns it in `dpop-key.ts` beside the module that requests the options. Round 6 did not face this because it issued no token. **The options fetch was unspecified on the one path where its posture matters.** The relying party's authentication pair is unauthenticated by construction, so the sign-in fetch is stated **uncredentialed** — main must not attach a token it may not hold — and the reply is validated against the **pairing-pinned** control-plane origin rather than any origin the response names, which is the same rule I-023-16 already applies and had not been written down for this leg. **A revoked authenticator had no server-side meaning.** Deleting this installation's wrapped copy is the only removal the client can perform, and it is not the thing a different machine's sign-in consults; Plan-018 gains the fifth route (T6.5) and this plan's removal leg is restated as the local half of it, with a server-first ordering — revoke, then delete the copy — so a failed revocation does not leave the participant believing a key is retired. **And CP-023-9 stops being macOS-scoped**: the obligation was written against a macOS-only PRF posture this amendment withdrew, so its Resolution is re-derived on **resolved host capability** (I-023-15) and Plan-022's four macOS-only sites and Spec-022's desktop KEK branch are corrected in the same diff — that branch had stated the HKDF over raw PRF output, named a renderer API for a main-process ceremony, and cited a superseded Candidate Recommendation. **Census:** no wire method, error code, table, or column is minted by this plan; the counts that move are inside its own closed sets — invariants nineteen → **twenty** (I-023-20, the DPoP proof key's custody separation) — while the Phase-4 task count holds at **eight** and the `api-payload-contracts.md` ceremony registry goes four → **five** rows, which is Plan-018's census, not this plan's.

Three partially-closed boxes are split into a ticked Tier-1 half and an open Tier-8 half rather than ticked whole.

- [ ] **(Tier 8 remainder)** The heavy dependency set deferred from Tier 1 is added: `electron-builder` 26.15.x, `electron-updater` 6.8.9 (re-pinned 2026-09-01), `@napi-rs/keyring` v1.2.0, `@sentry/electron` v7.11.0, `@electron/asar` v4.1.0+, `@electron/fuses`, `koffi` (exact, floor `3.1.6` — Windows and Linux WebAuthn bindings; needs no `onlyBuiltDependencies` entry, and `asarUnpack` must name `**/node_modules/@koromix/**` explicitly because the binary lives in a sibling package), `oauth4webapi` v3.8.5, `@playwright/test`. The macOS WebAuthn addon is built from `apps/desktop/native/webauthn-darwin/` in this repository rather than installed.
- [x] **(Tier 1)** `packages/contracts/src/desktop-bridge.ts` exports the `SidekicksBridge` interface from `Spec-023 §Preload Bridge Contract`; conditional-type negative test passes.
- [x] **(Tier 1)** Every `BrowserWindow` is created with `contextIsolation: true`, `sandbox: true`, `nodeIntegration: false`, `webSecurity: true`, a preload script, and the custom protocol load (not `file://`). Build-time assertion script (`assert-webprefs.ts`) greps the factory for each value and fails the build on drift.
- [x] **(Tier 1)** The `window.sidekicks` surface has no `any`-typed escape hatch; the bridge type negative test catches leaks of `token` / `dpop` / `prf` / `secret` names.
- [x] **(Tier 1)** Renderer ESLint config rejects `electron` / `node:*` / `fs` / `child_process` / `net` / `os` / `path` / `process` / `./src/main/**` / `./src/preload/**` imports from `apps/desktop/src/renderer/src/**`; CI gate fails on violation.
- [ ] **(Tier 8 remainder)** The renderer import-ban list extends with `keytar` / `@napi-rs/keyring` / `@sentry/electron` / `koffi`, the `main/keystore.ts` / `main/webauthn/**` modules, and the `apps/desktop/native/**` addon tree once they ship (T-023r-2-6).
- [x] **(Tier 1)** `app.requestSingleInstanceLock()` is held at startup.
- [ ] **(Tier 8 remainder)** `second-instance` handler receives deep-link URLs on Windows / Linux; `open-url` handler receives them on macOS.
- [ ] **(Tier 8 remainder)** Release builds ship with the nine Electron Fuses from `Spec-023 §Security Hardening Baseline` set as declared; `fuses.ts --verify` mode asserts the packaged binary's fuse wire matches on every CI build.
- [ ] **(Tier 8 remainder)** Release builds embed the ASAR Integrity Digest (new in Electron 41 via `@electron/asar` v4.1.0+) **before** code signing; packaging pipeline order is enforced, and a configuration that signs first fails the build rather than warning.
- [ ] **(Tier 8 remainder)** Daemon is supervised via `utilityProcess.fork()` after `app.whenReady()`; `"memory-eviction"` exit is special-cased (restart without incrementing failure counter); 5 consecutive non-memory-eviction crashes surface persistent-error state.
- [ ] **(Tier 8 remainder)** `@napi-rs/keyring` v1.2.0 is the primary keystore path; `safeStorage.getSelectedStorageBackend()` refuses `basic_text` / `unknown` on Linux; on refusal, `keystoreUnavailable` bridge event is emitted, long-lived token persistence degrades to memory-only, and the refusal is sticky for the session.
- [ ] **(Tier 8 remainder)** The WebAuthn ceremony routes to a native binding on **all three** platforms — the own-built `native/webauthn-darwin/` addon on macOS, `webauthn.dll` on Windows, `libfido2` on Linux — with the binding module selected by `process.platform` and the host's capability decided by a ceremony-time probe (I-023-15). A host whose probe finds no usable authenticator, or whose authenticator answers without PRF output, routes to the Device Authorization Grant loopback fallback on **any** platform. DAG fallback binds to `'127.0.0.1'` (not `'localhost'`), uses PKCE S256 via `oauth4webapi` v3.8.5, rejects `plain` method, times out after 5 minutes via `AbortSignal.timeout(300_000)`.
- [ ] **(Tier 8 remainder)** Auto-updater verifies `manifest.sig` (Ed25519 over the exact `latest-*.yml` bytes) against the build-time-embedded update-signing public key and hash-binds each downloaded artifact to the authenticated manifest before staging; the rollback state machine retains the prior version (integrity-hashed, bounded to one, deleted only on the confirmed-healthy handshake) before apply, refuses the update fail-closed when retention fails, and re-installs from the integrity-verified retained copy — never from the live executable path — on post-apply daemon-handshake failure; rollback-blacklist prevents automatic re-attempts of a version that just failed.
- [ ] **(Tier 8 remainder)** `sidekicks://` protocol handler routes `invite/<token>` URLs through the main process; the raw token never crosses the bridge to the renderer; the confirmation surface resolves display metadata through the non-consuming `invite.preview` read and the exchange via `controlPlane.call('acceptInvite', { token })` runs **only** after an explicit participant confirmation resolving an opaque single-use reference; a typed preview refusal or a transport failure mints no reference and reaches no exchange; and empty-segment URLs are rejected at the parser boundary.
- [ ] **(Tier 8 remainder)** `@sentry/electron/main` `init()` is the first thing called in `main/index.ts`; `beforeSend` scrubber strips tokens, session IDs, file paths, content payloads, proven by a negative test against a planted synthetic event. Renderer and utility-process Sentry SDKs are explicitly initialized (v7 does not auto-initialize renderers). `SentryMinidump` integration is enabled with opt-in `uploadToServer` gated by Plan-026 first-run telemetry prompt. Sentry server-side data-scrubbing rules are configured for PASETO / DPoP patterns.
- [ ] **(Tier 8 remainder)** Playwright `_electron` E2E suite covers launch, fuses, bridge surface, daemon supervision, deep-link, WebAuthn (per-platform), keystore, updater rollback. Tests tolerate Playwright API churn by pinning a specific Playwright version in `package.json`.
- [ ] **(Tier 8 remainder)** The renderer test harness named by BL-131 exit criterion (a) ships: a contract-generated `window.sidekicks` mock bridge exercisable from `test:renderer`, plus a two-client IPC dispatcher for E2E.
- [ ] **(Tier 8 remainder)** Code-signed release artifacts pass Gatekeeper (macOS), SmartScreen (Windows 10 + 11), `dpkg-sig --verify` (Linux `.deb`), `rpm --checksig` (Linux `.rpm`). macOS signing uses the detached `xcrun notarytool submit` + resumable-poll + `stapler staple` design in §Notarization Submission And Poll, under a 168-hour overall abandonment ceiling — above the 24–120+ hour queue-delay range `Spec-023 §Code Signing And Notarization` documents, so a normal queue never trips it.
- [ ] **(Tier 8 remainder)** Shell bundle size (post-asar, post-compression) is under 150 MB per ADR-016 Success Criteria; CI size-check fails on regression.
- [ ] **(Tier 8 remainder)** Clipboard access flows only through `window.sidekicks.native.copyToClipboard`; runtime assertion in renderer boot fails if `navigator.clipboard` becomes defined via transitive dep.
- [ ] **(Tier 8 remainder)** `App.tsx` mounts each Signature Feature view as a route against its owning plan's own `apps/desktop/src/renderer/src/` subtree; this plan creates no feature-view body and no `renderer/src/features/` tree exists. No mounted view duplicates daemon-side logic — a property this plan **verifies** at the composition boundary rather than one it implements.
- [x] **(Tier 1 supplement — Phase 1B)** The renderer scheme is registered `standard: true` / `secure: true` before `app.ready` (I-023-11); the bundle is served over `sidekicks-renderer://` by a handler that refuses every root-escaping path with an empty body and carries the locked CSP header on every response; the main window loads it; the two auxiliary windows are built by the locked factory with their own bridge instance and no shared store (I-023-12), reachable from the application menu through the `registerMenuSection` seam once Phase 1C registers their routes in `IMPLEMENTED_AUXILIARY_ROUTES` — empty at 1B by design, so the shipped menu renders no auxiliary entry; the `main-unit` Vitest project executes the `src/main/**` units; the smoke probe runs against the real bundle, with the `about:blank` arm gone; the Electron pin is on 44.x and `better-sqlite3` on 13.0.3 with no source compile in the install log, ADR-024's lifecycle test green under 44, and `Spec-015 §Driver Pin` amended with its re-verification recorded. _Shipped 2026-09-02 by PR #415 (`0ae91660`); see the Phase 1B manifest entry._
- [ ] **(Tier 1 supplement — Phase 1C)** The console is in-tree at `console/` with every pane kind of the closed set, the composer at its pinned home, the settings and operator pages, the two auxiliary-window routes, and the fixture bridge whose scenario-manifest live-status field agrees with `§Console growth slate` (I-023-13); every console PR passed all nine `Spec-023 §Console Test Tiers` and every budget as a CI gate (I-023-14); each view-family PR carries its jaw-drop verdict in its review notes.

## Tier Placement

Plan-023 splits across two tiers per [cross-plan-dependencies.md §5 Plan-023 Substrate-vs-Namespace Carve-Out](../architecture/cross-plan-dependencies.md#plan-023-substrate-vs-namespace-carve-out-tier-1--tier-8) (BL-101 (a) resolution):

- **Tier 1 partial-deliverable** — workspace package substrate (`apps/desktop/src/{main,preload,renderer}/` directory tree + electron-vite v5 toolchain + minimal entrypoints + `SidekicksBridge` type stub). Standalone (no upstream plan dependencies); unblocks [Plan-001 Phase 5](./001-shared-session-core.md#phase-5--client-sdk-and-desktop-bootstrap) and [Plan-024 Phase 3](./024-rust-pty-sidecar.md#phase-3--rustsidecarptyhost--env-var-opt-in). See §Tier 1 Partial PR Sequence above.
- **Tier 1 supplement — Phase 1B** (added 2026-09-01, NS-97) — the renderer-load substrate: privileged scheme + bundle handler, the window's load, the auxiliary-window factory and application menu, and the `main-unit` Vitest project. Standalone (gated only on Phase 1 merged; no upstream plan dependency), so the Phase 1C console runs in the shell without waiting on Plan-007 R3; T-023p-1B-4 moves the Electron and `better-sqlite3` pins. See §Tier 1 Partial PR Sequence.
- **Tier 8 remainder** — Implementation Steps 6–17 (step 14 reduced to the router + `renderer/src/shell/` subtree per §Renderer, since each Signature Feature view is created by its owning plan in that plan's own subtree; step 17's Tier-1 ban list extends with the keystore / Sentry entries) + 18–20: IPC handlers, daemon supervisor, crash reporter, keystore, WebAuthn, DAG fallback, auto-updater, deep-link, renderer shell + composer, build pipeline, release manifest signing, Playwright E2E, renderer test harness, CI gate, runbook. Decomposed into Phases 2–8 by [§Tier 8 Remainder PR Sequence](#tier-8-remainder-pr-sequence). Strictly **downstream of Plan-007** (consumes the daemon IPC contract), **downstream of Plan-018** (consumes PASETO tokens), **downstream of Plan-008** (consumes the control-plane tRPC + WebSocket client), **downstream of Plan-002** (the composer's typing producer leg consumes the daemon presence surface's `activity.typing` update path — CP-023-4), **parallel to Plan-024** (both are shell-surface plans but Plan-024 is owned by the daemon, not the shell), and **upstream of Plan-026** (Plan-026 consumes the `onboarding.*` preload-bridge surface authored here).
- **Tier 1 supplement — Phase 1C** (added 2026-09-01, NS-97) — the `console/` subtree at §Target Areas Renderer, built on lane 1 (T-023p-1C-1..8; `Plan-023` title token, manifest rows, by-label preflight) over the Phase-1B origin, inside the `Spec-023 §Console Design (Meridian)` envelope, against the fixture bridge. Standalone (gated only on Phase 1B merged; no upstream plan dependency); **adopted** by the Tier-8 Phase 6 (T-023r-6-1 EXTEND). Its live wiring for any surface waits on the row of `§Console growth slate` that names that surface's wire, or on the Tier-8 phase that registers it.

- [`electron-webauthn-mac` (Vault12)](https://github.com/vault12/electron-webauthn-mac) — v1.0.0 (2025-12-10), macOS-only; the **reference implementation** for the own-built `native/webauthn-darwin/` addon, not a dependency (2026-09-01: it generates the WebAuthn challenge internally and ships an arm64-only non-fat binary with no arch guard)
- [Chromium — WebAuthn origin restrictions](https://chromium.googlesource.com/chromium/src/+/main/content/browser/webauth/origins.md) — the allowlist (https, `localhost`, extension origins) that closes the renderer path regardless of scheme privilege
- [Apple — `ASAuthorizationPlatformPublicKeyCredentialProvider`](https://developer.apple.com/documentation/authenticationservices/asauthorizationplatformpublickeycredentialprovider) — macOS 12.0+; `createCredentialAssertionRequest(challenge:)` and `createCredentialRegistrationRequest(challenge:name:userID:)` take the caller's challenge
- [Apple — `ASAuthorizationPublicKeyCredentialPRFAssertionInput`](https://developer.apple.com/documentation/authenticationservices/asauthorizationpublickeycredentialprfassertioninput-c.class) — macOS 15.0+, the PRF assertion input the macOS leg uses
- [Microsoft — `WebAuthNAuthenticatorMakeCredential`](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/api/webauthn/nf-webauthn-webauthnauthenticatormakecredential) — the Win32 `webauthn.dll` surface, documented with no entitlement, package-identity, or browser restriction
- [Yubico `libfido2`](https://github.com/Yubico/libfido2) — BSD-2-Clause, 1.17.0; the Linux leg's runtime, taken from the distribution package rather than bundled
- [`koffi`](https://koffi.dev/) — MIT FFI, exact-pinned floor `3.1.6`; `napi: 8`, per-platform prebuilt `optionalDependencies`, and the documented Electron limit that forbids `koffi.view()`
- [Electron issue #24573](https://github.com/electron/electron/issues/24573) — WebAuthn origin eligibility for custom-scheme Electron apps; still open. The thread frames the refusal as an https requirement, which the 2026-09-01 measurement sharpens: a custom scheme registered `secure: true` **is** a secure context and is still refused, because Chromium gates the API on an origin allowlist
- [electron/electron #51255](https://github.com/electron/electron/pull/51255) and [#51411](https://github.com/electron/electron/pull/51411) — the macOS Touch ID platform authenticator, merged to `main` 2026-04-30 and backported into the release branches 2026-05-01; present on `44-x-y`, which is why the "Electron provides no platform-authenticator prompts anywhere" claim is corrected rather than restated
- [Playwright class `_electron` docs](https://playwright.dev/docs/api/class-electron) — still experimental in v1.58.x
- [Playwright release notes](https://playwright.dev/docs/release-notes)
- [`oauth4webapi` (panva)](https://github.com/panva/oauth4webapi) — v3.8.5 (2026-02-16) — PKCE S256 primitives
- [RFC 8252 §7.3 — Loopback Interface Redirection](https://datatracker.ietf.org/doc/html/rfc8252#section-7.3) — "MUST allow any port to be specified at the time of the request"
- [RFC 7636 §4 — PKCE code challenge methods](https://datatracker.ietf.org/doc/html/rfc7636#section-4)
- [RFC 9449 — OAuth 2.0 Demonstrating Proof of Possession (DPoP)](https://datatracker.ietf.org/doc/html/rfc9449) — `Authorization: DPoP` presentation scheme (§7.1) for the DPoP-bound control-plane token, never `Bearer`
- [CA/Browser Forum CSC-31](https://cabforum.org/working-groups/code-signing/requirements/) — 460-day cert validity cap (effective 2026-03-01)
- [Microsoft Azure Artifact Signing GA announcement](https://techcommunity.microsoft.com/blog/microsoft-security-blog/simplifying-code-signing-for-windows-apps-artifact-signing-ga/4482789)
- [Apple Developer forum thread 813441](https://developer.apple.com/forums/thread/813441) — notarization queue delays
